All old allocations remain closed.\n\n## Static function and exact formulas, before execution\n\nFor each enclosing m in {16,20,25}, set k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k,\nbeta=1/k, lambda=1+sqrt(2), eta_word=1-1/(4m),\nc=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a=eta_word/c. Every child word uses THESE parameters.\nThe physical step at zero-based index t is b_v2(t+1), with\nb_j=1+a*c^j; H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j). The length is n=2^m-1.\nThe selected indices are 2^(J+i)-1 for i=0,...,k-1. The ordinary block\nlevels are J,J,J+1,...,m-2. Their masses obey S_0=0,\nS_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j. The final suffix starts at query 2^(m-1).\n\nWrite C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), R_circle=1+beta, kap=1+beta^2.\nIn coordinates (p,-q), the FULL convex body is\nK={0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p, (p-beta)^2+(q+beta)^2<=R_circle^2}.\nIts boundary consists of the segment from 0 to (C,0), the segment\nfrom 0 to (beta,-1), and the circular arc connecting their far endpoints.\nThe displayed disk intersects those two half-planes in exactly the\nhorizontal sections beta*q<=p<=beta+sqrt(R_circle^2-(q+beta)^2).\n\nFor any centered normalized input w=(r,s), its projection is w if\nw belongs to K. Otherwise a nearest boundary point is among:\n(clip(r,0,C),0); (beta*t,-t), t=clip((beta*r-s)/kap,0,1);\nand the radial point (beta,beta)+R_circle*(w-(beta,beta))/||w-(beta,beta)||\nwhen that point lies on the stated arc. These are the exact nearest\npoints on the two segments and on the arc whenever the latter has an\ninterior stationary minimum; remaining arc minima are endpoints already\nincluded. Choose the candidate maximizing <g,w>-||g||^2/2.\nThe production implementation can return the radial disk projection\ndirectly when it belongs to the arc, since K is a subset of that disk.\nFor s>=0 the horizontal projection is exact. For r<=0 the diagonal\nprojection is exact, by maximizing first over U=p-beta*q>=0.\nThese are projection calculations, never projections of the GD state.\n\nThe full support function at d=(d_x,d_y) is the maximum of\n0, C*d_x, beta*d_x-d_y, and\nbeta*(d_x+d_y)+R_circle*sqrt(d_x^2+d_y^2) if d_x>0, d_y<0 and\n(-d_y)*sqrt(1+2beta)>=beta*d_x. This is obtained by maximizing the\nlinear functional along the entire arc; the derivative has a unique\ninterior maximum in precisely that angular sector. The endpoints pay\nall other directions, including d_x=0. Thus projection KKT is checked\nagainst the whole convex hull by h_K(w-g)-<w-g,g>, not sampled arc points.\n\nFor an ordinary block of level j, construct its local data backwards\nfrom u=beta,y=1. Reading the actual reversed physical step h, alpha=h/2,\nlet z=max(beta/sqrt(1+2beta),(y+(1-alpha)*beta)/(u-(1-alpha)*beta)).\nIf z takes the first branch use q=0,p=C; otherwise use\nq=(1+beta)*z/sqrt(1+z^2)-beta, p=beta+(1+beta)/sqrt(1+z^2).\nThen u<-u+alpha*p, y<-y+alpha*q. These are exactly the frozen source's\nbackward rule, with no asymptotic M approximation.\n\nThe sole permitted aggregation is the invariant capped backward prefix:\nonce q=0, y<=beta*(u-C)/sqrt(1+2beta). Further backward steps increase u\nby h*C/2 and keep y unchanged, preserving that inequality. A remaining\nprefix of r steps has exactly floor(r/2^l)-floor(r/2^(l+1)) occurrences\nof level l. Its EXACT mass is the sum of these counts times b_l.\nOnly this already derived invariant prefix can be aggregated. Save every\ncount and the mass used; distinguish explicit backward steps from these\nimplicit steps. No forward update may be aggregated.\n\nFor component i=1,...,k, let u_i,y_i be these backward data and\nM_i=u_i+y_i/beta. Start ell_1=0,D_1=1 and set\nsigma_i=D_i/M_i, ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i,\nA_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,\nD_(i+1)=sigma_i*(b_(J+i-1)/2-1).\nSet delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)). The one frozen function is\nF(x)=(1/2)*sum_i E_(sigma_i*K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))\n     +(1/2)*Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), starting at e_1.\nHere E_C(w)=max_(g in C)(<g,w>-||g||^2/2), and Hub_delta is\n0 for t<=0, t^2/2 for 0<t<delta, and delta*t-delta^2/2 above delta.\nAll half factors are physical. Projection scaling gives\n(P_i,-Q_i)=sigma_i*Pi_K((x_i-A_i)/sigma_i,\n                       (x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))/sigma_i).\nLet P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.\nThe entire gradient is (P_i-Q_(i-1))/2, including all old/future terms.\nEvery actual step is x<-x-h*gradient. No state clipping, checkpoint\nreplacement, independent suffix initialization, or face replay is allowed.\n\nCompleting the square in E proves its gradient is its Euclidean\nprojection. Projection optimality gives the firmly nonexpansive inequality,\nso E is convex and 1-smooth. Stacking adjacent pairs and the final scalar\ncounts coordinates at most twice; the half-sum F is convex and 1-smooth.\nIts minimum set is x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), and\nx_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1). The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible.\nSince 1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1, and\nu_1>=beta*y_1, we have A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For x_1<0 the initial\ndistance is at least 1; on 0<=x_1<=A_1 its square is bounded below\nby (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2, decreasing on this interval and attained\nby x_star. Therefore the TRUE radius, used in every display, is\nR_m^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.\n\nThe actual terminal numerator 2F is computed from every component.\nIndependently compare the Huber term with D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))].\nDuring the ACTUAL last suffix accumulate, without clipping U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i,\nX_flux=(1/2)*sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*U_i+U_(i+1)),\ntheta=(1-beta)/(1+beta), and at the terminal add\nX_terminal=C*sum_i sigma_i*max(x_i-A_i,0).\nThe positive part here is the definition of X, not a change to the state.\n\n## Six semantic slots, frozen before execution\n\n1. Calibration, including construction of m16 constants. {"id": "5784e63fc46dbec9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-02T04:20:31.444700+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "conclusion", "claim": "UNVERIFIED max4r67 immediate extension of Screen2: a static C1 piecewise-quadratic field with all regional Hessians in[mu I,I], mu>0, has exact true-radius payoff <=product(h_t-1)^2 whenever every h_t>=A>1 and mu>=2/A-1. Thus the specified point-plus-cone two-penalty law recreates the original distance-product rejection through a DIFFERENT full Hessian window; this is not an upper for unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# Well-conditioned static field product screen\nStatus: UNVERIFIED analytical conclusion, max4 round67.\nThis is the Hessian-window extension of Screen2, not a fourth field law.\n\nLet F be a static convex C1 piecewise-quadratic function on a finite\nEuclidean space, with an attained minimum x_star, and every regional\nHessian satisfying mu*I<=H<=I for some 0<mu<=1. Its gradient is continuous\nat the finite partition boundaries. Let the predetermined nonnegative\nword h have every h_t>=A>1, and suppose mu>=2/A-1.\nAdvertise smoothness1 and use x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t*grad F(x_t).\nThen its true-distance normalized terminal payoff satisfies\n2(F(x_n)-min F)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2\n<=product_(t<n)(h_t-1)^2.\n\nProof. Integrate the gradient on a segment from the minimizer to any x.\nThe finitely many regional matrices give\ngrad F(x)=Abar(x)*(x-x_star), with Abar symmetric and\nmu*I<=Abar<=I. Boundary segments use the continuous agreeing tangential\ngradient, so the same bounds persist. The lower bound also implies\nstrong convexity: integrating along any segment gives\nF(y)>=F(x)+<grad F(x),y-x>+mu*||y-x||^2/2.\nThus the attained minimizer is UNIQUE and dist(x_0,argmin F)=||x_0-x_star||.\n\nFor any theta in[mu,1] and h>=A, the absolute affine function\n|1-h*theta| takes its maximum at an endpoint.\nAt theta1 the value is h-1. At the other endpoint,\n1-h*mu<=h-1 because h*(1+mu)>=A*(1+mu)>=2,\nand h*mu-1<=h-1 because mu<=1. Consequently\n||(I-h*Abar)(x-x_star)||<=(h-1)||x-x_star||.\nApply this inequality successively along EVERY original physical step.\nThe Hessian upper bound, integrated from the minimum, also gives\nF(x)-min F<=||x-x_star||^2/2. Divide by the true initial squared radius\nto obtain the displayed product bound.\n\nIn particular mu=1/2 and every h_t>=4/3 meet the endpoint condition.\nThe specified two-penalty field\nF(x)=||x||^2/4+dist(x,K)^2/4 with the explicit narrow cone K in the\njoint report has Hessians I/2,(I+U)/2,(I+V)/2,I and therefore falls\ninside this screen even though its actual U,V factors do not commute.\n\nThe inherited-m tau1/4 word in the joint report has every h_t>4/3 and\nproduct_(t<n)(h_t-1)=beta^n*c^(-m). Accepted fact_idb98bb807d514e80d\ngives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). At n=2^m-1 the resulting benchmark ratio\nis at most5*(3/(1+sqrt(2))^2)^m, which tends to0.\nThis is an upper over THIS conditional well-conditioned class ONLY,\nnot an upper for unrestricted W or every two-distance field.\nAll new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. Result, scope, and actual horizons\n\nFor any nonnegative physical word h of length N, W_N(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_N)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional\nEuclidean spaces, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and\nan attained minimum, all minimizers x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, under\nx_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and arbitrary.\nThis is the original W=2R normalization. Define the exact basic OBJECTIVE module\n\n zeta_m=(1+sqrt(1+8A_m))/2,\n e_m=A_m/zeta_m=(zeta_m-1)/2,\n p_m=(q_m,1+e_m).\n\nIts length is 2^m, sum A_m+e_m, and objective mass\n2A_m+zeta_m=zeta_m^2. It has the complete rank-one objective\ncertificate given in Section 3. It is not a primitive module.\n\nAll coefficients in the three following families are selected before\nthe function, initial point, or oracle data. All parameters a_m,b_m\nbelow are arbitrary predetermined nonnegative real numbers and may\ndepend on m. No boundedness or fixed-coefficient assumption is made.\n\n A: H_A=(q_m,1+a_m A,q_(m+1),b_m),\n    N_A=3*2^m,\n    D_A=1+2sum H_A=2(1+lambda+a_m)A+2b_m-1.\n\n B: H_B=(p_m,1+a_m A,q_(m+1),b_m),\n    N_B=3*2^m+1,\n    D_B=1+2sum H_B=2(1+lambda+a_m)A+2e_m+2b_m+1.\n\n C: H_C=(q_m,1+4A/5,p_m,1+A,q_m,b_m),\n    N_C=3*2^m+1,\n    D_C=1+2sum H_C=48A/5+2e_m+2b_m+1.\n\nFamilies A and B have two outer bridging positions. Family C has three\nouter bridging positions. Even counting the cap inside each exact p_m\nas a bridging position gives respectively two, three, and four positions,\nall below the permitted seven. Every displayed entry is an actual single\nGD step; q_m and p_m are actual multi-step modules, never oracle steps.\nThe larger primitive q_(m+1) in A and B has capacity lambda A and\nlength 2^(m+1)-1, so these are unequal-block families. Family C has\nthree chronological modules of types primitive, objective, primitive.\n\nHere is the joint candidate theorem proved below. For EVERY integer\nm>=16 and EVERY allowed nonnegative coefficient choice,\n\n W_(N_A)(H_A)>2/(17A),\n W_(N_B)(H_B)>2/(17A),\n B_(N_A)W_(N_A)(H_A)>19/17,\n B_(N_B)W_(N_B)(H_B)>19/17,                              (1)\n\n W_(N_C)(H_C)>20/(189A),\n B_(N_C)W_(N_C)(H_C)>190/189.                            (2)\n\nEvery violating function is a whole-run asymmetric Huber or scalar\nquadratic with unique minimizer 0 and true initial radius 1. For C,\nEVERY b_m>=0 nevertheless has D_C>B_(N_C) for m>=16: its global\nHuber mass screen passes against the ACTUAL original benchmark.\nThus the C failure is not insufficient total mass. In particular the\nmember b_m=1 has zero unit-quadratic terminal payoff and still fails\non an accepted asymmetric-Huber cut.\n\nStatements (1)--(2) are UNVERIFIED new conclusions, not declarations\nthat the original problem is solved. No assertion is made about a fourth\nlayout, arbitrary unequal templates, first outer steps below 1, other\nmodule types, arbitrary three-block coefficients, or all nonnegative words.\n\n## 2. Accepted inputs, recall, and search\n\nThe only mathematical bricks used are these accepted facts:\n\n - 04219554e41d9750: full original primitive/dominant identities;\n   UNCONDITIONAL original denominator rays, doubling, adjacency, and\n   certified endpoint scope. Its conditional improving-seed hypotheses\n   are not assumed.\n - 1252b8823bb5a290: normalized interpolation necessity and the original\n   objective certificate on every formal vector and every value.\n - b98bb807d514e80d: exact global Huber and every original single-cut\n   asymmetric-Huber lower witness.\n\nThe FULL recalled report a4bf87c094d0b87e and counterexample\nce4f82215ba906ce have identical complete evidence. Both were read,\nas was dead_end 67a2cb0744398ddb. Their equal-block exclusions and\nfailed displacement arrays are awareness only. No calculation below\nuses an unverified finding as a theorem or repeats those failed arrays.\n\nSkills used: query-memory, search-math-results, obtain-immediate-conclusions,\npropose-subgoal-decomposition-plans, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,\nand identify-key-failures. Three plans were published as 772ef558f61dd6f3,\ne41ebb5c38f4521a, and 5cb3dea52a2c8343. The search stage is active_program;\nthe program is unequal and differently typed exact-block objective gadgets;\nthe missing mechanism is a useful full original-coordinate objective lift\nbeyond the known binary joins; the search mode is mutation.\n\nTwo Matlas theorem searches returned unrelated statements and imported\nno external theorem. Web fallback identified the primary Zhang--Jiang\npaper, arXiv:2410.12395v2. The exact local text\ndownloads/zhang-jiang-2410.12395.txt was read at the primitive/dominant\ndefinitions, Theorems 3.1--3.2 and their proofs. These clarify the types\nand the missing migration hypothesis. All mathematical identities used\nare from accepted042 or are derived jointly below, not imported from\nan unverified paper statement. There is no new technical branch requiring\nanother body of literature after these unchanged types are expanded.\n\nOnly fixed already-certified benchmark rows were read. No generator,\nchecker, mathematical evaluator, recurrence, numerical replay, trajectory,\noptimizer, solver, DP, installation, consult, or subagent was run. Reading,\nJSON serialization, text extraction, and hashing are administrative only.\n\n## 3. Exact base types and full original-coordinate identities\n\nNormalize smoothness to 1 and a selected minimizer to the optimum jet\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every finite actual word w=(w_0,...,w_(r-1))\nretain x_0 and ALL g_0,...,g_r and f_0,...,f_r. Put s=sum w_t and\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)w_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_w=2sum_(t<r)w_t(f_t-f_r)+sum_(t<r)w_t(1-w_t)||g_t||^2\n                                          -s(s+1)||g_r||^2.\n\nFor a zero-diagonal array Lambda, let e be the all-ones column,\nL_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and T_ii=1, T_it=w_t for t<i,\nT_it=0 for t>i. Define\n\n omega=(-2w_0,...,-2w_(r-1),2s)^T,\n D_prim=diag(w_t(2-w_t) for t<r,-s(s+2)),\n M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T), sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2.\n\nAccepted042 gives, under L_flow^T e=omega,\n\n V_w+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n           =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.                  (3)\n\nThe pairing is the trace matrix pairing. It is an identity on the full\nformal gradient space, not only on an actual trajectory. The x_0 row\nof this finite primitive identity is zero; no optimum row is used to\nsupply a missing initial-point diagonal. All internal function values\ncancel by the displayed full flow. A zero physical step removes no\nformal gradient and imposes no equality of formal coordinates.\n\n### 3.1 Complete primitive q_m arrays\n\nStart q_0 with the zero array. At the join from q_j to q_(j+1), let\nA_j=lambda^j, d=(lambda-2)A_j, kappa=lambda^2. Let p be the physical\nbridge query, z the parent terminal, and t range over EVERY right-child\nnonterminal query with its actual step w_t. Embed the entire left array\nwith factor 1 and the entire right array with factor kappa, then add\n\n Lambda_(t,p) += (kappa-1)w_t,\n Lambda_(z,p) += kappa-1,\n Lambda_(p,z) += (kappa-1)d,\n Lambda_(t,z) += (kappa-1)w_t.                            (4)\n\nOverlapping contributions add; all unlisted entries are zero. This\ndefines the full array without executing the recurrence. All signs\nare nonnegative. The exact identities d(2A_j+d)=A_j^2 and\n2A_j+d=lambda A_j match accepted042's primitive join. The full\nparent remainder is the original-coordinate sum of the two child\nremainders with weights 1 and kappa. Starting with zero gives\nM_prim=0 for EVERY actual q_m, on ALL 2^m original gradient coordinates.\nThe sum is A_m-1 and the length 2^m-1 by symbolic induction.\n\n### 3.2 Complete objective p_m array, including the initial mixed term\n\nFor weights u_i>=0 of sum D=1+2sum w define\n\n J_(w,u)=||z_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_r\n                                 -||z_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2. (5)\n\nHere z_0 is the ACTUAL local starting position relative to the same\nglobal minimizer. If the module occurs later in a physical word,\nz_0=x_0 minus the full previous weighted gradient displacement; it\nis not a new origin or an independently chosen initial point.\n\nFor p_m write p for the query after q_m and N for its terminal. Set\n\n u=(q_m,A+1,zeta_m), D=zeta_m^2=2A+zeta_m.\n\nAccepted042's dominant join with EMPTY dominant right child is the\nfull identity\n\n J_(p_m,u)=V_(q_m)-4A Q_(N,p).                           (6)\n\nTherefore the entire objective array at level 1/D consists of:\n\n - each q_m finite multiplier divided by D;\n - multiplier 4A/D on the ordered finite pair (N,p);\n - multiplier 2u_i/D on each original pair (i,star).\n\nEvery multiplier is nonnegative. At each internal q_m query, the\nfinite-child flow -2(q_m)_i/D is cancelled by 2u_i/D. At p the\nfull finite flow is [2(A-1)-4A+2(A+1)]/D=0. At N it is\n(4A+2zeta_m)/D=2. Thus every original function value cancels.\nBy (3),(6), the entire formal remainder is exactly\n\n (1/D)||z_0||^2-2f_N+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n                 =||z_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2/D.             (7)\n\nThis is PSD on ALL original gradients and the free local starting\nvector. When embedded later, its square retains all original initial\nmixed terms and all past gradients through the actual z_0. Its\nsingular directions are retained, not quotiented out. The matching\nwhole-word Huber from accepted b98 yields W_(2^m)(p_m)=1/D.\n\n### 3.3 A direct type counterexample\n\nThe objective completion p_m cannot be used as a primitive block.\nFor m>=1, on the actual unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, the\nendpoint factor of q_m is -1/A. At m=1 it is 1-sqrt(2)=-1/lambda.\nIf it is -1/A_j for a child, the next join gives\n(-1/A_j)^2[-(lambda-2)A_j]=-1/(lambda A_j).\nThis is an exact symbolic induction, with no executed trajectory.\n\nThe cap of p_m has factor -e_m, so its actual endpoint is 1/zeta_m.\nOn a unit-quadratic trajectory, summing\nw_t(2-w_t)x_t^2=x_t^2-x_(t+1)^2 gives\n\n V_w=1-(1+sum w_t)^2 x_r^2.\n\nFor p_m, 1+sum p_m=A+e_m+1=(zeta_m^2+1)/2, hence\n\n V_(p_m)=1-(zeta_m^2+1)^2/(4zeta_m^2)\n                   =-(zeta_m^2-1)^2/(4zeta_m^2)<0.       (8)\n\nThis is an actual smooth-convex transcript, not a formal array test.\nIt excludes EVERY primitive certificate for this exact objective\nmodule. The objective certificate (7) remains valid. This type\nfailure prevents a migration which simply puts p_m into the primitive\nslots of accepted042. Equations (5)--(7) show exactly what its actual\nobjective resource is; no virtual Qhat<=0 is assumed.\n\n## 4. Original benchmark at the actual physical horizons\n\nUse ONLY the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,\n U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. No dominant balanced-optimizer assumption is made.\nAn actual lower witness disproves improvement using a LOWER bound\non B_N. Establishing a mass/upper-certificate advantage requires an\nUPPER bound on B_N. Both directions are supplied below.\n\nAccepted042's unconditional conclusions give strict monotonicity,\nB_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n, and\n\n Z_3=lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(3*2^j-1),\n lambda^(-j)B_(3*2^j-1)<=Z_3\n    <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(3*2^j))+3/100)^2,\n B_(n+1)-B_n<=8sqrt(2)(n+1)^(rho-1).                     (10)\n\n### 4.1 Fixed accepted endpoint scope and exact rational comparisons\n\nAccepted042 proves B_3071>64000 in its Section 9. For its upper\nenclosure at j=10, its fully certified Section 8 table has denominator\n\n D80=1208925819614629174706176\n\nand row n=3072 upper numerator\n\n U3072=77986481275232950675757313318,\n B_3072<=U3072/D80<64520.                                (11)\n\nThe last comparison is the displayed positive-integer inequality\n77986481275232950675757313318 <\n64520*1208925819614629174706176. It is a textual endpoint comparison,\nnot a recurrence run. Correctness comes\nfrom that fact's complete certificate and checker proof, not the hash.\nNo checker or generator was run in this invocation.\n\nSince 140/99<sqrt(2)<99/70, direct expansion gives\n\n lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2),\n 6725<lambda^10<6730.                                   (12)\n\nFor the lower comparison, 2378*140=332920>3362*99=332838.\nFor the upper, 2378*99=235422<3367*70=235690. The two square-root\nbounds follow from 19600<19602 and 9801>9800, respectively.\n\nThus, using 64000>63935=(19/2)*6730,\n\n B_3071/lambda^10>19/2.                                 (13)\n\nAlso sqrt(B_3072)<255, so\n\n (sqrt(B_3072)+3/100)^2\n   <64520+(3/50)*255+9/10000<64536.\n\nThe UPPER ray enclosure in (10), not a lower split estimate, yields\n\n Z_3<64536/6725=48/5-24/6725.                            (14)\n\nThe fraction uses 48*1345=64560. In particular (13)--(14) bracket\nthe correct original ray by 19/2<Z_3<48/5 without a decimal constant,\na different-word comparison, a phase guess, or a new DP.\n\n### 4.2 Finite actual-horizon bounds, including the positive offset\n\nFor every integer m>=10, repeated application of accepted (10) at\nthe index 3071=3*2^10-1, and monotonicity, gives for each N_A,N_B,N_C\n\n B_N/A >= B_(3*2^m-1)/lambda^m\n                >= B_3071/lambda^10>19/2.               (15)\n\nFor the positive actual offset N_B=N_C=3*2^m+1, there are TWO\nadjacent increments from the base ray index. Because 1<rho<3/2,\n\n [B_(3*2^m+1)-B_(3*2^m-1)]/A\n <=16sqrt(2)(3+2^(-m))^(rho-1)2^(-m)\n <48*2^(-m).                                            (16)\n\nFor m>=14, 48*2^(-m)<=48/16384<24/6725, as\n2*6725=13450<16384. Combining (10),(14),(16) gives\n\n B_(3*2^m+1)/A<48/5.                                   (17)\n\nMonotonicity also gives this upper bound at N_A. Therefore EVERY\nphysical horizon used here has\n\n 19/2 < B_N/A < 48/5,  m>=14.                            (18)\n\nThese are correctly oriented original-benchmark bounds at N itself.\nNo offset is suppressed. The lower bound is used for rejection of all\nthree families; the upper bound proves the mass screen passes for C.\n\n## 5. Accepted legal screens, every original cut, and full trajectories\n\nFor ANY actual nonnegative word h of length N, accepted b98 supplies\n\n W_N(h)>=1/(1+2sum h),\n W_N(h)>=(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)] for EVERY j,    (19)\n\nwhere P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t and (v)_+=max(v,0).\nThe total sum in each family is (D_family-1)/2. In particular no\ninternal cut disappears: for an original step w inside any listed q\nmodule, let v be its exact preceding local step sum, S the mass before\nthat module, and s_total the complete family sum. Its cut is exactly\n\n (w-1)_+^2 / [(1+S+v)^2(1+2(s_total-S-v-w))].             (20)\n\nFor internal q_(m+1) steps use that unchanged word and its own v.\nFor internal q_m steps of p_m use the same formula; its final cap\nis separately displayed below. Equations (19)--(20) retain EVERY\noriginal internal checkpoint. There is no endpoint replacement.\n\nWrite a=a_m, b=b_m, zeta=zeta_m, e=e_m. The particularly relevant\nouter and objective-cap cuts are the following exact bounds.\n\n A:\n W>=max{1/D_A,\n         a^2/(2lambda A+2b-1),\n         (b-1)_+^2/[(1+lambda+a)^2 A^2]}.                (21)\n\n B:\n W>=max{1/D_B,\n         a^2 A^2/[(A+e+1)^2(2lambda A+2b-1)],\n         (b-1)_+^2/[(1+lambda+a)A+e+1]^2,\n         1/[zeta^2(1+2((lambda+a)A+b))]}.                (22)\n\nIn B the last displayed term is the INTERNAL cap of the leading p_m,\nnot an outer bridge. Its preceding sum is A-1; the full following mass\nis (lambda+a)A+b. The leading objective block is not shortened.\n\n C:\n W>=max{1/D_C,\n         (16/25)/(6A+2e+2b+1),\n         A^2/[((14/5)A+e+1)^2(2A+2b-1)],\n         (b-1)_+^2/[(24/5)A+e+1]^2,\n         25/[196zeta^2(4A+2b+1)]}.                      (23)\n\nThe first outer C bridge has preceding sum A-1 and following mass\n3A+e+b. The second has preceding sum (14/5)A+e and following mass\nA-1+b. The INTERNAL p_m cap has preceding sum (14/5)A-1 and\nfollowing mass 2A+b. The last outer step has preceding sum\n(24/5)A+e. All constants, cap steps, and zero-tail boundaries are\nretained in (21)--(23). For m>=1 every denominator is positive.\n\nHere is the full actual construction for each nonzero term, to make\nthe original-row audit explicit. For a selected physical step h_j>1\nput c=1/(1+P_j), d=c(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j), and use the SINGLE static\nasymmetric-Huber function\n\n F_(c,d)(x)=-d*x-d^2/2 for x<=-d,\n            x^2/2      for -d<=x<=c,\n            c*x-c^2/2 for x>=c.                         (24)\n\nIts derivative max(-d,min(c,x)) is continuous, nondecreasing, and\n1-Lipschitz; its unique minimum is 0 at 0. At every ORIGINAL prefix\nindex i<=j,\n\n x_i=1-c sum_(t<i)h_t>=c,\n g_i=c, f_i=c*x_i-c^2/2.\n\nAt the selected step, x_(j+1)=-d(1+2T_j). At every original suffix\nindex i>=j+1,\n\n x_i=-d(1+2T_j)+d sum_(j<t<i)h_t<=-d,\n g_i=-d, f_i=-d*x_i-d^2/2.\n\nThe terminal payoff is d^2(1+2T_j), exactly (19). These formulas\ninclude every internal step of every primitive or objective module,\nincluding the internal objective cap and a zero final step. If h_j<=1,\nthe cut term is zero and needs no zero-slope function. For the global\nterm use c=d=1/D; then x_i=1-sum_(t<i)h_t/D at every original index\nand 2f_N=1/D.\n\nEvery construction has x_0=1, unique minimizer 0, true squared initial\ndistance 1, and every original jet explicitly specified. Accepted1252\ngives Q_ij<=0 for EVERY ordered pair, including both orientations,\nthe optimum and terminal. The Gram is the actual scalar outer product\nand is PSD. Multiplying F by any L>0 gives the SAME actual points\nunder h_i/L and the same original W normalization.\n\n## 6. Known whole-word quadratic screens\n\nFor every theta in [0,1], f(x)=theta*x^2/2 is a legal normalized\nquadratic, with payoff theta times the square of the WHOLE physical\nproduct. For theta>0 its minimizer is uniquely 0. For theta=0 its\npayoff is 0. Set\n\n P_qm(theta)=product_(w in q_m)(1-w*theta),\n P_pm(theta)=P_qm(theta)[1-(1+e_m)theta].\n\nThe exact products, in chronological order, are\n\n A: P_qm(theta)[1-(1+aA)theta]P_q(m+1)(theta)(1-b theta),\n B: P_pm(theta)[1-(1+aA)theta]P_q(m+1)(theta)(1-b theta),\n C: P_qm(theta)[1-(1+4A/5)theta]P_pm(theta)\n                      [1-(1+A)theta]P_qm(theta)(1-b theta). (25)\n\nTheir squares multiplied by theta are additional lower bounds for W.\nNo maximization or numerical evaluation of (25) is performed. At\ntheta=1, the symbolic endpoint identities in Section 3 yield\n\n A: W>=a^2(b-1)^2/(lambda^2 A^2),\n B: W>=a^2(b-1)^2/(lambda^2 zeta^2),\n C: W>=(16/25)(b-1)^2/zeta^2.                            (26)\n\nThese use ONE quadratic across the entire original word. They are\nnot virtual oracle endpoint tests. In particular the last unit step\nb=1 makes all three unit-quadratic payoffs zero, but says nothing\nabout their asymmetric-Huber objective loss. Formula (26) is crucial\nin the C proof because it controls EVERY possible chosen final step.\n\n## 7. Uniform direct rejection of unequal families A and B\n\nThis proves (1) jointly, without first promoting a partial lemma.\nLet r_2=17/2 and suppose, for either family, that W<=1/(r_2 A).\nPut e=0 and u=0 for A; put e=e_m and u=(e_m+1)/A for B.\nFor m>=16, lambda>2 gives A>65536 and sqrt(A)>256. Since\nzeta_m^2-zeta_m=2A, zeta_m>sqrt(2A), hence\n\n 0<=e/A<1/sqrt(A), 0<=u<=2/sqrt(A)<=1/128. (28)\n\nFor A, the first line is an enlargement of its exact mass bound, and\nthe second and third are exact. The third line bounds\n\n (2b-1)/A<=1/A+2(1+lambda+a+u)/sqrt(r_2 A).\n\nBefore a has been bounded, 1+lambda+u<4. Therefore the second line\nof (28) gives\n\n r_2 a^2 < (33/32)[5+1/32+a/256]\n                         <21/4+a/128.                   (29)\n\nHere (1+u)^2<33/32; also 1/A+4/sqrt(A)<1/32. Thus a<1.\n\nNow 1+lambda+a+u<5, so\n\n 1/A+2(1+lambda+a+u)/sqrt(r_2 A)<1/32.\n\nThe same cut bound improves to\n\n r_2 a^2 < (33/32)(34/7+1/32)\n                      <165/32<136/25=r_2(4/5)^2.\n\nConsequently a<4/5. Include Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on\n[o_l,p_l]. For each of that node's right queries add\ns_(l-1)mubase^(l-1)_i into p_l. Together with(15), this\ngives every base coefficient as a finite sum/product on original indices.\n\nWrite Cbase^j for its gradient matrix and Mbase_j(q) for(6). No child PSD is asserted at epsilon>0.\nThe common-gradient contact identity is\n\n    Cbase^j e=D_j mubase^j/2.                         (20)\n\nIndeed Psrc is zero with zero derivative at all-common gradients by(17).\nAt x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2; both squares\nin(19) have zero vector argument. Two explicit GLOBAL laws and their entire polynomial\n\nAt the final depth m abbreviate mu=mubase^m. In the following formulas\nthe coefficients eta,chi are arbitrary nonnegative scalars. (21)\n\nThe two concrete laws are\n\n    Law A: eta=64epsilon, chi=0;\n    Law B: eta=0, chi=128epsilon.                     (22)\n\nFor a calibration child r<5 use eta=chi=0; for r>=5 use(22) with\nthe SAME enclosing epsilon. At epsilon=0 both laws use eta=chi=0 at\nevery depth. No retuning is performed after the obstruction.\n\nEvery final coefficient is a finite formula supplied by(15),(18),(21).\nThere are no decrements, so no zero coefficient is subtracted from.\nAll finite pairs, including pairs INTO and OUT OF the actual terminal,\nare retained. Law A adds equal mass to each pair i->j and j->i;\nLaw B adds equal mass to i->star and star->i. Thus every original\nvalue equation(5) stays exact. These are global balanced circulations,\nnot source-dependent terminal paths or changes of physical GD steps.\n\nTheir total additions over the ONE word are\n\n    finite addition = eta*(4-sum_i mu_i^2) <=4eta,\n    outgoing optimum addition = 2chi,\n    incoming optimum addition = 2chi. There is no per-node allowance.\n\nFor the entire original vector family define\n\n    Tmu=sum_i mu_i g_i,\n    Xmu=sum_i mu_i x_i,\n    Vmu=sum_i mu_i[||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>].              (24)\n\nValue cancellation and Q_(i,star)+Q_(star,i)=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>\ngive the exact polynomial\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q)=Mbase_m(q)\n           +eta*[2Vmu-||Tmu||^2+<Tmu,Xmu>]+chi*Vmu. (25)\n\nFor the finite term, sum over unordered pairs mu_i mu_j(Q_ij+Q_ji).\nThe difference-square part is 2sum mu_i||g_i||^2-||Tmu||^2 because\nsum mu=2. The position part is <Tmu,Xmu>-2sum mu_i<g_i,x_i>.\nThis proves(25) with every coordinate, including x_0 and g_n, present.\nIn the eta term the x_0 contributions cancel; the chi term contributes\n-chi<Tmu,x_0>. The final original terminal entries are\n\n    K_(i,n)=eta*mu_i*mu_n for i<n,\n    K_(n,i)=Kbase_(n,i)+eta*mu_n*mu_i for i<n,\n    nu_n=(1+chi)mu_n, xiCert_n=chi*mu_n,\n    gammaTerm=C_nn=1+eta*mu_n*(2-mu_n)+chi*mu_n>0.\n\nWith C from(7), put\n\n    vterm=(nu_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)C_(i,n)g_i,\n    Rterm=q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu_i<x_0,g_i>\n              +sum_(i,j<n)C_ij<g_i,g_j>-||vterm||^2/gammaTerm.\n\nThen the COMPLETE original polynomial, without a re-selection, is\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q)=gammaTerm*||g_n-vterm/gammaTerm||^2+Rterm.\n\nEvery coefficient in this identity is fixed by the finite formulas\nabove. Thus the actual terminal expenditure ||vterm||^2/gammaTerm\nis retained, and full PSD is equivalent to PSD of the entire Rterm.\nIt is not enough to check individual prefix planes or the common block.\n\n## 5. Whole-word common/contact payment for the two laws\n\nSet Pbar=sum_i mu_i P_i and Utail_i=sum_(j>i)mu_j. From(21),\n\n    Xin=2chi, Ein=chi(2+Pbar),\n    Hprice=chi(2D-2-Pbar), 0<=Pbar<=D-1.              (26)\n\nAt q=21/(20D), both laws have a positive initial/common block. They retain the\nsame whole-word D and do not rescale a child or count slack twice.\n\nFor clarity, the full contact vector can also be written explicitly.\nThe finite addition has row-sum vector\n\n    dfin_i=eta*mu_i*(P_i-Pbar/2),                    (29)\n\nand Vmu's gradient-matrix row sum is\n\n    cstar_i=mu_i*(1+P_i/2)+(h_i/2)*Utail_i. Balanced\nfinite position variations cancel; the remaining row-i position\ndifference is eta mu_i(2P_i-Pbar), half of which is the matrix row sum.\nFor(30), write Vmu=sum mu_i||g_i||^2-<Tmu,x_0>\n+sum_i mu_i<g_i,sum_(t<i)h_t g_t> and take its gradient row sums.\nThus the final row-sum vector is\n\n    csum=D*mu/2+dfin+chi*cstar.                       (31)\n\nEquations(11),(21),(26),(31) give the EXACT remaining transverse\nexpenditure, including every common, initial and terminal coupling.\nNo bound paying this transverse expenditure is claimed.\n\nFor A alone, dcontact=csum-D*mu/2=dfin. Completing the common block\nat q=zeta/D, zeta>1, gives the simpler exact expression\n\n    Price_A(v)=(csum^T v)^2/D\n                  +(dfin^T v)^2/[D(zeta-1)].        (32)\n\nAt zeta=1 the singular common block instead requires dfin=0.\nAs mu has positive weights at different physical prefixes, A at\nepsilon>0 does not satisfy that singular-contact requirement.\nAt zeta=21/20 the cost in(32) is finite but remains a real full-space\nexpense. The counterdirection below defeats both laws even before\nany initial Schur expenditure is deducted.\n\n## 6. General balanced-circulation contact obstruction\n\nThis calculation is not limited to the rank-product law. Let J_ij>=0\nbe ANY zero-diagonal finite-node array with equal row and column sums\nat every node. On ANY finite nonnegative word define\n\n    R_J(g)=sum_(i!=j)J_ij[<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].\n\nIts value terms cancel by balance and its x_0 terms cancel in position\ndifferences. Let C_J be its full symmetric gradient matrix. At common\ngradients, R_J(e)=0. Its row-sum vector satisfies\n\n    (C_J e)_i=(1/2)sum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j),\n    P^T C_J e=(1/4)sum_(i,j)J_ij(P_i-P_j)^2.         (33)\n\nFor the first equality, the derivative of the source gradient gives\nsum_j J_ij(P_i-P_j). The derivatives of the positions cancel because\nrow and column masses agree. The difference-square derivative vanishes\nat e. Divide the derivative by2. For the second equality, expand the\nsum of squared differences and use balance to equate the P_i^2 and\nP_j^2 sums. This proves both equalities directly without invoking a\ngraph theorem.\n\nIt follows that C_J e=0 if and only if every positive J_ij joins\nequal P_i and P_j. Necessity follows from the nonnegative sum in(33);\nsufficiency follows from its first equality. With nonnegative h,\nP_i=P_j means all intervening steps are zero, hence x_i=x_j as a\nFORMAL identity even with independent gradients. In this case\n\n    R_J(g)=(1/2)sum J_ij||g_i-g_j||^2 >=0.           (34)\n\nConversely, if some positive edge has unequal physical prefixes,\nC_J e!=0 while e^T C_J e=0. Taking v=C_J e and expanding\nR_J(e+t v)=2t||C_J e||^2+t^2 v^T C_J v shows a negative value\nfor sufficiently small negative t. Thus C_J is not PSD. In particular,\non a strictly positive word EVERY nonzero nonnegative balanced finite\ncirculation is an indefinite correction, despite exact value balance.\n\nMoreover no collection of such additions can cancel its physical-\nprefix contact work: the right side of(33) is additive and nonnegative.\nAt an exactly contacting base with zero incoming optimum rows and\nq=1/D, the common kernel condition forces C_J e=0. Hence no nonzero\nnonnegative finite circulation preserves full PSD at that exact level\non a positive word. This is NOT a statement at q>1/D, about signed\nchanges with paid decrements, about changed optimum profiles, or about\narbitrary full certificates. The explicit paid test in the next\nsection is needed to exclude our concrete laws at larger q.\n\n## 7. Entire zero-displacement restriction of BOTH global corrections\n\nConsider any original gradient assignment supported strictly within\nthe root's first left child, with x_0=0, zero gradient at that child's\nterminal, and\n\n    sum_(i in support)h_i g_i=0.                     (35)\n\nAll later formal positions are zero. On the supported indices(18)\ngives mu_i=2h_i/D. Therefore Tmu=0 exactly. By(24),(25),\n\n    D[M_(eta,chi)(q)-Mbase_m(q)]\n      =(2eta+chi) sum_(i in support)h_i(2-h_i)||g_i||^2. (36)\n\nHere is a direct verification retaining all prefix positions. Since\nx_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n\n    sum_i h_i<g_i,x_i>\n      =-(1/2)||sum_i h_i g_i||^2+(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2\n      =(1/2)sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.\n\nMultiplying Vmu by D consequently gives exactly the diagonal sum\nin(36). Every unsupported term in Vmu has gradient zero, and Tmu=0\nkills BOTH global rank terms in(25), including their terminal and\nremote-query contributions. Thus(36) is an entire subspace identity,\nnot a selected-edge or conditional-covariance approximation.\n\n## 8. The inherited base itself is negative on a deep original wave\n\nWe now strengthen the earlier two-coordinate screen by retaining the\nnegative terminal-source accumulation at ALL depths. Let pterm_j be\nPsrc_j evaluated with only its terminal gradient equal to1. Define\n\n    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).\n\nSubstitute Y=-(A_j-1), Z=-1 into(17). The bracket is precisely Fterm_j,\nand the left child is zero. Thus the finite closed expression is\n\n    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l\n                              product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (37)\n\nThis is a symbolic finite formula and has not been executed. At depth1,\npterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. At depth2, k_1<7,\n0<tstar-t_1<epsilon, and Fterm_1<13, so\n\n    pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2.                 (38)\n\nFor the last inequalities use(13),(14): k_1<2+5/2+(7/2)/2<7;\ntstar-t_1=(lambda epsilon-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)])/(lambda A_1)\n<epsilon; and Fterm_1=A_1^2-(1+epsilon)A_1+lambda+1<13.\nAlso A_2>5 makes34<2A_2^2. The step from depth2 to depth3 adds a\nnegative term and multiplies the normalized old term by a number in(0,1).\nIt follows that pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon, regardless of its sign.\n\nFor every l>=3, monotonicity of t_l and the exact relation\nt_(l+1)=c*t_l/(2+t_l) give\n\n    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)tstar,\n    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.                          (39)\n\nSince A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Also A_(l+1)=(2+t_l)A_l\nand t_l<1/2. Hence\n\n    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.          (40)\n\nThe normalized propagation factor is exactly\n\n    rnorm_l=k_l/(2+t_l)^2\n       =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,\n    1-epsilon<rnorm_l<1, l>=2.                          (41)\n\nIndeed (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon< (5/2)epsilon.\nThe product of any at most m such factors is greater than\n(1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the elementary last inequality\nfollows by induction from (1-u)^k>=1-ku for0<=u<=1.\nNo recurrence or product has been numerically evaluated.\n\nUnroll(37) from depth3, divide by A_j^2, bound the initial propagated\nterm above by2epsilon, and apply(40),(41) to EVERY negative term.\nFor3<=j<=m,\n\n    pterm_j/A_j^2\n       <[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon.                    (42)\n\nThere are j-3 terms, l=3,...,j-1. Each negative contribution has\nmagnitude greater than (8/125)epsilon times3/4. This explains the\ncoefficient and retains the entire same-m depth range. No PSD of a\npositive-epsilon child has been used.\n\nChoose ANY integer62<=j<=m-3. Let rbridge=2^j-1 and Hstep=1+d_j\nbe its actual physical step. Assign the original scalar vectors\n\n    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_rbridge=1,\n    g_i=0 for every other original i, including i=n.  (43)\n\nThe displacement is exactly b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later points and\nthe root left terminal are zero, so(35) holds. In the first Hword_j,\nonly its first and terminal gradients are nonzero. For j>=2 there\nis no source cross coefficient between them: g_0 enters only the\nfirst depth1 bracket in(17), while the final terminal enters disjoint\nbrackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final one is pterm_j.\nAt its next join, Y=0 and Z=1, contributing\n\n    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.\n\nAll larger source joins have Y=Z=0. At the final objective join(19),\nx_p, g_p, and T_R all vanish. Thus the ENTIRE base value, for any\nreal q, is exactly\n\n    D*Mbase_m(q;43)=(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j.       (44)\n\nBecause A_j>5, d_j<A_j/2, and b>7/5, Hstep/(b A_j)<1/2.\nUsing2-b^2<3epsilon, then(42), and discarding only the negative -Edef_j,\ngives\n\n    D*Mbase_m(q;43)\n      <[11/4-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon*A_j^2\n      <=-(41/500)epsilon*A_j^2\n      <-(1/20)epsilon*A_j^2, j>=62. In particular j=m-3 is allowed for EVERY\nm>=1536 and uses exactly the original physical horizon and enclosing m.\n\n## 9. All nonnegative mixtures fail: no parameter retuning can repair them\n\nOn(43), the full diagonal sum in(36) is\n\n    b(2-b)(Hstep/b)^2+Hstep(2-Hstep)\n       =2Hstep[1-(1-1/b)Hstep].                             (46)\n\nSince b>7/5, 1-1/b>2/7. Also j>=62, d_j>2A_j/5 and A_j>=2^j,\nso Hstep>7. Therefore the quantity in(46) is strictly negative.\nCombining the complete identities(36),(44) with(45),(46) proves,\njointly within this unverified draft, that\n\n    M_(eta,chi)(q;43)<-epsilon*A_j^2/(20D)<0          (47)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, EVERY62<=j<=m-3, EVERY eta,chi>=0,\nand EVERY real q. This includes eta=chi=0 and arbitrary unbounded\nmixture coefficients. Positive coefficients only make this direction\nworse. Thus no nonnegative tuning of the two specified global\ngenerators can produce a full PSD certificate on this base. Laws A\nand B are stopped at this declared-domain obstruction; no third law\nor tuned variant is proposed.\n\nThis is broader than failure of the three previous conditional paths:\nit permits every finite-node pair of the rank-product generator,\nincludes the independent incoming terminal rows, includes both\noptimum directions with nonzero whole-word expenditure, and excludes\nthe complete nonnegative cone of the two global generators. It still\ndoes NOT exclude other circulations, non-product weights, signed\nreallocations with paid decrements, different optimum profiles,\ndifferent bases or arbitrary certificates.\n\nIn particular(43) is NOT a primal hard function. A differentiable\nstatic function cannot have those two gradients at the same point;\nalso an actual start at the normalized optimum0 cannot have nonzero\ngradient. (48)\n\nEvery coefficient is positive because1/4<tstar<1/2. The empty base\nis ||x_0-g_0||^2. If the right child is PSD at1/Dc, its exact initial\nSchur remainder Rchild_R is PSD by completing that child's x_0\nsquare using(20). At depth2 the bridge is2-epsilon.\nHere D_1>19/5, D_2<75/7 and\ntheta_1=(2b+2-epsilon)/(3b+4-2epsilon)<59/100.\nFor the upper ratio use b<10/7 and2-epsilon>199/100; the ratio\nincreases with b and decreases with that bridge. Equation(19), the PSD source in(49), and the full\nright-child Schur remainder prove depth2 PSD with x_0 retained.\nThese finite calibrations do not establish positive-epsilon large-depth PSD.\n\nFor arbitrary zero entries in a word, ALL algebra(4)–(11),(21),(25),\n(33) and the general balanced-flow statements keep every formal query.\nIf P_i=P_j, nonnegative steps make x_i=x_j a formal identity, but\ng_i and g_j are still independent and remain in(34). These are derived jointly, not accepted facts.", "evidence": "# Tail-weighted full squares and an explicit balanced first-row repair\n\nJOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max, invocation83.\nOriginal exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No new fact or verifier verdict.\n\nThis is one joint derivation, not a sequence of accepted intermediate facts.\nTASK.md and master_guidance a16df0f0aca7349e permit ONE invocation and at\nmost TWO substantive coefficient laws. All those\ncounts remain zero. All scalar inequalities below are textual mathematics.\n\n## 1. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThe exact suffix-product scale is retained in BOTH the nonisotropic detail\nmetric and the actual terminal distribution. Every original queried gradient,\nthe actual unqueried terminal gradient, and the initial vector remain\nindependent. The two explicit laws use the same weighted full square and\ndistributed imbalance, but genuinely different original coefficient arrays.\n\nLaw A uses the canonical weighted skew for that imbalance. Throughout its\nentire domain, its incoming optimum multiplier at original query1 is\nnegative. Independently, making its first-to-last queried multiplier\nnonnegative forces its exact global square charge above the assigned\nqtar=17/(16D). This quantifies why paying through an exponentially tiny\ninitial tail weight fails in this particular matching.\n\nLaw B adds an EXPLICIT BALANCED skew transport, with no sign-feasibility\noracle. It equalizes the entire first row, and can make all its finite\nmultipliers nonnegative while maintaining the full PSD factor at qtar.\nNevertheless, for EVERY member of Law B, either the original multiplier\nfrom the last queried leaf to the preceding queried leaf is negative, or\nthe original incoming optimum multiplier at query1 is negative. This is\nan exhaustive obstruction for this displayed balanced correction, including\narbitrary positive metric scale and arbitrary nonnegative imbalance scale.\n\nNeither exclusion is an impossibility theorem for all balanced corrections,\nall weighted variances, all terminal probabilities, or the full dual cone.\nNo primal interpolability or lower bound on W is inferred from a formal\nnegative direction or a negative signed multiplier. No full upper bound on\nW, benchmark improvement, or resolution of the original problem is obtained.\n\n## 2. Original problem and accepted inputs\n\nFor a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n and L>0, W_n(h) denotes\nthe supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite\nEuclidean dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth function with an\nattained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the original recursion\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question concerns\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The original benchmark retains EVERY integer split:\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).\n\nAccepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation\norientation, full independent-vector certificate interpretation, and exact\nincoming-terminal-free reduction. It deletes NO queried-prefix row.\nAccepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-enclosing-m word below,\nits mass identity and the scalar comparison B_n/D<47/50. Its implication\nfrom W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) to a strict benchmark improvement is CONDITIONAL;\nthat full-class upper hypothesis is still missing. These are the only\naccepted mathematical inputs used here. Historical draft6e66efffac0795f1\nis recall only; its coordinate and row identities are rederived below.\n\nFix ANY integer m>=1536. All children use this SAME enclosing m:\n\n    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,\n    c=ell(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, z=n-1,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, Aword=1+S, D=1+2S=2Aword-1,\n    p=1/Aword, qtar=17/(16D), b=1+a.\n\nThe symbols p and q in this report mean p83 and q83; neither is a benchmark\nexponent or a primal derivative. The letter B without a subscript below is\na skew matrix, not the benchmark B_n. All other hierarchy notation is local.\nThe actual physical step is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2(j) is the exponent\nof2 dividing the positive integer j. The final three steps are\n(b,1+beta,b). The first and last queried steps equal b; index n is the\nACTUAL terminal, distinct from z.\n\nThe elementary bounds needed below are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.       (2.1)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<17/12; hence ell>12/5 and\nell<29/12, and (29/12)(1+1/6144)<5/2. Also beta>7/8,\na>(7/8)/(5/2)>1/3, and a<1/ell<1/2.\nThe accepted mass formula is\n\n    Aword=(1-vword)2^m+vword*c^m,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1).\n\nIndeed c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1 give c(c-2)>1. Consequently\n\n    2^m<Aword<c^m, 0<p<2^(-m)<1/128.                  (2.2)\n\nEvery h_i>1, consistently with these strict mass inequalities.\n\n## 3. Exact physical suffix products and the selected terminal law\n\nWrite sbit(L) for the binary digit sum of a nonnegative integer L, including\nsbit(0)=0. For 0<=L<=n define the product of the FINAL L actual factors:\n\n    r_L=product_(t=n-L)^(n-1)(h_t-1), r_0=1.\n\nThe index j counted backward from the final physical step has\nh_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(2^m-j)=a*c^v2(j), for 1<=j<2^m. The valuation equality\nfollows by factoring the exact power of2 from j: the remaining difference\nfrom2^m is odd. For any L, sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=sum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t).\nWriting L in binary and summing each digit's contributions gives\nsum_(t>=1)floor(L/2^t)=L-sbit(L). Therefore the EXACT suffix identity is\n\n    r_L=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)), 0<=L<=n.                 (3.1)\n\nNo product or recurrence has been numerically evaluated. In particular\ns:=r_n=beta^n/c^m, and the signed full-word product is -s since n is odd.\nDefine the actual terminal coefficient and its absolute scale by\n\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t),\n    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_(i<n)u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIf L=n-1-i, adding the next backward factor shows\n\n    u_i=r_L+r_(L+1), k_i=(-1)^L u_i=(-1)^i u_i.       (3.2)\n\nThus all u_i are strictly positive. The last three are\n\n    u_z=b, u_(z-1)=a(1+beta), u_(z-2)=b*a*beta,\n    u_0=(b/a)s.                                      (3.3)\n\nExpanding a product over the m binary digits gives the finite identity\n\n    Z=sum_(L=0)^n r_L=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Z-1-s <2(3/2)^m.                              (3.4)\n\nThe last inequality uses beta<1 and c>2. The three distinct last terms\nin (3.3), with (2.1), give\n\n    U>4/3+5/8+7/18=169/72>7/3.                       (3.5)\n\nBoth laws use these SAME explicit terminal probabilities:\n\n    alpha_star=p, alpha_i=(1-p)rho_i=delta*u_i,\n    delta=(1-p)/U, rplus=1-delta, rminus=1+delta,\n    v=k-alpha.                                       (3.6)\n\nThey sum to1 and are strictly positive on every original queried sample\nand the optimum. They are probabilities on real original rows, not on a\nselected child interface. Since U>7/3, one has 0<delta<3/7, rplus>1/2,\nand 1<rminus<3/2. Precisely\n\n    v_i=rplus*u_i for i even,\n    v_i=-rminus*u_i for i odd.                        (3.7)\n\nSeveral uniform small-initial-weight bounds will be used. The binomial\nexpansion implies n=2^m-1>m(m-1)/2>128m for m>=1536. Also\n(1-eps)^(-n)>(1+eps)^n>=1+n*eps, so beta^n<1/33. From Aword<c^m,\ns/p=beta^n*Aword/c^m<beta^n. Because b/a=1+1/a<4,\n\n    s<p/33, u_0<4p/33<p/8,\n    alpha_0<p/8, rho_0<3p/56<1/10.                  (3.8)\n\nThese are symbolic inequalities uniform in all enclosing m in the task.\n\n## 4. Full original coordinates and the complete signed matching\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep arbitrary independent\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n, with every original position defined by\n\n    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.\n\nThe residual orientation is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nLegal actual jets obey Q_ij<=0. For i<n let y_i=x_i-g_i and\nDelta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2, with the corresponding star quantities zero.\nLet e be the all-ones column of length n, e_i its coordinate columns, and\nT=T82 the lower triangular matrix T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, zero for t>i.\nFor vector columns g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1)) and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1)),\n\n    y=e*x_0-Tg, g=T^(-1)(e*x_0-y).                    (4.1)\n\nThis is invertible on the ENTIRE formal space. Explicitly, if\npi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), then\n\n    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i\n          +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (4.2)\n\nSubstitution into x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i proves\n\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y, e^T k=1+s, T^T k=h.           (4.3)\n\nFor the latter identity, h_j=1-(1-h_j) telescopes on each suffix:\nk_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i. In particular e^T v=p+s.\nThe cumulative terminal probabilities, distinct from detail weights below,\nare Cprob_i=p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j. Define\n\n    R_i=h_i*Cprob_i-alpha_i,\n    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, t=Te,\n    Uclock=sum_i R_i=t^T v,\n    gamma=2p/(p+s), eta=(p-s)/(p+s).\n\nThen T^T v=R and\nR_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0. Thus Uclock>0,\n1<gamma<2, eta>0 and gamma=1+eta. In particular\n\n    R_0=bp+a*alpha_0, H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a,\n    bp<R_0<25p/16, 7p/8<H<9p/8.                     (4.4)\n\nThe lower bound on H follows from H=p+s-rplus*u_0>p-u_0; its upper\nbound follows from the second expression in (4.4).\nThe actual terminal center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y.\n\nFor the moment let w_i>0 be arbitrary DETAIL weights, Ww=sum_i w_i,\nand let d be any real column with e^T d=0. Define\n\n    K=diag(w)-w*w^T/Ww,\n    A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww+B, B^T=-B, Be=0,\n    P=K+gamma*v*v^T, N=T^T(P+A).                     (4.5)\n\nThus Ke=0, A^T=-A and Ae=d. The matrices are instantiated explicitly in\nSections6 and7; an arbitrary sign-feasible B is NOT a proposed result.\nEvery scalar entry is\n\n    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)\n                                      +gamma*R_i*v_j. (4.6)\n\nThe entire ORIGINAL SIGNED coefficient array is\n\n    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,n-1},\n    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for i!=j<n,\n    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p*R_i+(T^T d)_i,\n    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i\n                      =(Kt)_i+gamma*Uclock*v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i,\n    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                  (4.7)\n\nSigns are not presumed. This lists every original coefficient, including\nboth optimum orientations and the actual outgoing terminal distribution.\nFor each prefix index i, the outgoing sum is N_ii, while the incoming\nPREFIX sum is N_ii-2alpha_i. The incoming terminal coefficient2alpha_i\ntherefore gives zero total divergence at i. Terminal divergence is2;\noptimum divergence is-2. Consequently all original sampled values cancel\nin M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.\n\nTo check the quadratic directly, Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\nfor prefix indices, including star. The prefix value terms cancel the\n-2sum alpha_i Delta_i from the outgoing terminal expansion. The remaining\nprefix term is -g^T N y. Since N=T^T(P+A), this equals\n\n    -<x_0,e^T(P+A)y>+y^T P y,\n    e^T P=2p*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.\n\nThe terminal expansion contributes ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2. Completing the\ncommon square gives the full identity, on arbitrary original vectors,\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.8)\n\nFor an expansion check, the unsquared prefix terms are\n(q-s^2)||x_0||^2+2(s-p)<x_0,v^T y>+eta||v^T y||^2\n+y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>. The relation eta(p+s)=p-s proves (4.8).\n\n## 5. Weighted completion, the real common derivative, and a row invariant\n\nLet ybar_w=sum_i w_i*y_i/Ww. Since sum_i d_i=0, expanding the squares gives\n\n    sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n    =y^T K y+<x_0,d^T y>+J||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i.                          (5.1)\n\nThus the COMPLETE factor is\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.  (5.2)\n\nThe charge J is paid ONCE globally. No child gradient, common mode, leaf,\ncheckpoint, terminal square or initial cross derivative has been removed.\nThe detail form has kernel exactly the constant y columns, because every\nw_i>0. The common square controls that remaining direction since e^T v>0.\n\nFull PSD in (5.2) is EQUIVALENT to q>=p^2+J. To prove necessity take scalar\nx_0=1, set c0=1+[sum_i v_i*d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i),\nand g_n=p. The weighted mean of y is c0 because sum d_i=0. All detail,\ncommon and terminal squares vanish. Reconstruct each original g_i by\n(4.2). The value of M is exactly q-p^2-J. This is a formal full-space\nnegative direction if that quantity is negative; it is NOT an actual\ninterpolable primal trajectory.\n\nSetting every original queried gradient equal to an arbitrary vector G\ninstead gives y_i=x_0-t_iG. Directly in (4.8),\n\n    R_common=(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>\n                      +(t^T Kt+eta*Uclock^2)||G||^2. (5.3)\n\nThe genuine nonzero initial/common derivative is -(d^T t)x_0. A balanced\nB changes neither this derivative nor the full square polynomial.\n\nNow derive the original FIRST-ROW off-diagonal sum including d_0.\nThe first column of T is (1,b,...,b)^T. Since K has column sums zero,\n(T^T K)_00=-a*K_00. Skewness gives A_00=0, and e^T A=-d^T gives\n(T^T A)_00=-b*d_0. Meanwhile (T^T d)_0=-a*d_0. Subtracting the diagonal\nfrom the row sum in (4.7) yields\n\n    sum_(j>0) N_0j\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*(p+s-v_0)+d_0\n       =a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H+d_0.                     (5.4)\n\nEvery balanced B leaves this sum invariant. Nonnegative finite first-row\nmultipliers require the right side to be <=0. A balanced correction can\nredistribute that sum among entries; it cannot change the sum itself.\n\nThere is also an exact inverse-metric price for this invariant. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz on i>0 gives\nsum_(i>0)d_i^2/w_i >=d_0^2/(Ww-w_0), since sum_(i>0)d_i=-d_0.\nConsequently\n\n    J>=d_0^2/(4*K_00), K_00=w_0(1-w_0/Ww).          (5.5)\n\nEquality holds precisely when d_i=-d_0*w_i/(Ww-w_0) for every i>0.\nBecause H>0, (5.4)--(5.5) imply for any nonnegative first row\n\n    J>=(a*K_00+gamma*R_0*H)^2/(4*K_00)\n      >=a*gamma*R_0*H.                               (5.6)\n\nThe final inequality expands the nonnegative square\n(a*K_00-gamma*R_0*H)^2. This is a necessary first-row screen, NOT a\nsufficient certificate condition. The two selected laws now instantiate\nall weights and skew matrices rather than leaving the screen unsolved.\n\n## 6. Law A: exact tail metric with minimum-price distributed imbalance\n\nFor EVERY kappa>0 and tau>=0 choose\n\n    w_i=kappa*u_i, Ww=kappa*U,\n    d=tau*(rho-e_0),\n    A_A=tau*(rho*e_0^T-e_0*rho^T), B_A=0.             (6.1)\n\nHere A_A=(d*w^T-w*d^T)/Ww exactly, and A_A e=d. The imbalance is\nnegative at0 and positive at EVERY other actual queried index, not just\nat one endpoint. It attains equality in (5.5). The metric is nonisotropic\nbecause u_0<p/8 whereas u_z=b>4/3. The terminal probabilities remain (3.6).\n\nThe exact global charge and common derivative are\n\n    J_A=tau^2*(1-rho_0)/(4*kappa*u_0),\n    d^T t=tau*(tbar-1), tbar=sum_i rho_i*t_i.          (6.2)\n\nThus (5.2) is a fully specified factor iff q>=p^2+J_A.\nFor a literal scalar description of EVERY finite entry put\nF_i=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j. Then\n\n    N^A_ij=kappa*(u_i*1_(i=j)+h_i*u_j*1_(j>i)-F_i*rho_j)\n          +gamma*R_i*v_j\n          +tau*((F_i/U)*1_(j=0)-1_(i=0)*rho_j).      (6.3)\n\nTogether with (4.7), this is the complete array. Both optimum orientations\ncan also be written explicitly:\n\n    mu_0=2p*R_0+a*tau*(1-rho_0)>0,\n    mu_i=2p*R_i+tau*F_i/U>0 for i>0,\n    nu^A_i=kappa*u_i*(t_i-tbar)+gamma*Uclock*v_i\n                         -tau*(rho_i-1_(i=0)*tbar)-2alpha_i. (6.4)\n\nAll terminal outgoing signs and all outgoing optimum signs pass. No sign\nis silently assigned to nu^A or an off-diagonal -N^A.\n\n### 6.1 A first-row charge obstruction at the assigned target\n\nSince v_z=rplus*u_z, the actual first-to-last queried entry is\n\n    N^A_0z=rho_z*(a*kappa*u_0-tau)\n                                +gamma*R_0*rplus*u_z. (6.5)\n\nIf lambda_(0,z)=-N^A_0z>=0, positivity of rho_z gives\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*(U-1+p).               (6.6)\n\nApply (a*x+C)^2/(4x)>=a*C for x=kappa*u_0>0 and\nC=gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)>0. Equations (6.2) and (6.6) imply\n\n    J_A>=a*gamma*R_0*(U-1+p)*(1-rho_0)\n       >(4/9)*p*(4/3)*(9/10)=8p/15.                (6.7)\n\nThe strict lower bounds use a*b>4/9, gamma>1, R_0>bp, U>7/3 and\nrho_0<1/10. On the other hand Aword>128 gives\n\n    qtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]<8/15,             (6.8)\n\nbecause this inequality is equivalent to Aword>128. Thus no member of\nLaw A has both full PSD and lambda_(0,z)>=0 at q<=qtar. This argument\nuses the exact SINGLE global charge and no local Schur-budget summation.\n\n### 6.2 A stronger literal incoming-optimum failure for every q\n\nThe weighted clock is uniformly large. By (3.3)--(3.5), t_z>n and\nn>2^(m-1), so\n\n    tbar>=rho_z*t_z> b*n/[2*(3/2)^m]\n                     >(1/3)*(4/3)^m>27.             (6.9)\n\nFor the last inequality m>=16 and (4/3)^4=256/81>3, so (4/3)^16>81.\nMeanwhile t_1=1+b<5/2<tbar/2. Query1 has v_1=-rminus*u_1<0.\nIn (6.4) its metric term is strictly negative, its rank term is strictly\nnegative, its skew term is nonpositive, and -2alpha_1 is strictly negative.\nHence\n\n    nu^A_1=lambda_(star,1)<0                         (6.10)\n\nfor EVERY m>=1536, kappa>0, tau>=0 and every real q. This excludes\nLaw A as an original-row certificate even at larger q. It does not exclude\na balanced re-selection, which can change nu_1 and (6.5). Law B performs\none such correction explicitly; it is not dismissed by (6.10).\n\n## 7. Law B: an explicit balanced transport that repairs the entire first row\n\nKeep the SAME w,d,alpha,p and arbitrary kappa>0,tau>=0 as in Law A.\nDefine the probability column on noninitial queried samples\n\n    r_0^pool=0, r_i^pool=u_i/(U-u_0) for i>0,\n    chi=e_0-r^pool,\n    xi=gamma*R_0/a,\n    bvec_0=0, bvec_i=xi*(v_i-H*r_i^pool) for i>0,\n    B_B=chi*bvec^T-bvec*chi^T,\n    A_B=A_A+B_B.                                     (7.1)\n\nThe r^pool column is distinct from the suffix product r_L. Its denominator\nis positive, and its entries sum to1. Since sum_(i>0)v_i=H, both chi and\nbvec have sum zero. Therefore B_B is skew AND B_B e=0. In particular\nA_B e=d, and the entire factor (5.2), charge (6.2), and real derivative\n(5.3) are unchanged. There is no hidden skew unknown in this law.\n\nFor complete scalar coefficients define\nC_i=chi_i+h_i sum_(j>i)chi_j and\nE_i=bvec_i+h_i sum_(j>i)bvec_j. Then\n\n    N^B_ij=N^A_ij+C_i*bvec_j-E_i*chi_j.              (7.2)\n\nEquations (4.7), (6.3), (7.1)--(7.2) specify EVERY original coefficient.\nThe outgoing optimum coefficients are still the strictly positive mu_i\nin (6.4). The incoming optimum coefficients are\n\n    nu^B_i=nu^A_i-chi_i*(bvec^T t)+bvec_i*(chi^T t).  (7.3)\n\nNo positivity of these incoming coefficients is assumed.\n\n### 7.1 The entire first row is explicitly equalized\n\nFor j>0, B_B,0j=bvec_j. Because B_B has zero column sums,\n(T^T B_B)_0j=-a*bvec_j. Substituting (7.1) in (6.3) yields\n\n    N^B_0j=r_j^pool*S0,\n    S0=(a*kappa*u_0-tau)*(1-rho_0)+gamma*R_0*H.      (7.4)\n\nIn particular ALL original finite first-row multipliers are nonnegative\niff S0<=0, equivalently\n\n    tau>=a*kappa*u_0+gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).           (7.5)\n\nThis realizes the invariant (5.4), rather than assuming it can be repaired.\nIt changes the demanding Law A quantity U-1+p in (6.6) to the much smaller\nH/(1-rho_0). Thus the Law A charge obstruction does NOT transfer to Law B.\n\nThe simultaneous first-row and full-PSD conditions at qtar are nonvacuous.\nWithin this same two-parameter law choose the explicit member\n\n    kappa_0=gamma*R_0*H/[a*u_0*(1-rho_0)],\n    tau_0=2*gamma*R_0*H/(1-rho_0).                    (7.6)\n\nThese are positive. Equation (7.4) gives N^B_0j=0 for every j>0, and\nJ_A=a*gamma*R_0*H. From (2.1), gamma<2 and (4.4),\n\n    J_A< (25p/16)*(9p/8)=225p^2/128<2p^2.\n\nConsequently p^2+J_A<3p^2<p/2<qtar, because p<1/128 and\nqtar/p=17*Aword/[16*(2Aword-1)]>17/32>1/2. This is a strictly positive\nfull factor at the target with all finite first-row multipliers zero,\npositive terminal probabilities, and positive outgoing optimum multipliers.\nIt remains a FAILED certificate because the complete array still has a\nnegative original coefficient, as the next subsection proves.\n\n### 7.2 Last-to-previous-leaf versus incoming optimum1: exhaustive sign conflict\n\nLet j=z-2, the genuine preceding queried leaf. Both z and j are even.\nFor i,j>0 the balanced correction has the explicit entry\n\n    B_B,ij=xi*(v_i*r_j^pool-r_i^pool*v_j).            (7.7)\n\nBy (3.7), v_z/u_z=v_j/u_j=rplus. Therefore B_B,zj=0. Also\nA_A,zj=0. The last row of T^T is the last coordinate row, so exactly\n\n    N^B_zj=u_z*u_j*(-kappa/U+gamma*rplus^2).          (7.8)\n\nIf kappa<gamma*U*rplus^2, this entry is positive and the ORIGINAL\nlambda_(z,z-2) is negative. Otherwise its sign passes and necessarily\n\n    kappa>=gamma*U*rplus^2>7/12.                     (7.9)\n\nThe strict lower bound follows from gamma>1, U>7/3 and rplus>1/2.\nWe now prove that throughout this remaining domain nu^B_1 is negative.\n\nPut tpool=sum_i r_i^pool*t_i=(tbar-rho_0)/(1-rho_0). The terms -xi*(p+s)/(U-u_0), -tau/U and\n-2delta are nonpositive, with the first and last strictly negative.\nFurther,\n\n    tpool < tbar/(1-rho_0),\n    xi*rminus*tpool < (9p)*(3/2)*(10/9)*tbar\n                                      =15p*tbar.    (7.13)\n\nSince t_1<tbar/2 by (6.9), dropping the negative terms in (7.11) gives\n\n    nu^B_1/u_1<(-kappa/2+15p)*tbar<0.
