The terminal contribution\nWITH -2f_n included is\n\n    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2\n                                         -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.\n\nIts value terms cancel the prefix divergence terms. Since\nPe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2, the full identity is\n\n    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n        +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n        +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)\n\nWith ybar_w=sum_i w_i y_i/Ww and e^T zeta=0, this becomes\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n      +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2\n      +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)\n\nThe initial charge is paid once. Necessity of q>=p^2+J follows by\nsetting scalar x_0=1, y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i),\nc0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E), and g_n=-s+v^T y;\nthen every square vanishes. Equation (4.1) reconstructs all gradients.\nThus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J, on the entire original formal\nspace, not just on legal trajectories. On the actual common queried\ngradient direction g_i=G, put X=t^T Kt, Z=t^T z and theta=t^T zeta.\nThe entire prefix remainder is\n\n    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.\n\nThe common derivative has not been discarded.\n\n## 5. The literal metric, paid column, skew, and all optimum signs\n\nThe fixed common column and incoming optimum load are\n\n    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, Z=t^T z=0,\n    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.\n\nThis z is not proportional to v, since its interior coordinates vanish\nand every odd interior v_i is strictly negative. Choose\n\n    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM), omega=p^2,\n    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho,\n    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,\n    Xreq=theta0/p-4+ellload=2/p-10+ellload,\n    tbase=sum_i what_i t_i/sum_i what_i,\n    Vbase=sum_i what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,\n    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,\n    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                            (5.1)\n\nOne has 0<xiM<1/4 and0<sigma<1/3: positivity follows from\n1-a-a^2/8>1-5/12-25/1152>0, and xiM<a(1-a)<=1/4.\nEvery what_i is positive. The two unit endpoints imply\nVbase>=Delta^2/2 by minimizing their two square terms over the center.\nAlso Xreq>0. Consequently all w_i>0, X=Xreq, e^T zeta=0,\nt^T zeta=theta, and\n\n    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).\n\nUseful exact bounds are\n\n    1<theta0<2, 2<theta<3,\n    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p, Xreq<2/p,\n    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<8p,\n    theta/Xreq>p.                                  (5.2)\n\nFor the first lower bound on Xreq, theta0/p>1024*4 and\nXreq=theta0/p-4+ellload. For the ratio, subtract2 from\n[2-(10-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]; the numerator difference is\n(2b-8+ellload)p, negative in absolute value less than8p after division\nby1-(b+1)p>3/4. The lower ratio1 follows directly from the same formula.\nFinally theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+4p>0.\n\nGeneral multiplication in (4.2) gives\n\n    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,\n    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.                (5.3)\n\nThe last two identities use e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0. Here\nalpha^T t=1/p-3, V=2 and Z=0, so the two moments in (5.3) are both\nellload, precisely matching nu_target. A literal skew realizing both\nAe=d and this nu is as follows:\n\n    rvec=Kt+2v-2alpha-nu_target,\n    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,\n    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,\n    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,\n    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.4)\n\nThe two matching moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.\nHence e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Direct multiplication proves\nAe=d and At=rvec; each summand is skew. Every other compatible skew\nis A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0. Equations (4.3)-(4.4) and (5.4)\ndefine every actual coefficient, with the literal law taking C=0.\n\nAll terminal multipliers are nonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers\nare exactly ellload at index0 and zero elsewhere. Every outgoing\noptimum multiplier is strictly positive: zeta_i has the sign of\nt_i-tbase, changes sign only once, and has zero sum. Every proper\nsuffix sum is positive, using either the negative preceding prefix or\nthe strictly positive remaining tail. If zeta_i<0, then\nsum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, giving\n\n    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j\n                                  >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.\n\nIf zeta_i>=0 the expression is positive as well, including i=l.\nTogether with R_i>0 this proves mu_i>0 for all queries.\n\nFull PSD also passes. By (5.2),\n\n    J<(theta0*p/4)(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)\n      <(4225/8184)p.\n\nSince4225/8184<67/128 and p<1/128, one gets\n\n    p^2+J<(17/32)p<17p/[16(2-p)]=qtar.             (5.5)\n\nThus PSD and every optimum sign are valid for this SIGNED coefficient\nlaw. The remaining finite coefficients must still be nonnegative to\nconstitute an upper-bound certificate; Section7 proves they cannot be.\n\n## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens pass\n\nAll identities in this section follow from (4.2)-(5.4), not from\nassumed finite multiplier signs. They are unchanged by every C with\nCe=Ct=0.\n\nFor the first row define\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.\n\nThe first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Since\nPe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta and (P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu, direct multiplication gives\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\n    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0.       (6.1)\n\nIf every finite first-row multiplier is nonnegative, then\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0,                      (6.2)\n\nbecause b<=t_j-1<=Delta for j>0. These necessary conditions comprise\nthe complete total/first-moment screen for that row; they are not\nindividual coefficient signs.\n\nPut Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbase-1)>0 and dclock=1/Delta. Normalize clocks by\nxclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1]. For any base what define\n\n    B=sum what, M=sum what*xclock, M2=sum what*xclock^2,\n    B0=what_0, H=M2-M^2/B,\n    f=B0*M/B, k0=B0(1-B0/B).\n\nHere H>=1/2, B<5/2, f>2/5, H<=B/4<5/8, and\n\n    Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/2,\n    K_00/Y=dclock*(B-B0)/M.\n\nSince B-B0<3/2 and M>=1, (2.1)-(2.2) give\n aK_00/Y<(5/6)p, while theta/Xreq>p.\nWriting S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+Bsource, the remaining term is\n\n    Bsource=2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0+a(v_0^2+eta z_0^2)<7p^2.\n\nFor this use R_0<2p, v_0<u_0<p^2/64, and0<z_0<2p.\nTherefore\n\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                           (6.3)\n\n### 6.1 First-row clock, with finite errors and the load retained\n\nWrite K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Expansion of (6.1)\nusing R_0=bE-a v_0, z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 yields\n\n    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,\n    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2]\n       +2bs(2-ET)\n       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-4a]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0.           (6.4)\n\nThe incoming load cancels exactly in Gendpoint: before cancellation its\nfirst bracket is Xreq-Ttheta-(p^2-s^2)T^2 and there is +a nu_0;\nXreq-Ttheta=btheta0-4-Delta*nu_0. This uses the ACTUAL load ellload,\nnot a zero-load substitution.\n\nAs theta0=2-6p and pT=1-bp,\n\n    btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2\n                  =2b-5-4bp-b^2p^2+s^2T^2.\n\nThe bounds (2.1)-(3.3) now give\n\n    |Gendpoint-a*dclock*(2a-3)|<20p^2.              (6.5)\n\nHere the term a*dclock*(4bp+b^2p^2) is <7p^2; the s^2 term is\n<s^2/p<p^2; 2bs|2-ET|<6s<p^2; the v_0 bracket has absolute value\n<4 and contributes <p^2; aDelta v_0^2<p^2 and2a alpha_0<p^2.\nThe displayed20p^2 safely bounds their sum.\n\nFor an AUXILIARY calculation omit omega*rho only, retaining unit masses\nat0 and1 and mass sigma at1-rclock. Put\n\n    Dclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.\n\nDirect use of the base moments f,k0,H in (6.4) gives the exact interior\nmetric correction\n\n    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n      [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                               -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].        (6.6)\n\nThis is an auxiliary formula; the positive tail weights are restored\nbelow. One has 1-2p<Dclock<=1 and\n |1/Dclock-1|<rclock. The bracket differs from theta in absolute value\nby less than35p, using a*dclock<p, rclock<4p, theta<3 andXreq*dclock<2.\nThus\n\n    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.       (6.7)\n\nIndeed comparison first with xiM*rclock*theta costs <100p^2, and\nreplacing theta by2+ellload costs <6p^2.\n\nAdding omega*rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega. Both old and\nnew H are >=1/2, and the old f,k0 are <=1. Direct quotient subtraction\nbounds the changes of f,k0,H by2omega,4omega,5omega respectively;\nhence each ratio f/H,k0/H changes by at most32omega. The exact metric\npart of G0 is\n\n    a(Xreq/Delta)(2f/H-k0/H)\n                       +theta(1-a/Delta)f/H-btheta.\n\nUsing a Xreq/Delta<5/2 and theta<3 bounds its change by400omega.\nEach base is rescaled by its OWN variance and the SAME Xreq,theta;\nthere is no neglected change of scale. Combining (6.5)-(6.7),\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<526p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).\n\nSubstitution of xiM gives\n\n    Cfirst=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM\n                                      -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM\n            >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.                   (6.8)\n\nThe last inequality follows from a>2/5, eps<=1/6144:\n4/125-3/24576>1/32. Also |Cfirst|<3. Since p<2^(-40),\n\n    p/64<G0<7p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<7p-1/32<0.               (6.9)\n\nEquations (6.3),(6.9) prove the entire screen (6.2).\n\n### 6.2 Last-row clock, including the nonzero incoming-load cost\n\nThe last row of Ltri^T is e_l^T, so\n\n    N_ll=P_ll,\n    (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.\n\nFor the last row define\n\n    Slast=sum_(j<l)N_lj=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-1)N_lj=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj\n                  =(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nNonpositive off-diagonals require Slast<=0, Jlast<=0 andHlast>=0.\nDefine actual base ratios\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/H,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/H.\n\nThen (Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast,\nzeta_l=(theta/Delta)Alast. Both ratios are positive and <3.\nFor the auxiliary base without omega*rho,\n\n    Alast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.\n\nRestoring omega*rho changes this ratio by at most32omega: its numerator\nchanges by at most3omega, H by5omega, H>=1/2 and the old numerator<=1.\nThus the actual Alast>=1-xiM-32p^2.\n\nUsing V=2, alpha_l=b-v_l, nu_l=0 and z_l=-E/Delta gives the EXACT\nlast-row invariant\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n       +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b-hgap*eta*z_l^2,\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.              (6.10)\n\nSince T=Delta+1,\n Cclock=2Xreq/Delta-theta+theta*(hgap-1)/Delta.\nBy (5.2),\n\n    2-ellload-20p<Cclock<3,\n    Cclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-40p.\n\nThe subtracted metric term has magnitude <24p, using Blast<3,\nhgap<2, Xreq/Delta<2 and1/Delta<2p. Also\neta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4.\nThe function4v-hgap*v^2+2p(b+hgap)v is increasing for a<=v<=v_l<1/2,\nsince its derivative is >2. Consequently (6.10) implies\n\n    Hlast>2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-65p\n          =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-65p\n          >3/8000-65p>1/4000.                      (6.11)\n\nHere the positive eps*a^2 term was dropped. The difference\n(2/5)^3/4-1/64 is3/8000. The load penalty ellload(1-xiM) is therefore\nexplicitly paid; it is not hidden in an asymptotic error.\n\nFinally mu_l=2p v_l+zeta_l<19p and P_ll>=v_l^2>a^2>4/25.\nThus Slast<19p-4/25<-1/8. Since (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l-2alpha_l<14,\n\n    Jlast<14-(3/(4p))*(4/25)<0.\n\nEvery last-row total/first-moment condition passes. Neither endpoint\nscreen, even together with all optimum signs and full PSD, guarantees\nnonnegative entries in the full array.\n\n## 7. Decisive column failure of the prescribed law\n\nThe incoming optimum equation in (4.3) is a COLUMN equation:\n\n    sum_i N_il=nu_l+2alpha_l=2alpha_l.\n\nIf each off-diagonal N_il were nonpositive, it would imply\nN_ll>=2alpha_l. But N_ll=P_ll for every skew A, not just (5.4).\nThe metric scale is small:\n\n    scale<=2Xreq/Delta^2<8p,\n    K_ll<=w_l=scale*(1+omega*rho_l)<12p.\n\nTogether with (3.3) and eta*z_l^2<4p^4, this gives\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta*z_l^2<12p+1/4+4p^4<1/3,\n    P_ll-2alpha_l<1/3-19/10=-47/30<-3/2.            (7.1)\n\nThus the putative nonnegative incoming transport masses have negative\ntotal:\n\n    sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)lambda_(i,l)\n      =sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)(-N_il)=P_ll-2alpha_l<-3/2.\n\nAt least one original finite edge INTO the last queried node has a\nnegative multiplier. This conclusion is invariant under all Ce=Ct=0\ncorrections and, more generally, under any skew with the same incoming\ncolumn sum. The complete prescribed law fails for every m>=1536.\nThis is not a primal counterexample, an infeasibility proof for all\npossible coefficient architectures, or a conclusion about W itself.\n\n## 8. Second mechanism: transport with diagonal exclusion and the full factor\n\nThis section treats an entire family, rather than adding another metric\natom. First give the exact finite transport interface for any fixed\nP,mu,nu,alpha in the architecture above. Write\n\n    D_i=N_ii, a_ij=-N_ij for i!=j, a_ii=0,\n    r_i=D_i-mu_i,\n    c_i=D_i-nu_i-2alpha_i,\n    b_i^clock=t_i D_i-(Nt)_i.                       (8.1)\n\nHere a_ij denotes transport mass and is unrelated to the physical scalar\na. If the original finite multipliers are nonnegative, then a_ij>=0 and\n\n    sum_j a_ij=r_i, sum_i a_ij=c_j,\n    sum_j t_j a_ij=b_i^clock.                       (8.2)\n\nThus every r_i,c_j is nonnegative, sum r=sum c, and\nsum_i b_i^clock=sum_j t_j c_j. A zero r_i forces b_i^clock=0.\nFor positive r_i the mean b_i^clock/r_i lies in the interval spanned\nby {t_j:j!=i}; retaining the exclusion is essential at either endpoint.\nWith Kmass=sum r=sum c, the forbidden diagonal further implies\n\n    r_i+c_i<=Kmass.                                 (8.3)\n\nIndeed all mass from row i must land outside column i, whose total\ncapacity is Kmass-c_i. This is a true cut inequality.\n\nThere are elementary clock cuts as well. For any real tau,\n\n    sum_i max(0,b_i^clock-tau*r_i)\n                              <=sum_j c_j max(0,t_j-tau).       (8.4)\n\nFor each row, max(0,sum_j a_ij(t_j-tau))<=sum_j a_ij max(0,t_j-tau);\nsumming proves (8.4). More generally for any convex function phi on[1,T],\nfinite convexity gives\n\n    sum_(i:r_i>0) r_i phi(b_i^clock/r_i)\n                                      <=sum_j c_j phi(t_j).    (8.5)\n\nThis is merely the finite convex-combination inequality, provable by\ninduction on the number of nonzero row entries, and imports no transport\nexistence theorem. It is necessary, not sufficient for the original law.\nThe following more general elementary cut keeps the missing diagonal\nvisible: if real Ucut_i,Vcut_i,Zcut_j obey\nUcut_i+Vcut_i*t_j+Zcut_j>=0 for EVERY i!=j, then\n\n    sum_i Ucut_i*r_i+sum_i Vcut_i*b_i^clock+sum_j Zcut_j*c_j>=0. (8.6)\n\nMultiply each allowed coefficient by a_ij>=0 and sum. No condition on\nUcut_i+Vcut_i*t_i+Zcut_i is imposed, because that edge is forbidden.\n\nCrucially, the chronological symmetric-factor constraint remains\n\n    Ltri^(-T)N+N^T Ltri^(-1)=2P.                    (8.7)\n\nA transport coupling satisfying row/column masses and clock conditions\nalone may violate (8.7). This equality cannot be replaced by PSD of P.\nFor precision, given D and an array a with zero diagonal, nonnegative\noff-diagonals, and the row and column sums in (8.2), define\nN=diag(D)-a. If (8.7) holds, then A=Ltri^(-T)N-P is skew.\nFor the fixed architecture mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta, one has\nAe=Ltri^(-T)mu-Pe=d. Moreover\n(P-A)t=N^T e=nu+2alpha, so At=Pt-2alpha-nu.\nThus this system recovers exactly the required skew and all original\ncoefficients; the clock equations follow with\n\n    Nt=Ltri^T(2Pt-2alpha-nu).\n\nThis is an equivalence of constraint descriptions, NOT an asserted\nfeasible unknown matrix or a new certificate law. We use it only to\nprove the explicit family exclusion below.\n\n### 8.1 Last-column cut and a metric concentration inequality\n\nThe last row of Ltri^(-T) is e_l^T. The (l,l) entry of (8.7) therefore\npins D_l=P_ll. The nonnegative column mass c_l gives\n\n    K_ll+eta*z_l^2 >= 2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2.   (8.8)\n\nThis combines the transport column cut with an exact original-space\nfactor constraint. Allowing D_l to float freely would lose it.\n\nFor ANY strictly positive metric weights w, set X=t^T Kt and\n\n    Qgap=sum_(i<l) w_i (T-t_i)^2 / sum_(i<l)w_i.\n\nThe weighted variance identity is\n\n    X=(1/Ww)sum_(i<j)w_i w_j(t_i-t_j)^2.\n\nRetaining only pairs involving l and using\nK_ll=w_l(Ww-w_l)/Ww gives the exact necessary estimate\n\n    X>=K_ll*Qgap, so K_ll<=X/Qgap.                  (8.9)\n\nEvery denominator is positive since w_i>0 and the clocks are distinct.\nFor d0>0 define the NONLAST far-clock fraction\n\n    Ffar(d0)=sum_(i<l:T-t_i>=d0)w_i / sum_(i<l)w_i.\n\nThen Qgap>=d0^2*Ffar(d0). If\n Bneed:=2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2-eta*z_l^2>0,\n(8.8)-(8.9) imply the quantitative concentration requirement\n\n    Ffar(d0)<=X/[d0^2*Bneed].                       (8.10)\n\nThis is a global statement about ALL nonlast metric weights; it does\nnot assume any sparse support or any special skew completion.\n\n### 8.2 A full family excluded, including arbitrary sparse metric atoms\n\nFix any same-enclosing-m word as in Section2. Consider ALL choices with\n\n (i) alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1-p, alpha_l>=1/2;\n (ii) v=k-alpha, z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, eta=F/E;\n (iii) arbitrary positive w with X=t^T Kt<=3/p;\n (iv) Ffar(Delta/2)>=256p;\n (v) any skew A and any nonnegative incoming optimum vector nu\n     satisfying the original equations (4.2)-(4.3).\n\nThere is no restriction to the literal skew (5.4), to nu=ellload e_0,\nor to the paid minimum-energy zeta. If the divergence and PSD happen\nto pass, they still cannot repair the following finite signs.\n\nFor alpha_l in[1/2,1-p], the function\n2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2 is strictly increasing, because its derivative\nis2+2b-2alpha_l>0. Using b<17/12 gives\n\n    2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2\n                >=1-(b-1/2)^2>23/144.              (8.11)\n\nAlso eta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4<1/144.\nOn the other hand, (8.9) and condition(iv) yield\n\n    Qgap>=64p*Delta^2,\n    K_ll <=(3/p)/(64p*Delta^2)<1/12,                (8.12)\n\nwhere Delta>3/(4p) was used. Therefore\n\n    c_l=P_ll-nu_l-2alpha_l\n       <1/12+1/144-23/144=-5/72<0.                 (8.13)\n\nThis contradicts a nonnegative column mass. Every member of the entire\nprecisely specified family has some negative original multiplier into\nl. No existence theorem, unknown optimizer, asymptotic coefficient law,\nor numerical evaluation enters this exclusion.\n\nAs a concrete sparse-metric subfamily, take ANY finite subset\nJsubset of {1,...,l-1}, arbitrary coefficients Mlast>0 and b_j>=0,\nand omega0>0, with\n\n    what=e_0+Mlast*e_l+sum_(j in Jsubset)b_j e_j+omega0*rho,\n    1+sum_j b_j+omega0 <= 1/(256p).\n\nScale what by ANY positive scalar for which t^T Kt<=3/p. The positive\nregularizer retains every coordinate. The initial atom is far from T,\nand its fraction of the nonlast base weight is at least\n1/(1+sum_j b_j+omega0)>=256p. Hence every such metric lies in the\nexcluded family whenever the terminal mass is >=1/2 and the common\ncolumn is as above. The number and positions of interior atoms and the\nlast weight are unrestricted. Outcome and exact scope\n\nThe prescribed late common column genuinely repairs the last-column\nshortage. Its full original-space PSD condition, all optimum signs,\nlast adjacent-pair cut, and both endpoint row-moment screens pass for\nevery SAME enclosing m>=1536. Even after changing the detail\nmatrix, its clock energy, the paid column, and the common column,\nfull original-space PSD implies the source capacity bound\n\n    N_00 <= b^2 q/a.\n\nConsequently nonnegative finite incoming coefficients require\n\n    nu_0 + 2alpha_0 <= b^2 q/a.                    (1.1)\n\nFor q<=17/(16D), the right side is <6p<1/128, whereas the prescribed\nnu_0 is1/64. No PSD-detail repair retaining that incoming load can\nsucceed in this architecture. No unspecified PSD matrix is assumed\nfeasible: the second mechanism derives a necessary inequality for\nall members and gives literal chronological detail families it covers.\n\nThis is a restriction on coefficients and their paid initial charge.\nIt is not full-dual infeasibility, not a legal primal counterexample,\nnot a lower bound for W, and not a resolution of the original question.\nThe surviving freedom includes nu_0 of order p or zero, with all other\nloads and individual signs then rederived. No third law is attempted.\n\nEvery new result below belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.\nNo earlier unverified finding is used as an accepted intermediate lemma.\nOnly accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used at their\nstated scopes. The latter's799/800 implication remains conditional.\n\n## 2. Original target, coordinates, and uniform scalar bounds\n\nFor a fixed nonnegative length-n word h and L>0, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional\nconvex differentiable L-smooth functions with a minimizer and x_0!=x_star,\nusing x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original problem is\nwhether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and\nU_n^ZJ the exact all-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark, not a decimal profile.\n\nAccepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the physical hierarchy and the scalar\ncomparison B_n/D<47/50, where U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its separation conclusion\nrequires the still-unproved full bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for all m>=1536.\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation orientation\nand exact unqueried-terminal reduction. No queried sample is deleted.\n\nFix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. All children retain these SAME parameters:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,\n    S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).\n\nAll short notation is LOCAL to this report. In particular rho below is\na probability vector, not the benchmark exponent; T is a scalar clock,\nLtri a matrix, and a superscript T means transpose. Public names ending87\nare aliases for this report's definitions, not project-glossary overrides.\nLet e be the all-ones column on the n queried indices and e_i its coordinate\ncolumns. Define\n\n    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, Ltri_ij=0 for j>i,\n    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,\n    T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1, hgap=1+beta, t_r=T-hgap.\n\nThe last three steps are (b,hgap,b). Elementary bounds are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12,\n    1-2a>a^2, 7/5<b<17/12, 15/8<hgap<2,\n    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40), 3/(4p)<Delta<1/p,\n    Delta-b>1/(2p), p<dclock:=1/Delta<4p/3,\n    0<rclock:=hgap/Delta<4p.                       (2.1)\n\nFor the lower a bound use sqrt(2)<10/7 and\n7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5. Its upper bound follows from beta/(1+eps)<1.\nThe quadratic inequality follows from1+a<sqrt(2).\nThe hierarchy mass, also obtained by substitution into\n1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is\n\n    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,\n    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n\nThis proves the p bounds; the remaining clock inequalities follow by\nsubstituting Delta=1/p-b-1. These are symbolic all-m inequalities,\nnot evaluated recurrences or asymptotic approximations.\n\nFor integer suffix length Ls in[0,n], let sbit(Ls) be its binary digit\nsum and put\n\n    rtail_Ls=product_(j=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_j-1)\n             =beta^Ls*c^(-sbit(Ls)), s=rtail_n,\n    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j), u_i=|k_i|,\n    U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.\n\nIndeed h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), where v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing j,\nand sum_(j=1)^Ls v2(j)=Ls-sbit(Ls). With Ls=l-i,\n\n    u_i=rtail_Ls+rtail_(Ls+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,\n    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_(l-2)=b*a*beta.\n\nThe last three u terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18, so U>7/3.\nAlso 2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, and\nbeta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2. Hence beta^n<2^(-4m), and\n\n    u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, s<p, rho_i>0.                 (2.2)\n\nFinite product telescoping gives\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S. Set\nZtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n rtail_Ls and Mtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n Ls*rtail_Ls.\nExpansion over binary digits gives\n\n    Ztail=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),\n    U=2Ztail-1-s,\n    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).\n\nChoose j with m/2<2^j<=m; Bernoulli's inequality gives its summand\n>3m/28. An index shift gives\nsum_i rho_i(l-i)=(2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns)/U>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2,\nusing ns<1 and positivity of the numerator. Each suffix mass\n1/p-t_i is at least b+l-i. Since\nVtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i), this proves Vtail>32 for m>=1536.\n\nKeep the EXACT assigned mixture\n\n    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),\n    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT rho], alpha_star=p,\n    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.\n\nSince4/3<Vlast<17/12 and Vtail>32,\n\n    0<xiT<1/45, e^T alpha=1-p, V:=t^T v=2,\n    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,\n    0<alpha_0<u_0, 0<v_0<u_0,\n    e^T v=E, 1-p/32<eta<1.                       (2.4)\n\nFor example alpha_l>44(1-p)/45>19/20 and\nv_l=b-alpha_l<17/12-19/20<1/2. At0,\nalpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1. The eta bound uses2s/E<p/32.\nThe vector R=Ltri^T v satisfies\n\n    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0,\n    R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.                             (2.5)\n\nThe first identity follows by subtracting Ltri^T alpha from (2.3).\n\n## 3. Full original-space identity and literal coefficients\n\nNormalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the original formal\nvectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n independently. From accepted1252 use\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nPut y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 on every queried index.\nThe actual GD substitutions give\n\n    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),\n    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y. For every i,j<n,\n\n    N_ij=P_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_uj+A_uj). Using Pe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2 gives\n\n    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2\n       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.          (3.4)\n\nLet ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. Completing the weighted squares gives\n\n    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2\n       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+zeta_i*x_0/(2w_i)||^2\n       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,\n    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                   (3.5)\n\nNecessity is literal: set scalar x_0=1,\ny_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), c0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E),\nand g_n=-s+v^T y. Every displayed square vanishes; (3.1) recovers\nall gradients. It would be false to mark ALL columns as passed.\nSpecifically at least one original lambda_(j,0), 1<=j<=l, is negative.\nNo compatible skew correction changes that conclusion. Having found\nthis cut, there is no need or justification to search individual signs\nof the enormous array. The remaining tests below distinguish which\nof the proposed repairs really work.\n\n### 5.1 Last column really is repaired\n\nThe scale is at most2Xreq/Delta^2<8p, so K_ll<=w_l<12p.\nBut the new common column gives\n\n    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta b^2>21/10,\n    c_l=P_ll-2alpha_l>1/10.                      (5.6)\n\nIndeed v_l>a>2/5, eta>1-p/32 and b>7/5 imply\nv_l^2+eta b^2>53/25-(49/800)p>21/10.\nThe incoming optimum mass nu_l is zero. Thus the prior last-column\nobstruction is removed, rather than merely overlooked.\n\n### 5.2 The last adjacent-pair factor cut passes\n\nLet hgap=h_r. Since the last two rows of Ltri^T are e_l^T and\ne_r^T+hgap e_l^T,\n\n    N_lr+N_rl=2P_lr+hgap P_ll.                   (5.7)\n\nThe skew terms cancel exactly. If both off-diagonals are nonpositive,\nthe right side must be nonpositive. For the prescribed law it equals\n\n    2K_lr+hgap K_ll\n     +v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)\n     +eta b[2E-eps*b].                          (5.8)\n\nHere K_lr<0, v_r=-a*hgap-alpha_r, and v_l<1/2. Thus\nv_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)<-(3/25)hgap<-9/40.\nThe metric term is <24p and the positive part of the common term\nis <4p. Therefore\n\n    2P_lr+hgap P_ll<-1/5.                       (5.9)\n\nThis necessary pair test passes, but does not remedy (5.5).\n\n## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens for Mechanism1\n\nThese are row tests, distinct from the column equation (5.5). They\nare included to audit the nonzero-clock change jointly, not as a\nsubstitute for the failed column test.\n\nDefine first-row quantities\n\n    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,\n    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,\n    G0=P0-Delta*S0.\n\nNonpositive first-row off-diagonals require\n\n    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                   (6.1)\n\nFrom (3.2), (4.5), and the first row b e^T-a e_0^T,\n\n    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\n    P0=b(4s+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0\n                       +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0. (6.2)\n\nFor the new z_0=0, the nonmetric part of S0 is\n2pR_0-2bs v_0+a v_0^2<7p^2. Hence (5.4) gives\n\n    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                        (6.3)\n\nFor the clock, put K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and\nY=Xreq/Delta+eY. Direct expansion of (6.2) gives\n\n    G0=(a/Delta)(b*theta0-4-eta Z^2)\n       +2bFZ-2pT R_0+4bs-4a v_0+2a alpha_0\n       +2bsDelta v_0-aDelta v_0^2+Epert,\n    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.  (6.4)\n\nThe incoming load cancels exactly in this identity because\nXreq-Ttheta=b*theta0-4-eta Z^2-Delta*ellload,\nand the expansion includes the original +a nu_0=a ellload.\nNo incoming load has been suppressed.\n\nFor an auxiliary base omit only omega rho, keeping its three literal\natoms at0,1,1-rclock with weights1,1,sigma. Put\nDclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.\nUsing B=2+sigma, M=1+sigma(1-rclock), and\nM2=1+sigma(1-rclock)^2 in the displayed definitions gives exactly\n\n    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)\n       [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)\n                         -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].              (6.5)\n\nSince1-2p<Dclock<=1, |1/Dclock-1|<rclock,\na*dclock<p, rclock<4p, Xreq*dclock<2 and theta<3,\nthe bracket differs from theta by less than35p. Consequently\n\n    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.    (6.6)\n\nRestoring omega rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega.\nBoth variances are >=1/2. Direct quotient subtraction bounds the\nchanges of fbase,kbase,Hvar by2omega,4omega,5omega, respectively,\nand those of fbase/Hvar,kbase/Hvar by32omega. The exact metric part\nof G0 is\n\na(Xreq/Delta)(2fbase/Hvar-kbase/Hvar)\n       +theta(1-a/Delta)fbase/Hvar-btheta.\n\nIts change is <400p^2. Each base is scaled by its own variance and\nthe same Xreq; hence this includes the change of scale.\n\nIn (6.4), replacing theta0 by2 and eta Z^2 by Zstar^2 costs\nless than42p^2. Replacing2bFZ by2b*dclock*Zstar costs less than63p^2,\nsince |p-dclock|<4p^2 and |Z-Zstar|<4p. Replacing-2pT R_0\nby-2b*dclock costs less than20p^2; use R_0=bE-a v_0 and pT=1-bp.\nThe five remaining s,v_0,alpha_0 terms total less than5p^2.\nTogether with (6.6) and the restored positive tail, this proves\n\n    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<1000p^2,\n    Cfirst=a(2b-4-Zstar^2)+2b(Zstar-1)\n                                  +xiM*hgap*(2+ellload)\n          =Cold+b*hgap*(2-a*b*hgap),\n    Cold=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).             (6.7)\n\nSubstitute xiM and hgap=2-eps to obtain\n\n    Cold=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM\n                                  -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM\n         >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.\n\nHere a>2/5 and eps<=1/6144 give4/125-3/24576>1/32.\nAlso a*b*hgap<85/72<6/5 and b*hgap>21/8. Thus the extra term\nin (6.7) exceeds21/10, so Cfirst>2. A coarse direct bound gives\n|Cfirst|<20. Therefore\n\n    p<G0<30p,\n    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<30p-1/32<0.            (6.8)\n\nEquations (6.3),(6.8) establish the entire first-row screen (6.1).\n\nFor the last row put\n\n    Slast=mu_l-P_ll,\n    Jlast=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,\n    Hlast=(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.\n\nThese are respectively the row total, its clock relative to1, and its\nclock relative to t_r. Nonpositive off-diagonals require\nSlast<=0, Jlast<=0, Hlast>=0. Set\n\n    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/Hvar,\n    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/Hvar.\n\nThey are positive and <3, with\n(Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast.\nFor the auxiliary three-atom base,\nAlast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.\nRestoring omega rho changes this ratio by at most32p^2, by the same\nquotient estimate. Now expansion gives the exact formula\n\n    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast\n          +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b\n          +eta*b*(2Z-hgap*b),\n    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.            (6.9)\n\nBy (4.3), Cclock>2-ellload-64p and Cclock<3. Hence\nCclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-100p.\nThe negative metric term has magnitude <24p. The v_l polynomial is\nincreasing for a<=v_l<1/2, because its derivative is greater than2.\nThe terms in (6.9) before the common-column contribution therefore exceed\n\n    2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-125p\n     =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-125p\n     >3/8000-125p>1/4000.\n\nThe exact incoming-load loss ellload(1-xiM) is retained.\nSince Z>3, hgap*b<17/6, eta>9/10 and b>7/5, the new common\ncontribution in (6.9) exceeds3. Thus Hlast>3.\nFinally mu_l=2p v_l+(theta/Delta)Alast<19p, whereas P_ll>21/10.\nAlso (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l+2eta Zb-2alpha_l<26. It follows that\n\n    Slast<-2, Jlast<26-(21/10)Delta<0, Hlast>3.   (6.10)\n\nBoth endpoint row screens really pass. The first incoming COLUMN,\nnot either endpoint row screen or the last adjacent pair, kills the law.\n\n## 7. Mechanism2: explicit chronological PSD details and their global price\n\nWe now enlarge the detail term itself, instead of changing another\nweight within one weighted-variance metric. The identified constraint\nis the first diagonal's ability to pay a column load under a small\ninitial charge. Both clock-bearing and clock-annihilating details are\nallowed below; the former change the paid column and all load moments.\nTheir price is recomputed, not assumed to vanish.\n\nOn the ORIGINAL queried indices define the literal chronological vectors\n\n    gdetail_i=e_i-e_(i+1), 0<=i<=n-2,\n    ddetail_i=h_(i+1)e_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))e_(i+1)+h_i e_(i+2),\n                                           0<=i<=n-3.\n\nThey obey\n\n    e^T gdetail_i=0, t^T gdetail_i=-h_i,\n    e^T ddetail_i=0, t^T ddetail_i=0.              (7.1)\n\nFor the latter clock identity substitute\n(t_i,t_(i+1),t_(i+2))=(t_i,t_i+h_i,t_i+h_i+h_(i+1));\neach term cancels. Thus zero sum alone does NOT imply zero clock energy.\n\nChoose this ONE explicit enlarged coefficient law:\n\n    gamma_i=Xreq/[2(n-1)h_i^2], 0<=i<=n-2,\n    tau_i=p/[1+h_i+h_(i+1)]^2, 0<=i<=n-3,\n    Hclock=sum_(i=0)^(n-2) gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T,\n    Hnull=sum_(i=0)^(n-3) tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T,\n    Qdetail=K/2+Hclock+Hnull.                      (7.2)\n\nEvery vector and weight is an original-index formula. No optimizer,\nrecurrence replay, matrix-existence claim, or selected support is used.\nAll sums are finite symbolic definitions, not executed computations.\nThe entries of K/2 correspond to the positive weights w/2. Consequently\nQdetail is PSD and positive definite on e-perp. From (7.1),\n\n    Qdetail e=0,\n    t^T Hclock t=sum_i gamma_i h_i^2=Xreq/2,\n    Hnull t=0, t^T Qdetail t=Xreq.                (7.3)\n\nRetain the same terminal pool and late common column, and REPLACE\n\n    zeta by zeta_new=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t,\n    P by Pnew=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d by dnew=zeta_new+F(v-z).\n\nUse (4.6) with Kt replaced by Qdetail t, d replaced by dnew, and\nrvec=Qdetail t+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target. This again is a literal skew\ncompletion and, with (3.3), specifies EVERY original multiplier.\nEquations(7.3) give e^T zeta_new=0 and t^T zeta_new=theta, so both\nincoming-load compatibility moments remain ellload. The incoming\noptimum vector is exactly nu_target; it is not a free unpriced correction.\n\nThe complete polynomial is (3.4) with K,zeta replaced by\nQdetail,zeta_new. In particular its paid part equals\n\n    (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)^T Qdetail\n                   (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)\n        +(q-p^2-theta^2/(4Xreq))||x_0||^2.         (7.4)\n\nThis equality follows by expanding the square and using (7.3).\nNecessity of this same charge follows with scalar x_0=1,\ny=-(theta/(2Xreq))*t+c0 e, choosing c0 to cancel the common square,\nand choosing the independent g_n to cancel the terminal square.\nThus the exact global charge remains\n\n    q>=p^2+theta^2/(4Xreq),                       (7.5)\n\nwhich passes at qtar by (4.4). There is no extra hidden initial cost,\nno omitted clock energy, and no uncancelled terminal square.\n\nAll optimum signs pass for this second law as well. Hnull t=0, and\nLtri^T Kt has strictly positive entries by the argument following (4.6).\nFor Hclock t write aedge_i=gamma_i h_i>0, with aedge_(-1)=aedge_l=0.\nThe i-th coordinate of Hclock t is aedge_(i-1)-aedge_i; its suffix\nafter i is aedge_i. Therefore\n\n    (Ltri^T Hclock t)_i=aedge_(i-1)+(h_i-1)aedge_i>0\n\nfor every i, interpreting endpoints by the zero conventions. This\nuses h_i>1. Multiplication by theta/Xreq>0, addition of the half-K\nterm and 2pR proves every mu_new,i>0. The incoming and terminal signs\nare the already specified nonnegative ones.\n\nThe source data have changed explicitly:\n\n    Qdetail_00=K_00/2+gamma_0+tau_0*h_1^2,\n    zeta_new,0=zeta_0/2-theta/[2(n-1)b].           (7.6)\n\nThus this mechanism changes the first diagonal and paid source column,\nnot just a row-clock summary. Clock-free details also alter diagonals:\nthe tau_0 term in (7.6) must be included, even though Hnull t=0.\nThe full failure of this enlarged law follows from the next global\ninequality, which covers considerably more freedom than (7.2).\n\n### 7.1 A source-capacity inequality for the entire PSD-detail class\n\nFor this necessary-condition argument allow ANY symmetric PSD Qdetail\nwith Qdetail e=0; no such matrix is asserted to solve a feasibility\nproblem. Allow ANY zero-sum paid vector zeta and ANY common vector z\nwith e^T z=E. Keep v=k-alpha, e^T alpha=1-p, 0<s<p, eta=F/E,\nand the original coefficient formulas\n\n    P=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,\n    d=zeta+F(v-z), A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).\n\nThe incoming vector may now vary as well. Assume only that the full\noriginal polynomial at level q is PSD. That assumption will be used\nto derive a necessary inequality, not to posit a feasible completion.\nFor any scalar x,u choose\n\n    x_0=x,\n    y=u e_0+[x-(z_0/E)u]e,\n    g_n=-s*x+v^T y.\n\nThese are permissible original formal directions by invertibility\nof Ltri. The common and terminal squares vanish EXACTLY. Because\nQdetail e=0 and e^T zeta=0, full PSD implies, for every x,u,\n\n    Qdetail_00*u^2+zeta_0*x*u+(q-p^2)*x^2>=0.    (7.7)\n\nIn particular the following explicitly evaluated quantity is nonnegative:\n\n    Rsource=Qdetail_00+(b/a)zeta_0\n                         +(b^2/a^2)(q-p^2)>=0.   (7.8)\n\nThe first diagonal formula (5.2) holds unchanged:\n\n    N_00=b(2s v_0+2Fz_0-zeta_0)\n                    -a(Qdetail_00+v_0^2+eta z_0^2).\n\nNow expand these THREE nonnegative terms:\n\n    b^2 q/a-N_00\n       =a Rsource\n         +a(v_0-bs/a)^2\n         +a*eta*(z_0-bE/a)^2 >=0.                (7.9)\n\nTo check the constant exactly, eta E=F and\ns^2+eta E^2=p^2. The constants on the right are therefore\n(b^2/a)[q-p^2+s^2+eta E^2]=b^2 q/a;\nthe linear terms are b*zeta_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,\nand the quadratic terms are a(Qdetail_00+v_0^2+eta z_0^2).\nThis verifies (7.9) term by term without an inverse or external theorem.\n\nIf the actual finite incoming multipliers at the first query are\nnonnegative, their total is N_00-nu_0-2alpha_0>=0. Hence\n\n    nu_0+2alpha_0 <= N_00 <= b^2 q/a.             (7.10)\n\nThis bounds the source load by the ENTIRE paid initial budget.\nIt cannot be repaired by moving clock energy between details,\nusing clock-annihilating details, changing their ranks or supports,\nchanging zeta, changing z (including its clock), or changing skew\ncorrections while keeping the large source load and small q.\nIt even allows a different terminal distribution with nonnegative\nalpha_0, as long as the displayed architecture and p,s apply.\n\nFor the physical parameters, b^2/a<(17/12)^2/(2/5)<6 and qtar<p.\nThus, whenever q<=qtar,\n\n    N_00<6p<1/128,\n    N_00-(1/64)-2alpha_0<-1/128                  (7.11)\n\nif alpha_0>=0. This proves a uniform failed ORIGINAL first column\nfor the explicit enlarged law (7.2), and for the full class in this\nsubsection retaining nu_0=1/64. It is independent of all compatible\nskew corrections. Equivalently that fixed load requires q>1/384,\nwhereas qtar<p<2^(-40).\n\nFor clarity, the obstruction includes arbitrary finite nonnegative\nweights on the literal vectors gdetail_i,ddetail_i, arbitrary positive\nvariance metrics, their sums, and any other zero-sum PSD detail\nconstruction. Only necessary inequalities are claimed for this class;\nthere is no arbitrary unknown PSD object adopted as a certificate.\n\n### 7.2 Moment accounting for the surviving enlargement freedoms\n\nA general sum H=sum gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T\n+sum tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T, with nonnegative scalar weights,\nhas He=0, t^T Ht=sum gamma_i h_i^2, and its second sum annihilates t.\nIf its clock energy Eclock is less than Xreq, the explicit replacement\nQdetail=(1-Eclock/Xreq)K+H has total clock energy Xreq.\nFor zeta=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t the exact charge is again (7.5),\nand the incoming total and clock are again ellload. This describes\nthe larger parameter class covered by (7.10); (7.2) is its single\nfully fixed original-index instance used in this report.\n\nIf all details annihilate both e and t and one leaves K,zeta fixed,\nthen the optimum vectors and load moments remain unchanged, but\nN_00 decreases by a H_00, N_ll increases by H_ll, and the last-row\nclock defect changes by -hgap H_ll. The latter follows from\nH t=0, mu unchanged, and the last row of Ltri^T being e_l^T.\nThese diagonal and endpoint changes still have to be paid; null\nclock energy is not permission to ignore them.\n\nIf one changes z, alpha, or the incoming vector beyond the fixed\nlaw, the general compatibility equations are\n\n    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),\n    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t,\n    V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, X=t^T Qdetail t,\n    theta=t^T zeta.\n\nThus changing nu_0 is a real global-moment change. The current\nnecessary bound requires nu_0+2alpha_0=O(p) at the target scale.\nIt does not assert that such a smaller load is sufficient, nor\nexclude a nonnegative incoming distribution supported elsewhere.\nNo attempt to choose a third coefficient law is made in this round.\n\n## 8. Scope, failed paths, literature, and resource record\n\nMechanism1 fails because its O(p) first diagonal cannot pay the\nfixed1/64 incoming optimum coefficient, despite genuine repair of\nthe last column and success of both endpoint row screens.\nMechanism2 changes the PSD detail term, pays all clock energy, and\nkeeps the exact initial charge; it still fails the uniform source cut.\nThe common cause is the global paid source capacity, not a poor\nchoice of skew completion or an untested transport existence claim.\n\nThe new report also diagnoses the earlier source-loaded laws: any\nmember of this architecture using nu_0=1/64 and q<=qtar fails (7.10),\nregardless of any previously successful endpoint row checks. This\nis awareness about unverified candidates, not a revocation of facts.\nNo accepted fact claims those candidates are legal.\n\nThe original minimax target stays OPEN. Accepted1252 permits retaining\na full certificate with no incoming UNQUERIED terminal edges; it does\nnot eliminate queried pairs. Accepted931 supplies only the scalar\nbenchmark comparison and its explicitly conditional separation.\nNeither a negative multiplier nor the formal vectors used in (7.7)\nare asserted to be a legal smooth-convex primal trajectory.\n\nSkills used: query-memory, obtain-immediate-conclusions,\nsearch-math-results, construct-counterexamples, identify-key-failures.\nThe counterexample being tested is coefficient-level sufficiency of\nPSD/optimum signs/endpoint screens, not the original primal conjecture.\nThe scoped proof establishes its failure by the full first-column sum.\n\nRetrieval: program_stage=mature_subproblem; active_program=full original\ncoefficient architecture with late common column and PSD details;\nmissing_mechanism=simultaneous paid column capacity;\nsearch_mode=repair, then the task-authorized scoped obstruction.\nOne Matlas query returned unrelated matrix/network/control statements,\nnone used. One web background search located the Schur-complement\nsection of Boyd--Vandenberghe's Convex Optimization at\nhttps://stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf . No theorem from it is\nimported or cited as a proof premise. No extra literature layer was\nneeded: the enlarged branch reduces directly to a two-variable\nquadratic evaluation in the already audited coefficient coordinates;\n(7.7)--(7.9) contain the whole elementary argument.\n\nThe no-match glossary search for the proposed suffix87 public names\nreturned exit1 and empty output; no existing definition was overwritten.\nSome initial aggregated recall outputs were truncated; the needed prior\nreport, latest guidance and accepted interfaces were reread in bounded\nsections. No mathematical result is certified by these text readbacks.\n\nResources: ONE invocation87, TWO substantive mechanisms, only ONE\nprescribed original law and ONE explicit enlarged law. Hence the four residue classes k=4q+d have radii theta^q,x theta^q,xy theta^q,theta^(q+1), with theta=x²y. {"id": "6464238b92a5b90e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T08:59:39.575950+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Changing symmetric PSD contractions, even with all pairwise cocoercivity and an exact GD step, do not ensure one legal smooth-convex function: an explicit two-dimensional three-point counterexample.", "evidence": "Status: refuted. Normalize L=1, x_*=g_*=0. Let x_0=(1,0), g_0=(1/2,1/2), h_0=1, x_1=(1/2,-1/2), g_1=(1/2,0). Then x_1=x_0-h_0 g_0. For every pair i,j in {*,0,1}, <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j>=||g_i-g_j||^2 (values for (*,0),(*,1),(0,1) are 1/2,1/4,1/4). The gradients are radial PSD-contraction images: g_0=P_0 x_0 with P_0=(1/2)[[1,1],[1,1]], and g_1=P_1 x_1 with P_1=diag(1,0); both are orthogonal projections. Nevertheless no convex differentiable 1-smooth f can interpolate these gradients and have 0 as minimizer. Necessity of smooth-convex interpolation gives f_i-f_j >= <g_j,x_i-x_j>+(1/2)||g_i-g_j||^2. Taking both directions against * forces f_0-f_*=1/4 and f_1-f_*=1/8. Taking (i,j)=(0,1) forces f_0-f_1 >= (1/2,0) dot (1/2,1/2)+1/8=3/8, whereas the forced difference is 1/8. Equivalently the cycle *->1->0->* has positive total lower-increment weight 1/8+3/8-1/4=1/4. Thus all pairwise two-cycle tests omit a decisive three-cycle. Source for the necessary interpolation inequality: Taylor, Hendrickx, Glineur, Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4 / Corollary 1; downloaded paper and read proof. This is a mechanism counterexample, not a verified fact or a resolution of (P).", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"mechanism": "noncommutative PSD-curvature product lifting", "target_claim": "Pairwise cocoercivity plus GD recursion and PSD curvature imply smooth-convex realizability", "status": "refuted", "normalization": "L=1, ||x_0-x_*||=1", "evidence_status": "explicit derivation; not verifier-submitted"}, "glossary": {"P_0": "Orthogonal projection onto span((1,1)) in this counterexample", "P_1": "Orthogonal projection onto the first coordinate axis in this counterexample"}}
{"id": "2a21f2929be856fc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:02:15.425856+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C1 refuted: arbitrarily long recent chronology, even together with the initial jet and every sampled point and gradient, need not determine feasible future objective values. A one-dimensional fixed-schedule example has a factor-16 difference after forgetting one old function value.", "evidence": "Normalize L=1 and x_*=0, f_*=0. Define F by F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=0, 0 for 0<=x<=1, (x-1)^2/2 for 1<=x<=3, and 2x-4 for x>=3. Define G(x)=integral_0^x q(t)dt, where q(t)=t for t<=1/2; q(t)=1/2 for 1/2<=t<=11/6; q(t)=t-4/3 for 11/6<=t<=10/3; q(t)=2 for t>=10/3. Both derivatives are continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz; 0 is a minimizer with value 0. At x=4 both jets are (value,gradient)=(4,2). At x=3/2 both gradients are 1/2, but F=1/8 and G=5/8. Both functions agree on the entire negative half-line. For arbitrary integer m>=1, take h=(5/4,5,1/2 repeated m times,1+(5/4)2^m). Both functions, starting at x0=4, generate exactly the same sampled points and gradients through the cut: x1=3/2; x_{2+j}=-2^{-j}, 0<=j<=m. The final step sends the common cut x=-2^{-m} to y=5/4. All steps are positive, finite and predetermined. Any convex 1-smooth function interpolating the full F-prefix obeys f(y)<=1/32: on [5/4,3/2], f'(t)>=1/2-(3/2-t)=t-1, hence f(y)<=1/8-integral_{5/4}^{3/2}(t-1)dt=1/32; F attains equality. Any function interpolating the full G-prefix has f(y)=1/2: on [0,3/2], convexity and smoothness imply f'(t)<=min(t,1/2); the integral of this upper envelope equals 5/8=f(3/2), so continuity forces equality everywhere, giving f(5/4)=1/2. Thus conditional terminal maxima differ by 16 despite the same initial jet, minimizer, radius, entire schedule, all points and gradients, and any prescribed fixed-length recent history (choose m large). In W normalization the two conditional values are 1/256 and 1/16 because ||x0||^2=16. The only differing sampled datum is the forgotten old value at x1=3/2. This refutes exact gluing or conditional-state kernels that discard that datum without an equivalent encoding. It does NOT refute all finite-dimensional sufficient statistics, determine the unrestricted worst case of this schedule, or resolve (P). Explicit self-contained construction, not verifier-accepted yet.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"mechanism": "chronological finite-window interpolation state", "status": "refuted", "scope": "conditional completion sets for fixed legal GD histories; not all finite-dimensional state methods"}, "glossary": {"F": "First explicit one-dimensional convex 1-smooth interpolant in the history counterexample.", "G": "Second explicit one-dimensional convex 1-smooth interpolant in the history counterexample.", "q": "The continuous piecewise-affine derivative of G.", "m": "Arbitrary positive integer controlling the common recent tail length.", "y": "The common final point 5/4 in the counterexample.", "C1": "Lossless block gluing using only boundary or fixed chronological-window data."}}
{"id": "992e7e9e6a1039d7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:13:47.850691+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The full smooth-convex worst-case W cannot be a function of the terminal scalar schedule polynomial: for every real H>2, W_2(H,1)=(H-1)^2/3 > W_2(1,H), although both terminal polynomials are (1-z)(1-Hz).", "evidence": "Complete self-contained proof: research/max2_order_sensitivity.md. Normalize L=1, x_*=0, f_*=0, ||x_0||=1. The smooth-convex inequality I follows directly by applying the descent lemma to f-<grad f(v),.>, whose minimum is at v; thus no unverified external lemma is used. For one unit GD step y=x-u, u=grad f(x), v=grad f(y), I gives f(y)<=<u,x>-||u||^2-||v||^2/2 and f(y)<=<v,x>-<v,u>-||v||^2/2. Averaging, with w=(u+v)/2, yields f(y)<=<w,x>-(3/2)||w||^2-(1/8)||u-v||^2<=||x||^2/6. Combining the first bound with f(y)>=||v||^2/2 also yields ||v||^2<=||x||^2/4. Hence for H>=2 descent from the first unit step gives W_2(1,H)<=1/3+(H^2-2H)/4. Conversely a first H-step satisfies ||x_0-Hg_0||^2<=1+H(H-2)||g_0||^2<=(H-1)^2 by cocoercivity and ||g_0||<=1. The subsequent unit-step bound gives W_2(H,1)<=(H-1)^2/3. Equality is attained by delta=(H-1)/3 and the globally convex differentiable 1-smooth asymmetric Huber function f(x)=x^2/2 for x>=-delta and f(x)=-delta x-delta^2/2 for x<=-delta. Its derivative max(x,-delta) is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz; minimizer is 0. From x_0=1 the iterates are x_1=-3delta,x_2=-2delta, giving 2f(x_2)=3delta^2=(H-1)^2/3. The gap between this exact value and the opposite order's upper bound is H(H-2)/12>0. For H=3 this reads W_2(3,1)=4/3>13/12>=W_2(1,3). Status: explicit refutation of an order-blind exact characterization, not a refutation of (P) or of every order-blind lower bound; not submitted under selective-verification policy.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_order_sensitivity.md", "mechanism": "terminal scalar schedule polynomial", "status": "exact order-blind characterization refuted", "evidence_status": "complete derivation, unverified"}, "glossary": {"H": "An arbitrary real long step greater than 2 in the two-step permutation example.", "delta": "The asymmetric Huber threshold (H-1)/3 in this example."}}
{"id": "7806c71bf93294c6", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T09:17:50.105343+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The local AR(1) rule h_i>=2, r_i=-1/(h_i-1), and constant gradient energy does not guarantee smooth-convex interpolation, already at h=(2,3).", "evidence": "Set x_star=0, x_0=(1,0), x_1=(-1,0), x_2=(2,0), g_0=(1,0), g_1=(-1,0), g_2=(1/2,sqrt(3)/2). If a convex1-smooth interpolant minimized at0 existed, the smooth-convex inequality in both directions against0 would force f_i-f(0)=1/2. But the inequality for i=2,j=0 requires f_2-f_0>=<g_0,x_2-x_0>+||g_2-g_0||^2/2=1+1/2=3/2. Contradiction. This exact argument invalidates only the stated local extension rule.
