{"id": "8519238ab086ffdb", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T10:42:42.820832+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The recursive-family obstruction extends beyond a terminal affine cap: every legal scalar function whose sampled gradients initially follow one quadratic and whose remaining iterates then stay on one side of the minimizer has normalized final error <=1458 c^13/(a c^m), c=13/5,a=c^-17. Thus arbitrary one-sided nonlinear continuations and interior interpolation switches still miss the exact profile on H_m.", "evidence": "# A recursive family defeats the combined ray, quadratic, and single-cap channels\n\nStatus: complete symbolic candidate, not verifier-accepted. Worker: max3.\nNo extra verifier allocation is assumed. The result bounds specified lower-bound\nfunctionals from above. It does not bound the true smooth-convex worst case from\nabove and does not decide the project's minimax target.\n\n## Statement and definitions\n\nPut c=13/5 and a=c^(-17). Define the ordered lists H_0=() and\n\n    H_m=(H_(m-1), a c^(m-1), H_(m-1)),   m>=1.\n\nTheir lengths are N_m=2^m-1. Write b_u=a c^u for the step at level u and\ns_m=sum H_m. Directly from the recursion,\n\n    s_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2),    s_(m+1)=2s_m+b_m.\n\nF_ray is precisely the verified functional of fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a, including\nall its sign, remote-offset, old-slope, and retained-area constraints. K is the\nfunctional of fact b98bb807d514e80d. Define the full quadratic functional by\n\n    Q_n(h)=max_{0<=lambda<=1} lambda product_(t<n)(1-lambda h_t)^2.\n\nFor clarity, define also the algebraic single-cap functional F_cap_n. For each\nlambda in (0,1], sigma in {-1,1}, and checkpoint j<n, put\n\n    p_t=product_(v<t)(1-lambda h_v),\n    R=max(0,sigma p_t:0<=t<=j), A=sigma p_(j+1), T=sum_(v>j)h_v.\n\nTake the supremum of\n\n    2 lambda r A - lambda(1+2 lambda T)r^2\n\nover A>0 and radii R<=r<=A/(1+lambda T), and include zero. This is exactly\nthe candidate quadratic-core/terminal-cap family, but its upper bounds below\nuse only this displayed definition, not any unverified prior lemma. It is a\nlegal lower-bound family directly: integrate the derivative of lambda x^2/2\nuntil sigma x=r and continue with constant derivative sigma lambda r beyond\nthat point. The record constraint preserves the quadratic prefix, while\nA-lambda rT>=r preserves the affine suffix. The derivative is continuous,\nnondecreasing, and lambda-Lipschitz; the minimum is zero at zero and x_0=1.\n\nFor every m>=18 the candidate conclusions are\n\n    F_ray_(N_m)(H_m)=K_(N_m)(H_m)<=1/(1+s_(m-1))\n                                      <=8/(5a c^m),\n    Q_(N_m)(H_m)<=1/(a c^m),\n    F_cap_(N_m)(H_m)<=729 c^13/(a c^m).\n\nIn addition the exact benchmark recursions satisfy\n\n    S_circle(n)+1<=(n+1)^(4/3),\n    B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1<=1+4n^(4/3).\n\nConsequently, since c^3=2197/125>16,\n\n    max(F_ray_(N_m), Q_(N_m), F_cap_(N_m))(H_m) / U_(N_m)^ZJ -> 0.\n\nThus even adding the one-terminal-cap extension to the verified ray functional\nand all convex quadratic witnesses cannot give the desired uniform comparison.\nThe comparison fails along an explicit subsequence of horizons, which suffices\nto refute a proposed comparison uniform over all horizons and all schedules.\n\n## 1. Elementary scale estimates\n\nLet d=2/c=10/13. Since d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5,\nd^17=d^15 d^2<1/40. Therefore for u>=17,\n\n    s_u/b_u=(1-d^u)/(c-2)>=(39/40)/(3/5)=13/8.\n\nWrite eta=13/8. Then eta>1, eta^2=169/64>13/5=c.\nEvery step b_u>1 has u>=18, so this estimate applies to every eligible\ncheckpoint. At ineligible levels b_u<=1, the inequality s_u>=b_u-1 also\nholds. Thus s_u>=b_u-1 for every level u, and s_u>=eta b_u whenever u>=17.\n\n## 2. Every feasible ray chain has at most one checkpoint\n\nThe j-th step of H_m, indexed here by 1<=j<2^m, is b_u where u is the\nlargest integer for which 2^u divides j. Each such occurrence has an exact\ncopy of H_u immediately before it and an exact copy immediately after it.\n\nConsider proposed first two checkpoints at indices i<j, with step levels\nu and v. Both steps must exceed one for a feasible chain: otherwise the\nverified first crossing or second return has nonpositive distance budget.\nLet P be the sum before i, and M the sum strictly between i and j.\nThe verified recurrence gives the distance budget after the second crossing as\n\n    [(b_u-1)(b_v-1)/(1+M)-P]/(1+P).\n\nAny chain of at least two checkpoints needs this budget positive. If more\ncheckpoints follow, the next magnitude must be positive; if these are the\nonly two, the terminal old-slope constraint is at least m_0>0. It therefore\nsuffices to show P M >= b_u b_v, which is a stronger obstruction.\n\nIf u>=v, the copy of H_u before i yields P>=s_u. The copy of H_v before\nj lies strictly after i, since j-i is a positive multiple of 2^v; hence\nM>=s_v. Thus P M>=s_u s_v>=b_u b_v.\n\nSuppose u<v. If i lies before the copy of H_v immediately preceding j,\nthen M>=s_v and P>=s_u, with the same conclusion. Otherwise i lies inside\nthat copy. Let P_loc be the sum in that copy strictly before i. The local\ncopies of H_u on each side of i give\n\n    P_loc>=s_u, M>=s_u, P_loc+M=s_v-b_u, P>=P_loc.\n\nFor two nonnegative numbers with this fixed sum and each at least s_u,\ntheir product is at least s_u(s_v-b_u-s_u). This follows by factoring\n(P_loc-s_u)(M-s_u)>=0.\n\nFor fixed u, put D_v=s_u(s_v-b_u-s_u)-b_u b_v. At v=u+1,\n\n    D_(u+1)=s_u^2-c b_u^2>=0.\n\nFor v>=u+1, using s_(v+1)-s_v=s_v+b_v and b_(v+1)=c b_v,\n\n    D_(v+1)-D_v=s_u(s_v+b_v)-(c-1)b_u b_v\n                  >=(3-c)b_u b_v>0,\n\nbecause s_u>=b_u and s_v>=b_v. Thus D_v>=0 for all v>u. This proves the\npair obstruction and rules out every chain with two or more checkpoints.\nNo offset or retained-area term has been dropped from a feasible chain;\nthe exact first-return condition prevents such a chain from existing.\n\n## 3. Bound on all remaining single cuts\n\nAt an occurrence of b_u, prefix and tail sums P,T satisfy\n\n    P,T>=s_u, P+T=s_m-b_u.\n\nPut V=s_m-b_u-s_u. Since b_u+s_u increases with u,\n\n    V>=s_m-b_(m-1)-s_(m-1)=s_(m-1).\n\nOn P in [s_u,V], the logarithm of\n(1+P)^2[1+2(s_m-b_u-P)] is concave. Its minimum is therefore at an\nendpoint. If b_u>1, the two endpoint values for the cut ratio are bounded by\n\n    (b_u-1)^2/[(1+s_u)^2(1+2V)] <=1/(1+2V),\n    (b_u-1)^2/[(1+V)^2(1+2s_u)]\n                               <=b_u/(1+V)^2.\n\nHere s_u>=b_u-1. Also b_u<=b_(m-1)<=1+s_(m-1) and V>=s_(m-1),\nso both ratios are at most 1/(1+s_(m-1)). Ineligible cuts give zero. The\nglobal Huber term 1/(1+2s_m) obeys the same bound. Fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a\ntherefore gives F_ray=K<=1/(1+s_(m-1)). For m>=18 the scale estimate yields\n1/(1+s_(m-1))<=c/(eta a c^m)=8/(5a c^m).\n\n## 4. Exact quadratic-product damping\n\nFor lambda in (0,1], put z=a lambda. The whole-block polynomial is\n\n    P_m(lambda)=product_(u=0)^(m-1)(1-b_u lambda)^(2^(m-u-1)).\n\nChoose the integer k>=17 such that c^k z<=1<c^(k+1)z, and set\ny=c^k z in (1/c,1]. If a factor is zero, the following whole-block bounds\nare immediate. Otherwise, when m>k,\n\n    log|P_m| / 2^(m-k-1)\n       =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1) 2^(-j) log|1-c^j y|.\n\nFor j<0 use log(1-c^j y)<=-c^j y. For j>=1 use\nlog|1-c^j y|<=log(c^j y)=j log c+log y; these latter upper bounds are\nnonnegative because c^j y>1. Extending only these nonnegative bounds to an\ninfinite series gives\n\n    log|P_m| / 2^(m-k-1)\n       <=-2y(1-d^k)/(c-2)+log[y(1-y)]+2log c\n       <=-(13/4)y+log(1/4)+2log c\n       <=-5/4+log(169/100)\n       <=-5/4+69/100=-14/25<-1/2.\n\nThe endpoint y=1 gives a zero factor when m>k and was already handled.\nWe have proved\n\n    |P_m(lambda)|<=exp(-2^(m-k-2))  if m>k.\n\nIf m<=k, every factor has b_u lambda<=1, so |P_m|<=1. In particular\n|P_m(lambda)|<=1 for every m and lambda in [0,1].\n\nIf lambda<=1/(a c^m), the desired weighted bound follows immediately.\nOtherwise m>k. Put r=m-k>=1. Then\n\n    lambda |P_m|^2\n       <=(1/(a c^m)) c^r exp(-2^(r-1))\n       <=1/(a c^m).\n\nThe last inequality uses c<e, hence r log c<r<=2^(r-1). For example,\ne>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3>13/5, so no decimal estimate is required. Taking the\nmaximum over lambda proves the Q bound. This is also the full quadratic\nclass: diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian with spectrum in [0,1]\nmakes normalized terminal error a weighted average of these scalar errors.\n\n## 5. Uniform prefix bound and the entire one-cap extension\n\nDefine R_u(lambda)=P_u(lambda)(1-b_u lambda), a prefix block of length2^u.\nEvery prefix of H_m of length ell, 0<=ell<=2^m-1, has polynomial equal to\nthe product of R_u over the nonzero binary digits of ell. This follows by\nsuccessively consuming its largest complete block (H_u,b_u), of length2^u;\nthe remaining list begins another copy of the same lower-level pattern.\n\nFor u<=k, all factors of R_u have b_v lambda<=1, so |R_u|<=1. For\nu=k+r with integer r>=1, the damping estimate gives\n\n    |R_u|<=c^r exp(-2^(r-2)).\n\nFor r>=4, 2^(r-2)>=r and c<e, so this bound is at most1. For r=1,2,3\nit is less than3: for r=1 use c<3; for r=2,3 use e>8/3 and respectively\nc^2/(8/3)<3 and c^3/(8/3)^2<3. There are therefore at most three factors\nexceeding one in any prefix, each less than3. Every prefix satisfies\n\n    |p_t(lambda)|<=27.\n\nWe also need the weighted bound\n\n    lambda |R_u(lambda)|^2 <= c^12/(a c^u).\n\nIf lambda<=1/(a c^u), it follows from |R_u|<=1. Otherwise u=k+r with\nr>=1, and the left side is at most\n\n    (1/(a c^u)) c^(3r) exp(-2^(r-1)).\n\nFor r<=4 this is at most c^12/(a c^u). For r>=5, 2^(r-1)>=3r and\nc<e make its extra factor at most1. This proves the bound for every u.\n\nNow consider any feasible single-cap checkpoint j in H_m. Maximizing over\nall real r, rather than only its legal interval, gives the rigorous upper bound\n\n    2 lambda r A-lambda(1+2lambda T)r^2\n                           <=lambda A^2/(1+2lambda T).\n\nLet u=m-1, and consider the central step b_u. If j precedes it, the tail\ncontains b_u, so T>=b_u, while |A|<=27. The cap payoff is at most\n729/(2b_u). If j is central or follows it, the prefix contains the complete\nblock (H_u,b_u), followed by a prefix of H_u. Hence |A|<=27|R_u| and\nthe cap payoff is at most lambda A^2<=729 c^12/(a c^u).\nBoth cases are bounded by 729 c^13/(a c^m). All signed-record and radius\nfeasibility constraints were retained in defining the family; enlarging the\nradius domain here only makes this upper bound more permissive.\n\n## 6. A sufficient bound on the exact benchmark recurrence\n\nThis part uses the exact phi/psi recursions in fact b98bb807d514e80d, not an\nasymptotic decimal constant. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1. Their shifted joins are\n\n    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nThe first joins C_k,C_(n-k-1), and the second joins C_k,B_(n-k-1).\nBoth are increasing in their nonnegative arguments.\n\nFor 0<=t<=1,\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.\n\nTo prove this, put x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3). Then x^3+y^3=1 and\n\n    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4\n      =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.\n\nEach reciprocal is at least1/3, and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3; the latter strict\ninequality follows by cubing, since1/4<8/27. Endpoints cause no division\nproblem. Since Omega is the positive root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, this proves\n\n    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).\n\nInduction in the exact primitive recursion now gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).\nAlso sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence G(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the exact\ndominant recursion gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3)\n\nfor every split, so it holds for the maximum. The base values are C_0=B_0=1.\n\nCombining Sections2--5 with this recurrence bound gives\n\n    max(F_ray,Q,F_cap)(H_m)/U_(N_m)^ZJ\n      <=[729 c^13/a] c^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nbecause 2^(4/3)<c. This completes the candidate restricted-channel result.\n\n## Structural consequence and next mechanism\n\nThis family has recursive large steps separated by substantial symmetric\nblocks. Those blocks prevent the first outward return in the ray construction\nand damp every quadratic scale; the prefix damping also defeats a lone terminal\ncap. A next witness must permit nonlinear changes followed by additional\nreturns and further changes, or use a genuinely different multidimensional\ninteraction. This result does not establish that these schedules perform well\non that richer class. No conclusion about W_(N_m)(H_m) from above is made.\n\nLiterature retrieval: a Matlas search for the displayed recursive-product\nclaim returned unrelated results, none used. Every new estimate above is\nproved in the text. Correctness status remains unverified.\n\n## 7. The same family controls every two-curvature homogeneous objective\n\nThis extension is part of the same unverified candidate proof. It does not\nassume correctness of a separate unverified finding. For u,v in (0,1], define\n\n    f_(u,v)(x)=u x^2/2 for x>=0, and v x^2/2 for x<=0.\n\nIts derivative is continuous, monotone, and 1-Lipschitz, including across zero;\nzero is a minimizer. Write F_two_n(h) for the supremum of normalized terminal\nerror on this family with either initial point +1 or -1. The claim is\n\n    F_two_(N_m)(H_m)<=c/(a c^m),   m>=18.\n\nScaling or reflecting the initial point adds no larger case, by positive\nhomogeneity and by allowing both initial signs. Random mixtures and separable\ndirect sums of such objectives cannot exceed their supremum, since their\nnormalized errors are convex combinations of component errors.\n\nIt suffices first to consider u<=v, with neither reciprocal equal to a step\nlevel. The reverse order follows by reflecting the objective. The exceptional\nparameter pairs follow by continuity: each finite GD update and final objective\nis continuous in (u,v), even at a sign change or zero iterate. All bounds are\nuniform in the parameters. The cases with a zero curvature follow by the same\nlimit if they are included in the definition.\n\nLet I be the first integer level with b_I v>1, and J the first with b_J u>1.\nThen 18<=I<=J. A whole block H_l maps each starting sign to a deterministic\nending sign and nonnegative magnitude. Represent these two magnitudes by the\ncolumns of a two-state map. Before level I, both signs are preserved, and the\nwhole-block magnitudes are P_l(u),P_l(v), both positive. If m<=I, the two\ntrajectories are ordinary quadratics, already bounded by Section4.\n\n### 7a. Different first-crossing levels: I<J\n\nThe step b_I crosses from the negative side to the positive side but leaves\nthe positive side positive. Thus H_(I+1) sends both initial signs to the\npositive side. Its amplitudes, from positive and negative starts respectively,\nare\n\n    A_(I+1)=(1-b_I u)P_I(u)^2,\n    D_(I+1)=(b_I v-1)P_I(u)P_I(v).\n\nEvery larger whole block also ends positive from both starting signs, because\nthe last copy of the preceding block has this property. Put\n\n    r_0=D_(I+1)/A_(I+1)>0.\n\nThe ratio D_l/A_l stays equal to r_0. Indeed composition with the central\nstep yields\n\n    (A_(l+1),D_(l+1))=(1-b_l u)(A_l^2,A_l D_l),  l<J,\n    (A_(l+1),D_(l+1))=(b_l u-1)(A_l D_l,D_l^2),  l>=J.\n\nIn particular, for I+1<=l<=J,\n\n    A_l=P_l(u),\n    D_l=(b_I v-1)P_I(v) P_l(u)/[(1-b_I u)P_I(u)].\n\nAll factors of P_l(u) in this range are positive. Dividing by\n(1-b_I u)P_I(u) removes a submultiset of its factors with total step mass\nb_I+s_I. Since 1-t<=exp(-t), it follows that\n\n    A_l<=exp(-u s_l),\n    D_l<=exp(-u s_l) exp(u(s_I+b_I)-v s_I+log(b_I v-1)).\n\nSet S=s_I/b_I>=eta>1, t_u=b_I u<1 and t_v=b_I v>1. The exponent in\nthe latter extra factor is at most\n\n    (S+1)-S t_v+log(t_v-1)\n       =1-S(t_v-1)+log(t_v-1)<=-log S<=0.\n\nHere the maximum of log w-Sw over w>0 is -1-log S, found by differentiating.\nConsequently both whole-block amplitudes obey exp(-u s_l) up to l=J.\nTheir common terminal side has curvature u, so for I< m<=J the error is\n\n    <=u exp(-2u s_m)<=1/(2e s_m)<=1/(a c^m),\n\nusing s_m>=eta a c^m.\n\nFor l>=J, put E_l=max(A_l,D_l). The exact recurrence gives\n\n    E_(l+1)<=E_l^2(b_l u-1),    E_J<=exp(-u s_J).\n\nLet t=b_J u in (1,c) and m=J+r with r>=1. Expanding this scalar recursion,\n\n    log E_m / 2^(r-1)\n      <=-2u s_J+sum_(j=0)^(r-1)2^(-j)log(c^j t-1)\n      <=-2eta t+2log c+2log t\n      <=-2eta+2<-1.\n\nThe series bound uses log(c^j t-1)<=j log c+log t and sums the nonnegative\nupper bounds to infinity. The last inequality uses log c<1 and the fact that\n-2eta t+2log t decreases on t>=1 because eta>1. Thus\n\n    u E_m^2<=u exp(-2^r)\n       <=[c/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^r)<=c/(a c^m),\n\nsince u<c/(a c^J), log c<1, and r<=2^r. This covers I<J completely.\n\n### 7b. Equal first-crossing levels: I=J\n\nAt level I both signs cross, so H_(I+1) interchanges the two starting signs.\nEvery larger block also interchanges them: an interchange, the central\ninterchange, and another interchange compose to an interchange. Let A_l be\nthe amplitude from a positive start and D_l the amplitude from a negative\nstart. Then\n\n    A_(I+1)=(b_I u-1)P_I(u)P_I(v),\n    D_(I+1)=(b_I v-1)P_I(u)P_I(v),\n    A_(l+1)=A_l^2(b_l v-1),\n    D_(l+1)=D_l^2(b_l u-1),     l>=I+1.\n\nPut S=s_I/b_I>=eta, t_u=b_I u>1, t_v=b_I v>1. Both t_u,t_v are less\nthan c. All earlier factors are positive, so P_I(w)<=exp(-w s_I).\nFor m=I+r, r>=1, the exact recursion therefore implies\n\n    log A_m / 2^(r-1)\n      <=log(t_u-1)-S(t_u+t_v)\n                   +sum_(j=1)^(r-1)2^(-j)log(c^j t_v-1)\n      <=[-S t_u+log(t_u-1)]+[-S t_v+log t_v]+2log c.\n\nFor any t>1, -St+log(t-1)<=-S-1-log S. Also -St+log t<=-S, because\nS>1. The displayed bound is consequently\n\n    <=-2S-1-log S+2log c<=-2eta+1=-9/4<-2.\n\nThe identical estimate for D_m follows after interchanging u,v. Thus both\namplitudes are at most exp(-2^r). Their terminal curvatures are at most\nv<c/(a c^I), giving normalized error at most\n\n    [c/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^(r+1))<=c/(a c^m).\n\nThis proves the asserted two-curvature bound. In conjunction with Sections1--6,\nthe maximum of F_ray, Q, F_cap, and F_two still has ratio tending to zero\nrelative to the exact ZJ benchmark along N_m=2^m-1.\n\n### Scope of the stronger exclusion\n\nThe proof controls a continuous two-parameter nonlinear family, with repeated\nsign changes and inner returns. It does not control a derivative whose slope\nchanges more than once along either ray, nor a genuinely coupled multidimensional\nfunction. Such changes remain concrete mechanisms to investigate. Six fixed\ncurvature pairs were checked using only forty compositions of a two-state map\n(about0.1 seconds, no long trajectory stored); those checks suggested the proof\nbut are not used in it. No numerical sweep or asymptotic computation is evidence\nfor the claimed bound.\n\n## 8. Arbitrary monotone continuations after a quadratic-gradient prefix\n\nOne can enlarge the single-cap class substantially without changing this\nobstruction. Consider any legal scalar convex1-smooth function with minimizer0,\ninitial point1, and a checkpoint j such that its sampled gradients through j\nsatisfy g_t=lambda p_t, where p_t=product_(v<t)(1-lambda h_v) and\nlambda in(0,1]. Function values on the prefix need not equal quadratic values.\nAfter step j, allow an arbitrary nonlinear continuation, with the sole\nrestriction that all subsequent iterates stay on the same side of zero. A\nzero iterate makes every later iterate zero and can be discarded as trivial.\nThe supremum of terminal error over this enlarged class, for H_m, is at most\n\n    1458 c^13/(a c^m),   m>=18.\n\nHere is a direct proof, not an appeal to a prior unverified claim about the\ncontinuation. Let A=|p_(j+1)| and T=sum_(t>j)h_t. Reflect the current side so\nthat its coordinates are positive. Convexity implies every later gradient is\nnonnegative; the no-crossing assumption and nonnegative steps make the\ncoordinates and gradients nonincreasing. Let x_f and g_f be the final radius\nand gradient magnitude. Then\n\n    x_f+T g_f<=A,    0<=g_f<=x_f.\n\nFor a monotone1-Lipschitz derivative with endpoint value g_f and value zero\nat the origin, its integral from0 to x_f is at most\nx_f g_f-g_f^2/2. Indeed its pointwise upper bound there is min(r,g_f), whose\nintegral has that value. Consequently\n\n    2f(x_f)<=2A g_f-(2T+1)g_f^2<=A^2/(2T+1).       (8.1)\n\nSet u=m-1, so the central step is b_u. If j is at or before the central\nstep, then T>=s_u, while Section5 gives A<=27. Thus (8.1) is at most\n729/(1+2s_u), below the asserted bound.\n\nIf j follows the central step, both p_j and p_(j+1) contain the complete\nfactor R_u and a prefix factor of H_u. Hence each has magnitude at most\n27|R_u|. There are two cases.\n\nIf lambda h_j<=1, this checkpoint does not cross zero. Since every later\npoint is closer to zero on the same side as p_j, monotonicity of the global\nderivative gives g_f<=lambda |p_j|. The integral bound, or just convexity,\nthen gives\n\n    2f(x_f)<=2x_f g_f<=1458 lambda |R_u|^2\n                               <=1458 c^13/(a c^m).\n\nIf lambda h_j>1, the checkpoint is a crossing. Its level v obeys b_v>1,\nand its following exact copy of H_v is included in the suffix. Therefore\nT>=s_v>=eta b_v>eta/lambda. Combining this with (8.1) yields\n\n    2f(x_f)<=729|R_u|^2/(1+2T)\n             <=[729/(2eta)]lambda |R_u|^2\n             <=[729/(2eta)]c^13/(a c^m).\n\nThis proves the stated bound. It applies to every monotone continuation\nconsistent with all old gradient data, including many changes of curvature and\ninterior interpolation gaps; neither a constant tail nor a preserved quadratic\nprefix value was assumed. It makes no assertion about a continuation with\nfurther sign crossings. Thus adding arbitrary curvature changes on one final\nray does not repair the earlier restricted constructions on this family. The\nnew scalar mechanism must permit nonlinear changes followed by additional\ncrossings of the minimizer.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_recursive_channel_obstruction.md", "extends_finding": "4d3911499920adbe", "scope": "quadratic sampled-gradient prefix followed by arbitrary no-crossing suffix; not general scalar functions", "status": "complete symbolic candidate; no verifier allocation", "mechanism_consequence": "A successful scalar repair here needs nonlinear changes followed by further sign crossings, beyond a single terminal switch.", "fact_ids": ["1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "b98bb807d514e80d"]}, "glossary": {"A": "Absolute coordinate at the end of the quadratic-gradient prefix, in Section8.", "T": "Sum of the remaining suffix steps in Section8.", "x_f": "Final distance to the minimizer in the one-sided continuation.", "g_f": "Final derivative magnitude in the one-sided continuation.", "H_m": "Explicit binary-recursive schedule in the evidence.", "c": "Fixed growth factor13/5.", "a": "Fixed scale c^-17.", "R_u": "Whole-block factor for the quadratic prefix (H_u,b_u), as defined in Section5."}}
{"id": "12ea2963a6e53e67", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:32:42.491816+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "An endpoint-only proof-weight correction cannot certify the standard three-step objective schedule h=(sqrt(2),1+sqrt(2),3/2) at its tight denominator D=6+4sqrt(2). The quadratic equality fixes the correction a=(1+sqrt(2))/2; a middle-cut Huber equality then makes the intrinsic objective energy strictly negative. Internal proof-weight changes are necessary for this template.", "evidence": "# The optimal three-step objective schedule needs more than endpoint proof-weight corrections\n\nStatus: complete unverified counterexample to a proposed certificate template. This is not a counterexample to the schedule, which is a standard optimal recursive objective schedule. No verifier call.\n\nFor a schedule h of length n and sum S, consider the restrictive proof-weight template\n\n  u_0=h_0+a,\n  u_i=h_i for1<=i<n,\n  u_n=b.\n\nHere a,b are real, and actual weights must be nonnegative. If the weight mass is the Huber-tight value D=2S+1, then b=S+1-a. Write d=x_0-x_n, U=sum_i u_i g_i, e=U-d. This template has the exact linear relation\n\n  e=a g_0+b g_n.                                    (1)\n\nThe intrinsic objective certificate is E_h(u)>=0, where\n\n  E_h(u)=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2\n          +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||U||^2.\n\nFor L=1 and minimizer0 it satisfies the exact identity\n\n  E_h(u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)\n           -2D f_n-||x_n-e||^2.                    (2)\n\nThe template is attractive because a one-step optimal schedule h=(3/2) has weights(2,2), hence a=1/2,b=2, and E=4(-Q_10)>=0. But it already fails on a basic three-step objective schedule.\n\nSet t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and\n\n  h=(t,lambda,3/2),\n  S=5/2+2t,\n  D=2S+1=6+4t=2lambda^2.\n\nThis schedule is ConPD((sqrt(2)),(3/2)). The bridge is lambda, by direct substitution in psi. The argument below only needs the explicit steps and two legal equality examples; it does not assume its optimality among all schedules.\n\n## Quadratic equality fixes the sole free endpoint parameter\n\nUse f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1. All Q_(i,star)=0, and\n\n  x_3=p=(1-t)(1-lambda)(1-3/2)\n       =-1/(2+t)=-1/sqrt(D).\n\nThus2D f_3=Dp^2=1. From(1)–(2),\n\n  E_h(u)=-[p-a-bp]^2.\n\nAny valid nonnegative intrinsic energy must therefore have a+(b-1)p=0. Substituting b=S+1-a gives the unique value\n\n  a=-Sp/(1-p)=S/(sqrt(D)+1)=lambda/2,\n  b=S+1-a=(3/2)(2+t).                              (3)\n\nThe associated weights are\n\n  u=((1+3t)/2,lambda,3/2,(3/2)(2+t)),\n\nand all are positive.\n\n## The middle-checkpoint Huber equality contradicts those weights\n\nPut c=1/lambda and q=t/(4lambda). Define the one-dimensional convex1-smooth function\n\n  f(x)=x^2/2 for -q<=x<=c,\n  f(x)=cx-c^2/2 for x>=c,\n  f(x)=-qx-q^2/2 for x<=-q.\n\nIts derivative is x clipped to[-q,c], which is nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz; f is differentiable, f(0)=min f=0. Starting at x_0=1, the specified GD steps give\n\n  x_1=c,\n  x_2=c-1=-t/lambda=-4q,\n  x_3=-4q+(3/2)q=-(5/2)q,\n  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(c,c,-q,-q).\n\nEvery sampled point is on an outer affine piece or its boundary. Therefore Q_(i,star)=f_i-g_i x_i+g_i^2/2=0 at all four samples. Also\n\n  f_3=(5/2)q^2-q^2/2=2q^2,\n  2f_3=4q^2=1/(2lambda^2)=1/D.\n\nThis is the middle-cut equality construction from accepted facts b98bb807d514e80d and1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with prefix sum t, checkpoint lambda, and tail sum3/2; all trajectory details are included here.\n\nFor the only possible template weights(3),\n\n  U=(u_0+u_1)c-(u_2+u_3)q\n    =(6+11t)/(8lambda),\n  U-1=(3t-2)/(8lambda)>0.\n\nSince e=U-(1-x_3), one has x_3-e=1-U. Equation(2) now yields\n\n  E_h(u)=-[(3sqrt(2)-2)/(8(1+sqrt(2)))]^2<0.\n\nThus no choice of a,b in the endpoint-only template can certify the Huber-tight denominator D for this schedule: the quadratic forces(3), and a second legal equality witness refutes it. The original schedule remains valid with a different weight vector. In the usual ConPD construction, one may use the nonnegative weights\n\n  (sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2)),\n\nwhich have mass D and adjust internal indices as well as endpoints.\n\nThe scope is precise. This rules out modifying only the initial and terminal entries of the physical-step weight vector. It does not exclude all fixed-dimensional potentials, all gradient-aggregate residuals, or another certificate with internal anchor weights. It explains why retaining a single initial-gradient correction while insisting every internal weight equal the physical step is too narrow for the upper-construction program.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"research_path": "research/max4_endpoint_anchor_obstruction.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "1fca3e5dd555cb4a"], "scope": "Only the stated endpoint-only weight template; not all boundary potentials or all fixed-dimensional certificates.", "status": "complete unverified construction; no submission"}, "glossary": {"a,b": "Initial and terminal weight corrections in u=(h_0+a,h_1,...,h_(n-1),b), with b=S+1-a for mass2S+1.", "E_h(u)": "Intrinsic objective energy defined in the evidence, whose nonnegativity suffices for W<=1/sum(u).", "c,q": "Positive and negative gradient caps1/(1+sqrt(2)) and sqrt(2)/(4(1+sqrt(2))) in the displayed exact Huber instance.", "U": "Weighted gradient aggregate sum_i u_i g_i.", "e": "U minus the GD displacement x_0-x_n."}}
{"id": "50b0bb4cd67cac9f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:44:53.572973+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact hostile suffix for the positive periodic witness: two explicit steps, t1=115/54 and t2=max(1,(38/375)/E_N), annihilate its objective at every even prefix length. The first equalizes all even planes; the second uses their explicit minimum-norm convex combination. This does not upper-bound unrestricted W.", "evidence": "# Positive-value periodic Markov relay with signed history\nStatus: complete explicit construction, UNVERIFIED. No new verifier call.\nThis tests the specific repair suggested after the minimum-reset and residual-sign obstructions: allow positive proximal function values, and check actual past-plane deficits even when the signed residual is positive.\n\n## 1. A parameterized model\nFix a in(0,1), chi>=1, T>=2, and kappa in[0,T-2]. Assume\nT>=2a chi,                                               (1)\n(T-2-kappa)-(T-kappa)chi^2+T chi/a>=0.                     (2)\nFor any number m>=1 of blocks, in R^(m+1) set\nzeta_0=e_0,\nzeta_(i+1)=-a zeta_i+sqrt(1-a^2)e_(i+1).\nDefine alpha_i=chi for even i and alpha_i=1 for odd i, omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i. Thus\n<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|\nand all omega_i are independent.\n\nEach real block consists of one unit step followed by\nb_i=T-1+(T/a)alpha_(i+1)/alpha_i.\nTake the ONE finite max-affine function\nJ(z)=max{0,max_(0<=i<=m)(<omega_i,z>-kappa alpha_i^2)}\nand f=env_1 J. Start at X_0=T alpha_0 zeta_0.\nWe prove that block i queries X_i and X_i-omega_i with gradient omega_i at both, and that\nX_(i+1)=X_i-(1+b_i)omega_i.\nThe terminal point is X_m with gradient omega_m.\n\nThe function f is legal globally: J is finite nonnegative convex with J(0)=0, and its envelope is finite convex differentiable1-smooth. For precision, the proximal minimization has a unique minimizer by the quadratic term; its subgradient equation implies grad f(x)=x-prox_J(x), and monotonicity gives\n||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2<=<grad f(x)-grad f(y),x-y>.\nAt any point where plane i is active at x-omega_i, that subgradient equation yields the claimed gradient and value\nf(x)=<omega_i,x>-alpha_i^2/2-kappa alpha_i^2.\nThus it remains to compare every affine plane at the indicated proximal points.\n\n## 2. Exact signed residuals\nLet Z_i=X_i-T omega_i and put\nB_i=(T/a)alpha_(i+1), D_i=B_i(1-a^2).\nThe update gives\nZ_0=0,\nZ_(i+1)=Z_i-B_i(zeta_i+a zeta_(i+1)).\nDirect multiplication of Markov correlations proves\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for j>=i,\n<Z_i,zeta_j>=-S_(j,i) for j<i,\nwhere\nS_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1)D_l(-a)^(l-j).                     (3)\nIndeed zeta_l+a zeta_(l+1) is orthogonal to all zeta_j with j>=l+1, and its inner product with earlier zeta_j is(1-a^2)(-a)^(l-j). This proves(3) by summing the update; no sign of S is assumed.\n\nAt a real query x=X_i-u omega_i with u=0 or1, its proposed proximal point is\nZ_i+d omega_i, d=T-u-1>=T-2.\nThe terminal query has d=T-1 and obeys the same lower bound.\nThe current affine plane has value(d-kappa)alpha_i^2>=0.\nThe current-minus-j-th plane difference is\nd alpha_i(alpha_i-C_ij alpha_j)\n +alpha_j S_(j,i)-kappa alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2          (4)\nfor j<i, with C_ij=(-a)^(i-j); for j>i the same formula has S=0.\nEvery coefficient of d is positive: at even separation the amplitudes agree and C<1, while at odd separation C<0. Hence it suffices to check d=T-2.\n\nFuture planes are already controlled. At even separation their offsets agree and their deficit is d alpha_i^2(1-C)>=0. At odd separation the deficit is\n(d-kappa)alpha_i^2+kappa alpha_j^2+d a^|i-j| alpha_i alpha_j>=0.\nThe substantive point is the full past history.\n\n## 3. All past planes, including positive residuals\nFor even separation i-j=2q, q>=1, let z=alpha_j=alpha_i and y be the opposite amplitude. Formula(3) is the exact geometric sum\nS_(j,i)=(T/a)(y-a z)(1-a^(2q)).\nSubstituting into(4) gives, at d=T-2,\n(1-a^(2q)) z[-2z+(T/a)y]>=0\nby(1), since z/y is either chi or1/chi.\n\nFor odd separation i-j=2q+1, q>=0, let z=alpha_j and y=alpha_i be the opposite amplitude. Now\nS_(j,i)=(T/a)[y-a z+a^(2q)(a z-a^2 y)].\nSubstitution into(4), still at d=T-2, gives\nF_infty(y,z)+a^(2q)[T z^2-2a yz],                       (5)\nwhere\nF_infty(y,z)=(T-2-kappa)y^2-(T-kappa)z^2+(T/a)yz.\nThe second term in(5) is nonnegative by(1).\nAmong the two choices(y,z)=(1,chi),(chi,1), the first has smaller F_infty: the difference is\n2(T-1-kappa)(chi^2-1)>=0.\nIts nonnegativity is precisely(2). This proves every old-plane comparison, at every lag, with the signed residual retained. Together with the future and zero-plane checks it proves the entire trajectory in Section1.\n\nThe exact sampled values are\nf(X_i-u omega_i)=(T-u-1/2-kappa)alpha_i^2.\nAt a checkpoint query u=1, the nonsmooth proximal value is\nJ(X_i-2omega_i)=(T-2-kappa)alpha_i^2.                    (6)\nIt is strictly positive whenever kappa<T-2. Thus these checkpoints are not minimum resets.\n\n## 4. Closest minimizer and payoff\nThe minimizer set of f is exactly\nP_min={z:<omega_i,z><=kappa alpha_i^2 for every i}.\nThe point y_proj=kappa alpha_0 zeta_0 lies in P_min: for even i the amplitude equals alpha_0 and the correlation is at most1; for odd i the correlation is negative. Constraint0 is active, and\nX_0-y_proj=(T-kappa)omega_0\nis a positive multiple of its normal. For any z in P_min this gives\n<X_0-y_proj,z-y_proj><=0,\nso squared-distance expansion proves that y_proj is the unique closest minimizer and\ndist(X_0,P_min)^2=(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2.\nThe normalized terminal value of this one instance is therefore exactly\n2(T-kappa-1/2)alpha_m^2 / [(T-kappa)^2 alpha_0^2].        (7)\nFor even m, alpha_m=alpha_0 and this is\n[2(T-kappa)-1]/(T-kappa)^2.\nAll smooth-convex interpolation inequalities, including the optimum sample, hold because all samples have been checked on this one explicit envelope.\n\n## 5. Rational parameters, quadratic stability, and a strict failure of the old test\nChoose\na=9/10, chi=6/5, T=23/10, kappa=1/10.\nCondition(1) has positive margin\nT-2a chi=7/50.\nThe left side of(2) is exactly37/375>0.\nThe alternating checkpoint sizes are\nb_even=463/135, b_odd=131/30,\nboth in[3,5]. Therefore for m=2r and every integer r>=1 the length4r schedule\n(1,463/135,1,131/30) repeated r times\nhas the explicitly realized normalized error\nW_(4r)(h)>=85/121.                                     (8)\nAt every checkpoint its proximal value is alpha_i^2/5>0.\n\nThis model violates the earlier nonpositive-residual criterion. For any even j with j+2<=m,\nS_(j,j+2)=(T/a)(1-a^2)(1-a chi)<0\nbecause a chi=27/25>1. Thus\n<Z_(j+2),zeta_j> >0,\nand D_j>=a D_(j+1) is false at every such pair. Nevertheless the exact plane comparisons above hold. This is an explicit repair, not an assumption that local correlation checks suffice.\n\nFor comparison, each quadratic block multiplier is(1-lambda)(1-b_i lambda).\nFor0<=lambda<=1/5 it is nonnegative and at most exp(-lambda).\nFor1/5<=lambda<=1, its positive value is at most8/25, since b_i>=3; the magnitude of its negative minimum is at most4/5, since b_i<=5 and max_(b<=5,b>=3)(b-1)^2/(4b)=4/5.\nHence the full finite-dimensional quadratic normalized objective after m blocks is at most\nmax{1/(2 e m),(4/5)^(2m)} ->0.\nThe nonlinear persistent value is not a large quadratic product.\n\nThe omega_i are independent, adjacent ones have negative inner product, and their nonnegative-offset max-affine system has isolated zero/plane hinges. At such hinges the envelope has rank-one Hessian projectors onto omega_i. Adjacent projectors do not commute, proving that this function is nonseparable in every fixed orthonormal basis. Independence permits exposing each hinge by solving for one affine value0 and all others strictly negative. The dimension m+1 grows with the horizon and the gradient energies alternate between36/25 and1.\n\n## 6. Scope\nThis is a parameterized, fully signed-history construction with positive checkpoint values and a concrete rational periodic test outside the earlier residual-sign interface. It does not claim that(1)--(2), or a generalization of them, can realize an arbitrary schedule at the benchmark profile. The endpoint values of the exact all-past comparison now show where positive residuals can be paid for by real face margins and offsets. A future extension must solve those inequalities quantitatively for nonperiodic data, rather than imposing S>=0 again or assuming feasibility at target strength.\n\n\n# Exact two-step annihilator of the rational periodic Markov witness\nStatus: complete hostile test, UNVERIFIED. This concerns the specified witness family only; it is NOT an upper bound on unrestricted W.\n\nUse the explicit rational periodic construction in research/max_positive_value_periodic_relay.md:\na=9/10,chi=6/5,T=23/10,kappa=1/10,\nalpha_even=chi,alpha_odd=1,\nomega_i=alpha_i zeta_i,\n<zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-a)^|i-j|,\nJ=max(0,max_i L_i), L_i(x)=<omega_i,x>-kappa alpha_i^2,\nf=env_1 J,\nand endpoint X_m after the2m-step periodic prefix.\nTake any EVEN m=2r>=2, so there are N=r+1 even normals.\nThe explicit two appended steps are\nt_1=115/54,\nt_2=max{1,(38/375)/E_N},\nwhere\nE_N=(36/25)(1+81/100)/[2+(N-2)(1-81/100)].\nThen the same f has exact terminal value0 after these two GD steps.\n\nHere is the full algebra of the hostile test. The trajectory/legality proof of the prefix is included in the associated global-memory extended candidate; no new claim that those findings are accepted facts is made.\n\n1. The first extra step uses grad f(X_m)=omega_m, hence Y=X_m-t_1 omega_m.\nFor an even index j<m, put c_j=a^(m-j). The signed-history formula yields\nL_j(X_m)=38/375+(46/15)c_j,\n<omega_j,omega_m>=(36/25)c_j.\nThe choice t_1=(46/15)/(36/25)=115/54 therefore gives\nL_j(Y)=A_tail:=38/375\nfor EVERY even j, including j=m since\nL_m(Y)=(T-kappa-t_1)chi^2=38/375.\nFor an odd j<m, the same formula gives\nS_(j,m)=(T/a)[chi-a+a^(m-j-1)(a-a^2 chi)].\nSince <omega_j,omega_m>=-chi a^(m-j), substitution shows that the decaying terms cancel at this same t_1:\nL_j(Y)=-13/15\nfor EVERY odd j.\nThus the first step equalizes all positive even-plane values exactly, while every odd plane has the same negative value.\n\n2. Write rho=a^2=81/100 and let D_N=2+(N-2)(1-rho).\nChoose weights on the even normals:\nlambda_0=lambda_(N-1)=1/D_N,\nlambda_l=(1-rho)/D_N for0<l<N-1.\nThey are positive and sum to1. Set\nw=sum_(l=0)^(N-1)lambda_l omega_(2l).\nBecause the even Gram matrix has entries chi^2 rho^|l-v|, finite geometric summation gives\n<omega_(2l),w>=chi^2(1+rho)/D_N=E_N\nat EVERY even index. Therefore ||w||^2=E_N.\n\nFor each odd index2l+1, its inner products with the entire even span coincide with those of\n-[a/(chi(1+a^2))](omega_(2l)+omega_(2l+2)).\nThis follows by substituting the geometric correlations on each side of l. Consequently\n<omega_(2l+1),w>\n=-[2a/(chi(1+a^2))]E_N=-(150/181)E_N.\n\n3. Put tau=min{1,A_tail/E_N}.\nAt the candidate proximal point Y-tau w, every even affine piece has the common value\nA_tail-tau E_N>=0.\nEvery odd piece has value\n-13/15+tau(150/181)E_N\n<=-13/15+(150/181)A_tail\n=-13/15+76/905<0.\nIf the even value is positive, tau=1 and w is a convex combination of precisely those active normals. If the even value is0, tau w is a convex combination of0 and the active even normals. In both cases\ntau w belongs to the subdifferential of J at Y-tau w.\nThe Moreau optimality equation proves grad f(Y)=tau w.\n\nThe second appended step t_2=A_tail/(tau E_N)=max{1,A_tail/E_N} therefore reaches\nZ=Y-(A_tail/E_N)w.\nAll even planes have value0 at Z, while every odd plane has the negative value -13/15+76/905. Thus J(Z)=0 and f(Z)=0, proving exact annihilation.\n\nFor even m>=32 this coexists with the uniform one-extra-step lower bound in6192a365859c116f: a single appended gradient step cannot remove the remote-plane error, but the first step above reorganizes all even planes to a common height, enabling the second step to remove them together.\n\nThis explicitly refutes an attempted extension from one arbitrary terminal step to arbitrary-length suffixes for THIS fixed rational periodic witness. It does not rule out the nonstationary modulation family366067681df90ad9, changes of parameters, the arc construction, or other legal functions on this padded schedule. The next repair must break the parity-level equalization or use an adversary selected with the entire suffix in view.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_periodic_two_step_annihilator.md", "refutes_extension_of": "6192a365859c116f", "base_candidate": "8cce883b9f93f556", "status": "complete hostile test with full prefix proof included; unverified", "scope": "this fixed rational periodic witness only", "next_direction": "test nonstationary modulation against parity equalization; retain entire suffix"}, "glossary": {"N": "Number m/2+1 of even gradient normals in the rational periodic witness.", "E_N": "Squared norm chi^2(1+a^2)/[2+(N-2)(1-a^2)] of their explicitly weighted convex combination.", "D_N": "Weight normalizer2+(N-2)(1-a^2).", "A_tail": "Common positive even-plane height38/375 after the first appended step.", "tau": "Scale min(1,A_tail/E_N) of the exact gradient after that step."}}
{"id": "84abbf940857fa8d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:49:35.009003+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact two-cut inner return refutes max4's actual eleven-step paired four-copy primitive candidate: the fixed legal scalar function in the evidence gives saturated intrinsic primitive energy -508/225. This blocks its primitive-seed amplification; it does not refute a different direct objective certificate.", "evidence": "# Two-cut inner returns and an exact rejection of the paired four-copy seed\n\nStatus: complete explicit candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No additional\nverification allocation is assumed. This refutes a proposed primitive schedule;\nit is not a negative or positive resolution of the unrestricted minimax target.\n\n## 1. Exact legal inner-return construction\n\nPartition any nonnegative schedule chronologically into\n\n    (block_0, b_0, block_1, b_1, block_2),\n\nwith ordinary block sums s_0,s_1,s_2. Choose numbers 0<w<=1 and R>=w.\nPut\n\n    r_outer=R+1-w,\n    A=b_0-r_outer=b_0-1-R+w,\n    v=A/(1+s_1),\n    z=(b_1-1)v.\n\nRequire A>0 and\n\n    (1+s_2)w <= z <= R.                                    (1)\n\nThe following SINGLE derivative is fixed before running the schedule:\n\n    g(x)=-v                     for x<=-v,\n    g(x)=x                      for -v<=x<=w,\n    g(x)=w                      for w<=x<=R,\n    g(x)=x-R+w                  for R<=x<=r_outer,\n    g(x)=1                      for x>=r_outer.             (2)\n\nIt is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz: the successive slopes are\n0,1,0,1,0 and endpoint values agree. Define f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.\nThen f is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and minimized at zero with f(0)=0.\nExplicitly,\n\n    f(x)=-v x-v^2/2                            for x<=-v,\n    f(x)=x^2/2                                 for -v<=x<=w,\n    f(x)=w x-w^2/2                             for w<=x<=R,\n    f(x)=(x-R+w)^2/2+wR-w^2                    for R<=x<=r_outer,\n    f(x)=x-r_outer+1/2+wR-w^2                   for x>=r_outer.\n\nStart at x_0=s_0+r_outer. The entire first block has gradient1 and reaches\nr_outer. The first checkpoint reaches -A. The middle block has gradient -v:\nevery partial sum is at most s_1, so it stays in [-A,-v] and ends at -v.\nThe second checkpoint reaches z. By (1), the final block stays on the inner\npositive plateau [w,R], with gradient w, and ends at\n\n    x_n=z-s_2 w >= w.\n\nAll individual steps, including zero steps, obey the supplied GD recursion.\nThe exact normalized terminal objective is\n\n    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2\n       = [2wz-(2s_2+1)w^2]/(s_0+R+1-w)^2.                 (3)\n\nThis differs from the verified outward-ray C6 construction: the second return\ncan have gradient w strictly smaller than the old positive gradient1. The old\npositive plateau lies farther out. The connecting ramp and its area cannot be\ndiscarded in a primitive-energy calculation.\n\n## 2. Exact primitive energy, retaining the remote area\n\nLet s=s_0+b_0+s_1+b_1+s_2. The saturated intrinsic primitive energy is\n\n    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g_n^2.          (4)\n\nA primitive schedule must make (4) nonnegative for every legal smooth convex\ntrajectory. Set Q_i=f_i-x_i g_i+g_i^2/2. Expanding\nx_(i+1)^2=x_i^2-2h_i x_i g_i+h_i^2g_i^2 and summing gives the exact identity\n\n    V_h=x_0^2-x_n^2-2s f_n-s(s+1)g_n^2+2sum_(i<n)h_i Q_i.\n\nFor all first-block samples and the first checkpoint query, (2) gives\n\n    Q_i=-(1-w)(R-w).\n\nFor every remaining sample, including the terminal sample, Q_i=0, since its\nray is a constant extension of the unit quadratic core. Also g_n=w and\nf_n=w x_n-w^2/2. Therefore\n\n    V_h=(s_0+R+1-w)^2\n          -[z+(s_0+b_0+s_1+b_1)w]^2\n          -2(s_0+b_0)(1-w)(R-w).                          (5)\n\nThe last term is the contribution of the old positive area. It is negative\nand is required by the exact function values. Formula (5), with constraints\n(1), is a new necessary scalar test for any claimed primitive schedule.\nIt is not a sufficient certificate.\n\n## 3. The actual eleven-step candidate is not primitive\n\nThe candidate in finding0bdb4dfb180b15a0 is\n\n    h=(3/2,3/2,19/5,3/2,3/2,5,3/2,3/2,9/5,3/2,3/2).\n\nIts sum is s=113/5 and shifted sum is118/5. Choose the two checkpoints19/5\nand5, so s_0=s_1=3 and s_2=39/5. Set\n\n    w=1/6,       R=89/60,       r_outer=139/60,\n    v=89/240,    z=R,          x_0=319/60.\n\nHere A=89/60=4v and (1+s_2)w=22/15=88/60<R, so all feasibility conditions\nhold. The complete exact GD data are:\n\n| i | x_i | g_i | f_i |\n|---|---|---|---|\n|0|319/60|1|1339/360|\n|1|229/60|1|799/360|\n|2|139/60|1|259/360|\n|3|-89/60|-89/240|55447/115200|\n|4|-89/96|-89/240|7921/28800|\n|5|-89/240|-89/240|7921/115200|\n|6|89/60|1/6|7/30|\n|7|37/30|1/6|23/120|\n|8|59/60|1/6|3/20|\n|9|41/60|1/6|1/10|\n|10|13/30|1/6|7/120|\n|11|11/60|1/6|1/60|\n\nEvery row follows from (2) and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. In particular the first\ntwo ordinary pairs use the outer positive and negative plateaus, while the\nlast five steps use the lower positive plateau. Direct substitution in (4),\nor the shorter identity (5), gives\n\n    V_h=(319/60)^2-(79/20)^2\n          -2(34/5)(5/6)(79/60)\n        = -508/225 < 0.                                    (6)\n\nThe literal primitive property of this eleven-step schedule is false. The\ncounterexample is to the actual paired seed, not merely to the generic join\nrule with an empty seed. The shifted-sum advantage therefore cannot be used\nin the conditional primitive-seed amplification argument.\n\nThe objective error of this particular instance is only\n2f_11/x_0^2=120/319^2. Thus (6) must not be presented as a strong lower bound on\nits worst-case objective or as a disproof of some different direct objective\ncertificate. It specifically blocks using this schedule as a primitive seed.\n\n## 4. A compact symbolic slice explaining the failure\n\nKeep s_0=s_1=3, b_0=19/5, b_1=5, s_2=39/5 and impose z=R. Then\n\n    R=7/5+w/2,       0<w<=14/83.\n\nFormula (5) becomes\n\n    V_h=204/25-(112/5)w-(6016/25)w^2.\n\nThe rational choice w=1/6 lies in this interval and gives (6). This is an\nanalytic family, not a numerical sign inferred near a floating-point zero.\nThe finite rational calculation of all twelve rows was independently run as\nan arithmetic sanity check; no SDP, sweep, or verifier call was used.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_inner_return_primitive_test.md", "refutes_finding": "0bdb4dfb180b15a0", "status": "complete exact candidate, unverified; no new verifier call", "mechanism": "lower positive plateau on an inner return, with the old outer area retained", "scope": "actual eleven-step primitive seed, not a full objective upper-bound rejection", "fact_ids": []}, "glossary": {"w": "Lower positive plateau gradient in the new inner-return construction.", "R": "Upper radius of the lower positive plateau; local to this construction.", "r_outer": "Radius R+1-w where the upper positive plateau of gradient1 begins.", "v": "Negative plateau gradient magnitude (b_0-1-R+w)/(1+s_1).", "z": "Positive landing radius (b_1-1)v after the second checkpoint.", "V_h": "Saturated intrinsic primitive energy defined in the evidence.", "Q_i": "The origin interpolation residual f_i-x_i g_i+g_i^2/2 used in the exact energy identity."}}
{"id": "cfe9f969be95cc9a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:51:55.942657+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Complete tight-certificate obstruction to dropping the remote-edge condition: at h=(2,9/4,3), q=25/4, nu_1=9/2 and lambda_02=5, the unchanged two-step transport preserves every multiplier sign and flow but makes the full matrix indefinite for every 0<eta<344/81. Its matching legal quadratic alone misses the negative kernel direction.", "evidence": "# Complete-certificate obstruction to the unchanged remote-edge extension\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample. This refutes one specified extension\nof the suffix transport, not the existence of some descent and not Huber\ntightness at global minimizers. It directly addresses TASK.md's unrestricted\ntwo-step-suffix test.\n\n## 1. A complete tight certificate\n\nTake n=3, L=1, h=(2,9/4,3), q=25/4, and zeta=sqrt(78)/5.\nUse Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, with\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0. All unspecified multipliers below are zero:\n\nmu_0=lambda_(0,star)=25/2+5sqrt(78)/8,\nmu_1=lambda_(1,star)=5sqrt(78)/8,\nnu_0=lambda_(star,0)=5sqrt(78)/4+1,\nnu_1=lambda_(star,1)=9/2,\nnu_2=lambda_(star,2)=5,\n\nlambda_10=25/2-5sqrt(78)/8,\nlambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,\nlambda_02=5, lambda_20=4.                            (1)\n\nAll are nonnegative; lambda_10>0 because sqrt(78)<9.\nThe finite flow equations can be checked explicitly:\nat0, out=in=35/2+5sqrt(78)/8;\nat1, out=in=25/2;\nat2, out=in=12;\nat3, out-in=2.\n\nFor independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3), define\n\nR0=x_0-g_0,\nU=g_0+g_1,\nV=g_2+(5/4)g_1,\nZ=g_3+2g_2.\n\nThe FULL matrix has the exact sum-of-squares representation\n\nM=q||x_0||^2-2f_3+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n =(25/4)||R0-(sqrt(78)/20)U||^2\n   +(1/32)||U-16V||^2+||Z||^2.                     (2)\n\nHere GD substitution and the displayed flows cancel every f coefficient.\nThus this is a full PSD certificate, with no omitted prefix or remote entry.\n\nA short derivation of (2) also checks every coefficient. At the first step2,\nthe multiplier set\nmu_0=2+zeta/2, mu_1=zeta/2, nu_0=zeta,\nlambda_10=2-zeta/2\ngives the identity\n||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda Q\n =||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2.\nMultiply it by25/4. For a step of length1+r, r>=1, direct expansion gives\n\n2r^2 f_i-2f_(i+1)+2Q_(i+1,i)+2(r^2-1)Q_(star,i)\n =||r g_i+g_(i+1)||^2.\n\nAdd four times this identity with i=1,r=5/4 and once with i=2,r=2.\nThis gives a base certificate with the same q and the same multipliers\nas (1), except lambda_02=lambda_20=0, nu_0=5sqrt(78)/4,\nand nu_2=6. Its slack is\n\nM_base=(25/4)[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]\n        +4||V||^2+||Z||^2.                         (3)\n\nFinally add the exact zero-flow multiplier change\n5Q_02+4Q_20+Q_(star,0)-Q_(star,2).\nIts quadratic form is\n-5||U||^2-<U,V>+4||V||^2.\nCombining with (3), and using\n(25/4)(1-zeta^2/16)=161/32, gives (2).\n\nThe certificate is TIGHT on the globally legal one-dimensional function\nf(x)=x^2/2, with x_0=1. Its GD data are\n\n(x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),\n(g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),\n(f_0,f_1,f_2,f_3)=(1/2,1/2,25/32,25/8).\n\nEvery Q_ij, including star pairs, is zero on these data, (2) vanishes,\nand 2f_3=q. Thus W_3(h)=25/4. This is a complete legal matching\nprimal, not merely a PSD principal block.\n\nThe selected star multiplier is nu_(n-2)=nu_1=9/2>0.\nThere is a genuine remote prefix-to-suffix edge lambda_02=5.\n\n## 2. The precise extension being refuted\n\nApply the previously valid c_2=0 two-step transport to k=1 while\nleaving the remote edge unchanged. In this certificate,\n\nc_1=mu_2+lambda_20+lambda_21=12,\nc_2=mu_3+lambda_30+lambda_31=0,\nc_3=lambda_32=2.\n\nFor any 0<eta<=9/2, the proposed extension changes\n\nh'_1=(27+eta)/(12+eta),    h'_0=2, h'_2=3,\nlambda'_21=8+eta,\nnu'_1=9/2-eta, nu'_2=5+eta,                          (4)\n\nand leaves every other multiplier and q unchanged. All steps and\nmultipliers remain nonnegative, and every flow equation remains exact.\nThus the only issue is the full PSD condition.\n\nDirect expansion gives\n\nM_new-M\n =eta||g_2||^2\n   +[25 eta/(4(12+eta))]<g_0,g_1>.                  (5)\n\nThe second term is the previously absent remote cut contribution\n(h_1-h'_1)lambda_02<g_0,g_1>. It cannot be discarded.\n\n## 3. Exact negative direction in the OLD full kernel\n\nUse one-dimensional formal vectors with\n\ng_0=47/40,  g_1=-7/40,  g_2=9/32,  g_3=-9/16,\nx_0=47/40+sqrt(78)/20.                              (6)\n\nThese need not be a legal new primal: they are a legitimate test of\nPSD, which is required on ALL formal vectors.\n\nHere U=1, V=1/16, Z=0, and R0=sqrt(78)/20.\nConsequently the OLD FULL slack (2) is exactly zero.\nOn (6), equation(5) is exactly\n\nM_new=eta(81 eta-344)/[1024(12+eta)].                (7)\n\nIt is negative for every 0<eta<344/81. In particular eta=1 is allowed\nby (4), all actual multipliers remain nonnegative, but\nM_new=-263/13312<0. There is therefore no sufficiently small positive\nmove of this unchanged transport that preserves PSD.\n\nOn the actual matching quadratic data from Section1, in contrast,\n(5)/eta is 25/16-25/[4(12+eta)]>0. Thus checking only the\nmatching primal would falsely suggest a gain. The second full-kernel\ndirection is essential.\n\n## 4. Scope and a different successful action on this example\n\nThis is NOT a stationary tuple and NOT a globally optimal schedule.\nIt does not refute some other simultaneous descent. Indeed one exists\nwith a different certificate re-selection.\n\nFirst continuously undo the last zero-flow adjustment in Section1,\nmoving from (1) to its base certificate (3) at the SAME h,q.\nEvery intermediate actual multiplier is nonnegative, and every\nintermediate slack is a convex combination of the PSD matrices (2)\nand (3). The same legal quadratic remains matching throughout.\nThe base slack (3) has no extra kernel beyond that quadratic direction,\nbecause zeta^2/16=39/200<1.\n\nNow use the schedule h'=(2,28/13,3), corresponding to eta=1 in (4).\nAn explicit full certificate with value q'=900/169<25/4 is obtained\nby keeping the first-step multiplier pattern\n(mu_0,mu_1,nu_0,lambda_10)\n=(2+zeta/2,zeta/2,zeta,2-zeta/2),\nmultiplying it by q'=4(15/13)^2, and then adding the two\nfunction-growth identities from Section1:\nfour times the i=1 identity with r=15/13, and once the i=2\nidentity with r=2.\n\nIts slack is\n\nq'[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]\n +4||(15/13)g_1+g_2||^2+||2g_2+g_3||^2 >=0,\n\nall multiplier signs hold since15/13>1 and zeta<4, and its\nfunction-value flow is exactly2e_3. The quadratic again attains q'.\nThis is a real fixed-length improvement for this example.\n\nThe obstruction therefore distinguishes two mechanisms:\nthe same sparse transport can fail at an unnecessarily degenerate\noptimal dual certificate, whereas reselecting the dual certificate\ninside the fixed-h optimum face can make a different descent available.\nNo theorem is claimed that such re-selection removes remote edges\nfor every schedule, nor that the maximal-rank dual face always has\nthe needed kernel structure.\n\nNext action: analyze certificate re-selection together with the\ninactive-interpolation costs before attempting a universal descent.\nDo not add a remote-edge-absence hypothesis and call the original\nextension repaired. The eventual exact-profile mass-cap issue remains\nseparate even if global Huber tightness were established.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_remote_kernel_counterexample.md", "responds_to": "84871816ccd203db", "target_claim": "The c_2=0 suffix transport from cd577f86c5896600 remains PSD when arbitrary remote prefix-to-suffix edges are left unchanged.", "status": "refuted for that explicit transport only", "related_findings": ["cd577f86c5896600", "8a77ec65ac2eeb52", "8c998d29efd3da99"], "assumptions_satisfied": ["Complete tight full certificate", "All original and changed multipliers nonnegative", "All flow equations exact", "Explicit globally legal matching quadratic", "Positive nu_(n-2) with remote forward edge"], "scope": "Not stationary; not a counterexample to global Huber tightness or to all possible descents.", "next": "A different exact descent succeeds after re-selecting the fixed-h dual certificate; investigate which face/kernel obstructions survive such re-selection.", "verification_allocation": "unchanged"}, "glossary": {"zeta": "sqrt(78)/5, the coefficient parameter in the first-step certificate.", "R0": "Formal residual x_0-g_0.", "U": "Formal gradient sum g_0+g_1 in this counterexample.", "V": "Formal residual g_2+(5/4)g_1 in this counterexample.", "Z": "Formal residual g_3+2g_2 in this counterexample.", "eta": "Positive transport amount in the precise failed update (4)."}}
{"id": "2b63ea480303fd7c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T11:55:10.649306+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Stronger repair boundary for max4: the four-copy rule with bridge factors7/10,1,1/5 fails on EVERY finite nonnegative seed, regardless of its primitive certificate or quadratic product. Use the globally fixed derivative g=-v on x<=-v, g=x on [-v,w], g=w on [w,R], g=x-R+w on [R,r_outer], and g=1 beyond r_outer. Start x0=A+R-w. The first seed remains on gradient1 and reachesr_outer. Bridge1+7A/10 lands at-R. The next seed has gradient-v and ends at-v. Bridge1+A returns toR.
