Integratingg gives the function values, convexity and1-smoothness. The exact identity V=x0²-(x_n+s w)²-2(s0+b0)(1-w)(R-w), derived by telescoping squared iterates and retaining Q_i=f_i-x_i g_i+g_i²/2, applies. General c=7/10 gives V/A²=1-c²-2cw-[(3+c)²+2(2+c)/A]w². Atw7/44 the coefficients are exactly -2293/38720 and -1323/(9680A). This proves the stated uniform negative value. The test only refutes this fixed bridge rule, not every four-copy rule or a different direct objective certificate.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_inner_return_primitive_test.md", "extends_finding": "84abbf940857fa8d", "refutes_finding": "0bdb4dfb180b15a0", "status": "complete explicit candidate, unverified; no verifier call", "scope": "any seed in one specific four-copy rule"}, "glossary": {"A": "Shifted sum 1+sum(a_seed) of the arbitrary input seed in this universal refutation.", "a_seed": "Any finite nonnegative schedule, including the empty seed.", "J": "The four-copy concatenation with bridges1+(7/10)A,1+A,1+(1/5)A."}}
{"id": "f2899f24e4d5962d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:07:44.125088+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The exact C10 two-cut inner-return objective channel also misses the ZJ profile on H_m, uniformly over every feasible checkpoint pair and both continuous parameters: its payoff is <=2/[eta^2 a c^(m-1)], c=13/5,a=c^-17,eta=13/8. Its primitive-energy counterexample remains useful, but this objective channel is not the needed quantitative repair.", "evidence": "# Quantitative limit of the exact two-cut inner-return objective witness\n\nStatus: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verifier\ncall. This bounds the explicit two-cut construction, not the entire scalar\nclass and not the unrestricted smooth-convex worst case.\n\n## 1. The construction being bounded\n\nTake a schedule split as (prefix,b,middle,d,tail), with nonnegative ordinary\nblock sums P,M,T and b,d>1. Choose 0<w<=1 and R>=w. The fixed derivative is\n\n    g(x)=-v                 when x<=-v,\n    g(x)=x                  when -v<=x<=w,\n    g(x)=w                  when w<=x<=R,\n    g(x)=x-R+w              when R<=x<=R+1-w,\n    g(x)=1                  when x>=R+1-w,\n\nwhere v=(b-1-R+w)/(1+M)>0. Start at X=P+R+1-w, and put z=(d-1)v.\nRequire (1+T)w<=z<=R. As in the complete construction proof, every ordinary\nblock stays on its indicated plateau, the checkpoints cross the minimum,\nand the normalized terminal objective is\n\n    J=[2wz-(2T+1)w^2]/X^2.                                  (1)\n\nAll inequalities below use just these displayed definitions. In particular\nno unverified result is imported as a proof premise. An overall scaling of\nthe gradient/radii is immaterial to (1).\n\nSince X>=P+R, z<=R, and completing the square gives\n2wz-(2T+1)w^2<=z^2/(2T+1), we have\n\n    J <= [z/(P+R)]^2/(2T+1) <= 1/(2T+1).                    (2)\n\nAlso w<=1 and R>=w imply b-1-R+w<=b, so z<=(d-1)b/(1+M).\nThe strict crossing and z<=R moreover give R<b and z<b: the latter follows\nfrom z<=min(R,(d-1)(b-R)/(1+M)), where w<=1 was used. Consequently\n\n    J <=2z/X^2 <=2b/P^2                                    (3)\n\nwhen P>0. Combining the first inequality in (2) with the preceding bound on z\ngives\n\n    J <= [b(d-1)/(P(1+M))]^2/(2T+1)                        (4)\n\nwhen P>0. These are distinct valid relaxations; the w<=1 restriction in (3)\nis essential near the end of the schedule.\n\n## 2. Uniform bound on the recursive hostile family\n\nPut c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and\n\n    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),\n    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).\n\nFor m>=19 let B=b_(m-1) be the central, largest step. Write eta=13/8.\nSince (2/c)^17<1/40, direct substitution gives s_u>=eta b_u for all u>=17.\nAny eligible selected step greater than1 has level u>=18. An occurrence of\nb_u has a complete H_u immediately before and immediately after it, so its\nentire prefix and tail sums are at least eta b_u.\n\nConsider ANY feasible pair of selected checkpoints in H_m and ANY feasible\nw,R in Section1. There are three exhaustive cases.\n\n1. The first selected checkpoint is at or after the central B-step. Then\nP>=s_(m-1)>=eta B and b<=B. Equation (3) gives\n\n    J <=2/(eta^2 B).\n\n2. The second selected checkpoint is at or before the central B-step. Then\nT>=s_(m-1)>=eta B. Equation (2) gives\n\n    J <=1/(1+2eta B) <=1/(2eta B).\n\n3. The first selected checkpoint is strictly before the central step and the\nsecond is strictly after it. The middle sum contains the central step, hence\nM>=B. Also P>=eta b, T>=eta d, and d<=B/c. Equation (4) yields\n\n    J <=d^2/[eta^2 B^2(1+2eta d)]\n       <=d/(2eta^3 B^2)\n       <=1/(2eta^3 c B).\n\nEach bound is at most2/(eta^2 B). Thus, uniformly over every feasible pair,\nevery threshold, and every choice of ordinary blocks within H_m,\n\n    sup J <=2/(eta^2 a c^(m-1))\n           =[2c/(eta^2 a)] c^(-m).                        (5)\n\nThis covers the arbitrary lower-positive-plateau height and the retained old\npositive ramp/area in the exact C10 object. It does not cover more than two\ncheckpoints, visits inside nonconstant ordinary blocks, or arbitrary core\ncurvatures; no such generalization is asserted.\n\n## 3. The exact benchmark comparison, with an elementary bound included\n\nLet C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 for the exact project recursions.\nTheir shifted joins are\n\n    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nWe prove the sufficient rough bounds C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3) and\nB_n<=1+4n^(4/3) directly here. For positive A,D put\nZ=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3). The inequality Z>=Omega(A,D) is equivalent,\nafter normalizing A^(3/4)+D^(3/4), to\n\n    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1,  0<=t<=1.\n\nTo prove it set x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. The difference\nbetween the right and left sides equals\n\n    x^3 y^3 [1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy].\n\nEach reciprocal is at least1/3, and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3, so the bracket is\nnonnegative. This proves the join inequality and, by induction in the exact\nprimitive recursion, C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).\n\nNext G(A,D)<=D+4A follows from sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A. In the dominant recursion\neach left subtree has k+1 leaves for k steps and the right subtree has n-k-1\nsteps. Induction, together with u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3), yields\nB_n<=1+4n^(4/3). The empty-horizon initial values are C_0=B_0=1.\n\nAt n=2^m-1, (5) and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n imply\n\n    (sup J)/U_(2^m-1)^ZJ\n       <=[2c/(eta^2 a)] c^(-m) [1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,\n\nbecause c^3=2197/125>16. Therefore the exact two-cut inner-return objective\nchannel also cannot repair the recursive-family profile gap.\n\nThis does not undo its primitive-energy counterexample: the latter includes\nweighted old-area residuals absent from the last-iterate objective. Nor does\nit imply that H_m has a small unrestricted worst case; the coupled-coordinate\nconstruction already shared provides a genuine divergent lower bound there.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max3_inner_return_strength.md", "construction": "research/max3_inner_return_primitive_test.md", "status": "complete scoped candidate proof, unverified; no verifier call", "scope": "two selected crossings with constant-gradient ordinary blocks; no arbitrary-core or full scalar claim", "fact_ids": []}, "glossary": {"J": "Normalized objective payoff of the specified two-cut inner-return construction.", "P": "Prefix step sum before the first selected checkpoint.", "M": "Middle step sum between the two selected checkpoints, local to this proof.", "T": "Tail step sum after the second selected checkpoint.", "B": "Central largest step a c^(m-1) of H_m, local to this proof.", "eta": "Explicit comparison constant13/8 with s_u>=eta b_u at eligible scales."}}
{"id": "7e5ba92b7db8211a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:24:40.950030+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Four explicit equality instances for the tight benchmark h=(sqrt2,1+sqrt2,3/2) force every PSD quadratic remainder in its intrinsic objective energy to be identically zero, for ANY nonnegative proof weights of mass D=6+4sqrt2. The new fourth instance is a capped quadratic with an inner return. Its gradient row completes a basis of all four sampled gradients, so no positive coercive memory term in their linear aggregates can survive exact calibration here.", "evidence": "# Four equality instances forbid positive intrinsic quadratic storage at a benchmark schedule\n\nStatus: unverified exact counterexample argument. No new solver or verifier call. This is a limitation of positive quadratic remainders in the intrinsic objective energy, not of all certificates or of the unrestricted minimax problem.\n\nPut t=sqrt(2), lambda=1+t, and consider the known three-step schedule\n\n    h=(t,lambda,3/2), S=5/2+2t, D=2S+1=6+4t=2lambda^2.\n\nFor nonnegative proof weights u=(u_0,u_1,u_2,u_3) with sum D, define\n\n    d=x_0-x_3, U=sum_i u_i g_i, e=U-d,\n    E_u=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2\n          +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_0-x_i>-||U||^2.\n\nUse Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n\n## Claim\n\nIf a symmetric PSD matrix P of size4 satisfies\n\n    E_u >= sum_(i,j=0)^3 P_ij <g_i,g_j>               (1)\n\nfor every legal smooth convex trajectory of this schedule, then P=0.\n\nThis applies to any positive quadratic storage formed from fixed linear combinations of the four gradients: substituting those combinations into the storage matrix gives a PSD matrix P in (1). A nonzero matrix on redundant coordinates is possible only if its quadratic form vanishes identically on the GD coordinate subspace.\n\n## 1. Why equality instances constrain every choice of proof weights\n\nFor every minimizer x_star, expansion gives\n\n    ||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)\n       -2D(f_3-f_star)=||x_3-x_star-e||^2+E_u.      (2)\n\nEach one-dimensional instance below has x_star=0, f_star=0, x_0=1, Q_(i,star)=0 at every query, and 2f_3=1/D. Thus (2) implies\n\n    E_u=-|x_3-e|^2<=0.\n\nUnder (1), PSD gives E_u>=0, so E_u=0 and the gradient vector belongs to the kernel of P. This argument does not assume the standard proof-weight vector or any completeness theorem for interpolation certificates.\n\n## 2. Four explicit legal equality instances\n\nEvery derivative displayed below is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Its integral from zero is therefore differentiable, convex, 1-smooth and minimized at zero. The formulas also directly show f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2 at the sampled points, giving Q_(i,star)=0.\n\n### A. Global Huber plateau\n\nLet c=1/D and set g(x)=max(-c,min(x,c)). All four sampled points remain on the positive affine piece, so their gradients are(c,c,c,c). The endpoint is x_3=1-Sc=(S+1)/D, and 2f_3=1/D. After division by the nonzero initial gradient, the gradient row is\n\n    v_H=(1,1,1,1).\n\n### B. Full quadratic\n\nTake f(x)=x^2/2. The gradient row, with g_0=1, is\n\n    v_Q=(1,1-t,2-t,-1+t/2).\n\nThe last entry is -(2-t)/2=-1/(2+t), and (2+t)^2=D, so 2f_3=1/D.\n\n### C. Middle-cut asymmetric Huber\n\nLet c=1/lambda and a=t/(4lambda). Set g(x)=max(-a,min(x,c)). Its exact points are\n\n    x_0=1, x_1=c, x_2=-4a, x_3=-(5/2)a,\n\nand its gradients are(c,c,-a,-a). Hence f_3=2a^2 and 2f_3=4a^2=1/D. Dividing the gradients by c gives\n\n    v_M=(1,1,-t/4,-t/4).\n\n### D. Capped quadratic with an inner return\n\nAgain let c=1/lambda, but use the different fixed derivative\n\n    g(x)=x for x<=c, and g(x)=c for x>=c.\n\nThe exact points are\n\n    x_0=1, x_1=c, x_2=-tc, x_3=(t/2)c.\n\nThe first step stays on the upper affine piece; the bridge enters the quadratic core, and the last step returns to its positive side. All points from x_1 onward are in the quadratic piece. Thus f_3=t^2 c^2/8=c^2/4 and 2f_3=1/(2lambda^2)=1/D. Dividing the gradients by c gives\n\n    v_C=(1,1,-t,t/2).\n\nThis fourth equality case is essential. Using only the previous three rows would incorrectly leave room for a square proportional to\n\n    ||g_2-g_3-(3/2)(t-1)(g_0-g_1)||^2.\n\nFor the capped quadratic, g_0=g_1 but g_2-g_3=-(3t/2)c is nonzero, whereas E_u=0 under any certificate (1).\n\n## 3. The kernel spans the entire gradient space\n\nForm the4-by4 matrix whose rows are v_H,v_Q,v_M,v_C. Subtract its first row from the other three. Expansion down the first column gives determinant\n\n    (-t)(-1-t/4)(3t/2)=3(1+t/4)>0.                  (3)\n\nThe four rows therefore span R^4. For a PSD matrix, v^T P v=0 implies P v=0, as follows by diagonalizing P with nonnegative eigenvalues. Part1 puts all four independent rows in its kernel, proving P=0.\n\n## 4. The obstruction occurs at an actual valid tight certificate\n\nThe usual weights are\n\n    u=(t,2+t,2+t,2+t), sum u_i=D.\n\nTheir intrinsic energy has the explicit identity\n\n    E_u=2lambda(-Q_10)+2(-Q_01)+D(-Q_32)\n          +2lambda(-Q_21)+2lambda(-Q_31).            (4)\n\nAll coefficients are positive, so E_u>=0 and (2) yields W<=1/D. The four instances above attain this bound. Equation(4) can be checked directly from the GD differences x_1=x_0-tg_0, x_2=x_1-lambda g_1 and x_3=x_2-(3/2)g_2. This is also the primitive-one-step/dominant-one-step ConPD certificate, but no external closure theorem is needed for the displayed identity.\n\n## Implication for retained-state propagation\n\nThe finite pair residual in max4_finite_pair_residual_transport.md is valid: that two-step objective bound leaves room for a positive quadratic remainder. Such a remainder cannot be carried as a nonzero PSD intrinsic-energy term through every exactly calibrated dominant schedule. At this three-step benchmark it must disappear, or become a structural zero, regardless of which nonnegative tight proof weights are selected.\n\nThe result does not exclude an indefinite storage coupled to function values, a state retaining actual interpolation slacks, a certificate at a smaller denominator, a different schedule, or a complete objective certificate in which the initial-distance square is still present. It rules out the specific plan of obtaining exact benchmark calibration and retaining a uniformly positive, nontrivial quadratic penalty depending on gradient-history linear aggregates alone. The complete original minimax target remains unresolved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max4_joint_objective_dual_storage", "research_path": "research/max4_tight_benchmark_storage_obstruction.md", "target_claim": "A nonzero positive quadratic intrinsic-energy storage can persist at exact benchmark mass through all dominant schedules.", "status": "refuted", "related_findings": ["3c02ccd1fb137184", "b750ce63e2821fa8", "12ea2963a6e53e67"], "verification_status": "unverified exact construction; no new solver or verifier call", "impact": "Retain function-value/interpolation slack information or allow another form of storage; mere enlargement of the linear gradient-history PSD penalty does not solve this calibration obstruction."}, "glossary": {"v_H,v_Q,v_M,v_C": "The four explicitly displayed normalized gradient rows of the global Huber, full quadratic, middle-cut asymmetric Huber, and capped-quadratic equality instances.", "P": "A symmetric PSD coefficient matrix of a proposed intrinsic-energy remainder in the four sampled gradients."}}
{"id": "81a9c03cf73670f3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:40:49.216750+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact face-level counterexample candidate at a GLOBAL minimizer: h=(sqrt2,(3+sqrt(9+8sqrt2))/4) has a UNIQUE full optimal certificate, with unavoidable lambda_01=2W_2^*>0. Three explicit legal equality functions force the entire matrix; their explicit direct-sum mixture is a full stationary primal. Thus even strict complementarity and optimal re-selection cannot remove ALL forward edges.", "evidence": "# A globally minimizing stationary schedule with an unavoidable forward edge\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample package. No additional verifier call\nis allocated. The two-step optimum is used here as a fully certified calibration\nexample for a FACE-LEVEL support claim, not as a solution to the asymptotic target.\n\n## 1. The precise property being refuted\n\nThe proposed property is:\n\n    At a globally minimizing positive schedule, one can reselect a tight\n    full interpolation certificate with lambda_(i,j)=0 for every\n    finite chronological forward pair i<j.\n\nThis property is false. The example below is BOTH globally minimizing\namong all nonnegative two-step schedules AND a full matching stationary\nprimal-dual tuple. Its ENTIRE optimal certificate set is a singleton,\nand its multiplier lambda_01 is strictly positive.\n\nThe forced edge0->1 is ADJACENT. This does NOT refute the narrower\nunproved property that only nonadjacent remote forward edges can be removed.\n\n## 2. Exact constants and a complete full certificate\n\nDefine\n\n    u=sqrt(2), a=u-1,\n    b=(-1+sqrt(9+8u))/4,\n    r=a b, q=r^2,\n    h=(u,1+b).                                      (1)\n\nThe useful exact identities are\n\n    a^2+2a=1,  u=1+a,  1/a=a+2,\n    2b^2+b=a+2,\n    r(2b+1)=1,\n    q=1/[1+2(u+1+b)].                               (2)\n\nFor example, r(2b+1)=a(2b^2+b)=a(a+2)=1, and\n1+2(u+1+b)=2a+2b+5=(2b+1)^2.\nAlso 2/5<a<1/2, 3/4<b<1, 0<r<1/2.\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Use the complete inequalities\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n\nSet all unlisted multipliers to zero and take\n\n    mu_0=lambda_(0,star)=2u r^2,\n    mu_1=lambda_(1,star)=2u b r,\n    mu_2=lambda_(2,star)=2r,\n\n    lambda_01=2r^2,\n    lambda_10=2r^2/a,\n    lambda_21=2(1-r).                               (3)\n\nAll are positive. There are no star-outgoing multipliers.\nThe finite flow equations are\n\n    mu_0+lambda_01=lambda_10,\n    mu_1+lambda_10=lambda_01+lambda_21,\n    mu_2+lambda_21=2.\n\nThey follow from (2); for example 2r^2/a+r=1 and\nu r+u b+1=2b+1=1/r.\n\nAfter x_1=x_0-u g_0 and x_2=x_1-(1+b)g_1, the FULL quadratic identity is\n\n    q||x_0||^2-2f_2+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n         =||r x_0-u r g_0-u b g_1-g_2||^2.          (4)\n\nOne way to check all coefficients is to write its gradient block as\n\n    A=(u r,u b,1)^T(u r,u b,1),\n\nwhose diagonals are (2r^2,2b^2,1) and off-diagonal entries are\nA_01=2rb, A_02=u r, A_12=u b.\nIts x_0 cross coefficients are minus the three mu_i from (3).\nThus every multiplier sign, flow, and full PSD entry is explicit.\n\nThis proves W_2(h)<=q.\n\n## 3. Three exact matching legal functions\n\nEach scalar function starts at x_0=1 and is minimized at zero.\n\nH: the even Huber function of threshold q, with derivative\nmax(-q,min(q,x)). Its vector (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2) is\n\n    v_H=(1,q,q,q),\n\nand its final value is f_2=q/2. At every sampled point its\nstar-outgoing slack is strictly positive:\n\n    f_i-||g_i||^2/2=q[1-q(P_i+1)]>0,               (5)\n\nwhere P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, including P_2=u+1+b.\nThe strict inequality follows from P_i<=P_2 and\nq=1/(2P_2+1), with P_2>0.\n\nQ: the quadratic function x^2/2. Its transcript vector is\n\n    v_Q=(1,1,-a,r),\n\nand f_2=r^2/2=q/2.\n\nC: the one-sided capped quadratic\n\n    f_C(x)=x^2/2 for x<=a,\n           a x-a^2/2 for x>=a.\n\nIts derivative min(x,a) is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz.\nThe trajectory is x_0=1, x_1=a, x_2=-r, with\n\n    v_C=(1,a,a,-r),  f_2=q/2.                       (6)\n\nAll are actual globally legal smooth-convex functions. They establish\nW_2(h)>=q and hence equality with the full certificate (4).\n\n## 4. Global optimality among ALL two-step schedules\n\nThe only verified predecessor used here is fact b98bb807d514e80d:\nits exact Huber and both single-cut lower bounds apply to arbitrary\nnonnegative schedules. The elementary quadratic witness adds its own\nexplicit lower bound. Thus for every X,Y>=0,\n\n    W_2(X,Y)>=max{\n       1/(1+2X+2Y),\n       (X-1)_+^2/(1+2Y),\n       (Y-1)_+^2/(1+X)^2,\n       (X-1)^2(Y-1)^2\n    }.                                             (7)\n\nThe last term is the error on x^2/2; the other three are the verified\nsingle-cut/Huber bounds. Suppose W_2(X,Y)<=q=r^2. The Huber term gives\n\n    X+Y>=u+1+b=2+a+b>3.                            (8)\n\nIf X<=1, then Y>1 and the third term in (7) gives\nY-1<=r(1+X), whence X+Y<=2+2r<3, contradicting (8).\nIf Y<=1, then X>1 and the second term gives\nX-1<=r sqrt(1+2Y)<=r sqrt(3), whence X+Y<3, again impossible.\n\nWe may therefore set U=X-1>0 and V=Y-1>0. Equation (7) implies\n\n    U V<=r,\n    V<=r(U+2),\n    U^2<=r^2(2V+3).                                (9)\n\nFor U<=a, use the second inequality:\n\n    U+V<=U+r(U+2)<=a+r(a+2)=a+b,\n\nwith equality only at U=a,V=b.\nFor U>=a, the first and third inequalities imply\n\n    U^3-3r^2 U-2r^3<=0,\n    (U-2r)(U+r)^2<=0,\n\nso a<=U<=2r. Also V<=r/U. The strictly convex function\nU+r/U attains its maximum on [a,2r] at an endpoint. Its values are\n\n    a+r/a=a+b,\n    2r+1/2.\n\nThe first is strictly greater, since\n\n    (a+b)-(2r+1/2)=a^2(b-1/2)>0.\n\nThus U+V<=a+b, again with equality only at U=a,V=b.\nCombining with (8), every schedule with W_2(X,Y)<=q is exactly\n(X,Y)=(u,1+b). In particular (1) is the UNIQUE global minimizer, and\n\n    W_2^*=q.                                       (10)\n\nAll nonnegative schedules, including zero steps and arbitrary large\nsteps, were included. This is an exact two-step argument, not an\nasymptotic profile claim.\n\n## 5. The ENTIRE optimal certificate set is a singleton\n\nTake ANY full nonnegative-flow PSD certificate at this fixed h,q.\nEvery actual instance in Section3 attains q. Therefore PSD and the\ninterpolation signs force the certificate matrix M to annihilate\nv_H,v_Q,v_C, and every multiplier to vanish on a strict interpolation\nslack of any one of these instances.\n\nThe three transcript vectors are independent. Indeed, v_H-v_C has\nequal, nonzero g_0 and g_1 coordinates, whereas v_Q-v_C has those\ncoordinates 1-a and -2a, which are unequal. Both differences have\nx_0 coordinate zero, while v_C has x_0 coordinate one. The nonzero\nclaim uses q<a, which follows from a b^2=(1-r)/2<1/2.\n\nTheir common orthogonal complement is generated by\n\n    N=(r,-u r,-u b,-1).\n\nIt annihilates v_Q and v_C by (2), and N dot v_H=0 follows from\nu r+u b+1=1/r. Since M is PSD and its x_0,x_0 coefficient is q=r^2,\n\n    M=N N^T.                                       (11)\n\nThis is the WHOLE matrix for every optimal certificate. No assumption\nabout a numerical maximal rank or a chosen observed kernel is used;\nthree explicit legal equality instances force it.\n\nEquation (5) forces every star-outgoing multiplier nu_i to be zero.\nThe x_0 row of (11) fixes mu uniquely at the values in (3).\nThe final gradient diagonal satisfies\n\n    A_22=out_2-1=1+in_2\n\nby the full flow equation. In (11), A_22=1, so every incoming\nterminal multiplier is zero: lambda_02=lambda_12=nu_2=0.\n\nFor function C a direct substitution gives\n\n    Q_20=(a-1)(a+r)<0.\n\nTherefore lambda_20=0 for every optimal certificate as well.\nThe first gradient diagonal now reads\n\n    A_00=mu_0-(u-1)lambda_01\n         =2u r^2-a lambda_01.\n\nIn (11) it equals 2r^2, hence\n\n    lambda_01=2r^2=2q>0.                            (12)\n\nFlow then forces lambda_10=2r^2/a and lambda_21=2(1-r), leaving\nexactly (3). All directed pairs have now been accounted for.\n\nThus the optimal certificate set is a singleton, and re-selection,\nrelative-interior selection, and maximal-rank selection all leave\nthe positive adjacent forward edge unchanged.\n\n## 6. An ACTUAL matching stationary primal, with no assumed regularity\n\nTo check stationarity directly, set\n\n    omega_H=1/(1+a^2 b^3),\n    omega_C=omega_H*a^2 b^2*(b-a)/(1+a^2),\n    omega_Q=omega_H*a^3 b^2*(1+a b)/(1+a^2).          (13)\n\nThey are positive and sum to one:\nomega_C+omega_Q=omega_H a^2 b^3.\nConstruct the actual three-dimensional function\n\n    F(z_H,z_Q,z_C)\n       =sum_(J in {H,Q,C}) omega_J f_J(z_J/sqrt(omega_J))\n\nand start at (sqrt(omega_H),sqrt(omega_Q),sqrt(omega_C)).\nIt is convex and 1-smooth, the initial squared distance is one, and\nits full trajectory Gram matrix is\nG=sum_J omega_J v_J v_J^T. Its final normalized value is q.\n\nFor the certificate (3), the two formal matrix derivatives evaluated\non any scalar/vector realization are\n\n    D_0=2b^2<g_0,g_1>+2r<g_0,g_2>-2r^2||g_0||^2,\n    D_1=2<g_1,g_2>.                                (14)\n\nTheir values on the three equality instances are\n\n              H              Q                C\n    D_0       2r^4           -2a b^2           2a r^2\n    D_1       2r^4           -2a r             -2a r.\n\nThese values are obtained from (14), not by differentiating a\nnonsmooth family of extremal functions. In particular the fixed\ncapped function C has a gradient kink at x_1=a, so that shortcut\nwould be unsafe.\n\nThe identities\n\n    omega_Q-a^2 omega_C=omega_H a^3 b^2,\n    omega_Q+omega_C=omega_H a^2 b^3\n\nmake both weighted derivatives zero. Therefore\n\n    <G,partial M/partial h_0>=<G,partial M/partial h_1>=0.\n\nAll primal interpolation inequalities, PSD, normalization, full\nmatrix complementarity, multiplier complementarity, and both\nstepsize stationarity equations hold for this actual instance.\nMoreover rank(G)=3, rank(M)=1, and the formal matrix size is4.\nThis example even has strict complementarity; a hidden nonphysical\ncommon kernel is not responsible for the obstruction.\n\n## 7. Consequences and precise limits\n\nThis is a complete counterexample to removing ALL forward finite\nmultipliers by re-selection, even at a positive globally minimizing\nschedule with a full stationary matching instance and no extra\ncommon kernel. It closes a possible attempt to turn the triangular\nsufficient hypothesis in the earlier suffix-deformation candidate\ninto a universal property of optimal schedules.\n\nIt does not refute removal of ONLY nonadjacent remote forward\nedges: lambda_02=0 in the example. It also has q equal to the\nHuber value, every nu_i=0, and Z=0; consequently it is not a\ncounterexample to global Huber tightness. The asymptotic exact-profile\nquestion and the mass-cap bridge remain unresolved.\n\nNo literature result beyond the verified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d\nwas used as a proof brick. The targeted Matlas query returned no\napplicable statement. The global two-step calculation is included\nsolely to make the counterexample's global and stationary status\nreviewable without relying on a numerical optimum.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max2_global_forward_face.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["792aa4b6dbbad8bf", "56f99268b45fbce8", "8c998d29efd3da99"], "scope": "Adjacent forward edge only; does NOT refute elimination of only nonadjacent remote forward edges."}, "glossary": {"u": "sqrt(2), the first step of the exact two-step example.", "a": "u-1, satisfying a²+2a=1.", "b": "(-1+sqrt(9+8u))/4, the final step minus one.", "r": "a*b=1/(2b+1), the square root of the exact objective level.", "q": "r², the exact two-step worst-case value in this example.", "v_H": "Formal transcript vector for the even Huber equality function.", "v_Q": "Formal transcript vector for the quadratic equality function.", "v_C": "Formal transcript vector for the one-sided capped quadratic equality function.", "omega_H": "Positive direct-sum weight of the Huber equality function.", "omega_Q": "Positive direct-sum weight of the quadratic equality function.", "omega_C": "Positive direct-sum weight of the capped equality function."}}
{"id": "6348179e97b86202", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T12:42:24.802976+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Precise qualification of the three-profile sufficiency test: Phi/Psi/Dist and the boundary center/unit direction alone do not encode the terminal gradient magnitude. Two explicit positive-height legal histories have identical profiles but different next GD points. Retain G=||g|| (equivalently the actual boundary query), as already included in97e8798c6b1e9dfc; with G the exact updates stand.", "evidence": "Let J(x)=max(0,1+x,1+2x), f=env_1 J, and x_init=2. At x in[1,2], prox_J(x)=0 because x belongs to partial J(0)=[1,2]; hence grad f(x)=x and J(prox_J(x))=1. Prefix h=(1/2) ends at x=1 with G=1; prefix h=(1/4) ends at x=3/2 with G=3/2. Both histories have p=0,u=-1 and all proximal samples equal0 with height1. Hence both Phi(t)=max(0,1-t,1-2t), Psi(s)=1. Their common zero set is(-infinity,-1], so Dist(t)=(2+t)^2 for t>=1 and infinity otherwise. Both have the same x_init. Yet the identical next bridge b=3 sends them to(1-b)G=-2 and-3 respectively. Thus no rule based only on those identical profiles and p,u can recover the bridge displacement. This does not contradict the state update in97e8798c6b1e9dfc, whose input explicitly retains G and sets ell=(b-1)G. Full artifact research/max_three_profile_state.md now includes this concrete omitted-scalar test. All claims remain unverified research evidence; no verifier call.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "research/max_three_profile_state.md", "qualifies": "97e8798c6b1e9dfc", "responds_to_guidance": "38008a0bdc8c6797", "status": "unverified; boundary scalar explicitly retained"}, "glossary": {"G": "Actual magnitude of the terminal gradient, needed to compute the prescribed next GD step.", "Phi,Psi,Dist": "The outgoing-plane, old-query-acceptance and zero-slice-distance profiles from the state test."}}
{"id": "5157db3087a188bc", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:16:14.392316+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact closure obstructions: a legal three-dimensional one-step prefix needs all THREE terminal anchors even after optimizing the final step, with value9/154 and a two-anchor error1/8162; independently, the exact unrestricted W is not jointly convex in the whole schedule.", "evidence": "# Exact tests of two terminal-game closures\n\nStatus: self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate counterexamples. No verifier request\nis allocated. Only fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is used as a mathematical predecessor;\nthe new minimax candidate 40601f4c6f496ab8 is not a premise.\n\n## 1. Three anchors are necessary even after optimizing the final step\n\nThe claim tested here is that, for every legal fixed prefix, its exact optimized\nterminal potential can be evaluated with a mixture supported on at most TWO\nprefix anchors, counting the optimum anchor. This claim is false already for\none queried GD step in dimension three. The example keeps both orientations of\nevery queried-prefix interpolation constraint.\n\nWork with L=1 in R^3. Use the optimum jet (0,0,0), the positive first step\nh_0=2, and the following prefix:\n\n    x_0=(8,9,3),  g_0=(0,2,1),  f_0=19/2,\n    x_1=(8,5,1),  g_1=(1,0,0),  f_1=11/2.\n\nThus x_1=x_0-2g_0 and ||x_0||^2=154. The proximal points and offsets are\n\n    y_0=x_0-g_0=(8,7,2),    delta_0=f_0-||g_0||^2/2=7,\n    y_1=x_1-g_1=(7,5,1),    delta_1=f_1-||g_1||^2/2=5.\n\nUsing the signed Q from the predecessor, ALL six prefix residuals are\n\n    Q_(0,star)=-9,   Q_(star,0)=-7,\n    Q_(1,star)=-2,   Q_(star,1)=-5,\n    Q_(0,1)=-3,      Q_(1,0)=-1.                         (1)\n\nFor example the identity Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>\ngives Q_(0,1)=2-<(0,2,1),(1,2,1)>=-3. Hence the predecessor constructs\nthe pointwise maximal legal prefix interpolant as the conjugate of\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+\n             max{0, <(8,7,2),v>-7, <(7,5,1),v>-5}.       (2)\n\nCall it F. It is one fixed everywhere-finite convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction, with minimum F(0)=0, and it interpolates exactly the jets in (1).\nFor any nonnegative final step b, its terminal point is\n\n    x_2(b)=(8-b,5,1).\n\nPut v_*=(0,1,0). All three affine planes inside the maximum in (2) are\nzero at v_*. Thus H(v_*)=1/2, and the conjugate definition gives\n\n    F(x_2(b)) >= <x_2(b),v_*>-H(v_*)=9/2\n                         for EVERY b>=0.                   (3)\n\nAt b=3, let alpha_star=alpha_0=alpha_1=1/3. The weighted affine bound on\nH and completion of a square give\n\n    F(x_2(3))\n      <= (7+5)/3 + (1/2)||x_2(3)-(y_0+y_1)/3||^2\n       =4+(1/2)||(0,1,0)||^2=9/2.                         (4)\n\nConsequently the exact final-line-search value of this legal fixed prefix is\n\n    min_(b>=0) 2F(x_2(b))/||x_0||^2 = 9/154.               (5)\n\nThe minimizing step and mixture are UNIQUE, and all three weights are\nstrictly positive. Here are two direct ways to check the asserted necessity.\n\nFirst, equality in (3) makes v_* the unique conjugate maximizer. The\nsubgradient condition for (2) then requires\n\n    x_2(b)-v_* = alpha_0 y_0+alpha_1 y_1,\n    alpha_0,alpha_1>=0,  alpha_0+alpha_1<=1.               (6)\n\nThe subgradient condition follows by separating from the convex hull of\nthe active plane slopes: a point outside that hull gives a direction in\nwhich <x,v>-H(v) increases. Thus it uses only the finite maximum in (2).\nThe third and second coordinates of (6) read\n\n    2alpha_0+alpha_1=1,\n    7alpha_0+5alpha_1=4.\n\nTheir unique solution is alpha_0=alpha_1=1/3. The first coordinate then\ngives b=3. The remaining weight on the optimum is 1/3 as well.\n\nSecond, the full quadratic mixture expression can be checked directly:\n\n    J(b,alpha_0,alpha_1)\n      =2(7alpha_0+5alpha_1)\n        +(8-b-8alpha_0-7alpha_1)^2\n        +(5-7alpha_0-5alpha_1)^2\n        +(1-2alpha_0-alpha_1)^2.                          (7)\n\nIt is the upper bound on 2F supplied by the weighted conjugate quadratics.\nAt (b,alpha_0,alpha_1)=(3,1/3,1/3), its value is 9 and all three derivatives\nare zero. Its Hessian is positive definite because the three vectors\ng_1,y_0,y_1 are independent; their determinant has absolute value three.\nThus the minimizer of (7) on b>=0 and the simplex is unique. Equations\n(3)-(4) show that this bound is exact at the minimizing point, independently\nof any general hull or minimax theorem.\n\n## 2. Exact loss from using at most two anchors\n\nThe failure has an explicit quantitative size. For every feasible mixture,\nthe best b in (7) is\n\n    b=8-8alpha_0-7alpha_1>=0.\n\nAfter optimizing it, set\n\n    Y=(7alpha_0+5alpha_1, 2alpha_0+alpha_1).\n\nThe remaining expression is precisely\n\n    9+||Y-(4,1)||^2.                                    (8)\n\nThe simplex maps bijectively to the triangle with vertices\nO=(0,0), A=(7,2), B=(5,1), and the unconstrained target (4,1) has the\nthree barycentric weights 1/3. A support of size at most two places Y on\nthe boundary of this triangle. Its distances to the three edges are:\n\n* To [O,A], squared distance 1/53. The projection coefficient is 30/53,\n  lying strictly between zero and one; the determinant is 4*2-1*7=1.\n* To [O,B], squared distance 1/26. The projection coefficient is 21/26;\n  the determinant is 4*1-1*5=-1.\n* To [B,A], the projection coefficient from B is -2/5, so the nearest\n  point of that segment is B and the squared distance is one.\n\nTherefore optimizing BOTH the last step and all mixtures of support at\nmost two gives J=9+1/53, strictly larger than its exact value 9. In the\noriginal normalized objective this is\n\n    9/154 + 1/8162,                                     (9)\n\nwhereas the true value is 9/154. No count of active anchors in this\nexample is inferred from a numerical solution.\n\nThis refutes a universal two-anchor formula for the fixed-prefix terminal\ngame even when the final step is optimized. It does NOT assert that this\nparticular prefix maximizes the full PEP at h=(2,3), and it does NOT refute\nthe distinct, unproved statement that some globally worst prefix always\nadmits a smaller terminal support. The full selected certificate of an\nentire PEP is also a different object from the fixed-prefix alpha here.\n\n## 3. The whole schedule value is not jointly convex\n\nFor EVERY nonnegative schedule all of whose steps satisfy h_t>=2,\n\n    W_n(h)=product_t(h_t-1)^2.                            (10)\n\nHere is a complete elementary proof. Normalize L=1 and the minimizer to\nzero. Adding the two interpolation inequalities between x and zero gives\n<x,g>>=||g||^2, and smoothness gives ||g||<=||x||. Thus for h>=2,\n\n    ||x-hg||^2\n      =||x||^2-2h<x,g>+h^2||g||^2\n      <=||x||^2+h(h-2)||g||^2\n      <=(h-1)^2||x||^2.\n\nIterate this inequality and use 2f(x_n)<=||x_n||^2. This proves the upper\nbound in (10) in every finite dimension. The scalar function f(x)=x^2/2\nstarting at x_0=1 has exactly that normalized error, proving equality.\n\nIn particular\n\n    W_2(2,4)=W_2(4,2)=9,\n    W_2(3,3)=16 > (9+9)/2.                              (11)\n\nThus the exact unrestricted value is not jointly convex in all step\ncoordinates, even on strictly positive schedules. This is a value-level\nobstruction, stronger than exhibiting a nonconvex response on one fixed\nfunction. It prevents replacing the proven final-step convexity by an\nunqualified joint-convexity argument for all earlier steps. It does not\nby itself disprove every possible all-step minimax identity.\n\n## 4. Scope for continued proof search\n\nThe three-anchor obstruction is inside the full smooth-convex prefix class\nand uses a finite, explicit legal function. It is not caused by dropping\nincoming queried-prefix inequalities; every such inequality is strictly\nsatisfied in (1). It shows that any terminal state compression must retain\nenough geometry to represent this triangle after projection along the\nlast gradient. One scalar value and an assumed pair of active anchors\ncannot reproduce the fixed-prefix game.\n\nThe current global two-step calibration in finding 81a9c03cf73670f3 remains\nseparate and unverified: its unavoidable adjacent forward multiplier rules\nout universal triangular certificate re-selection. Nothing in the present\nexample contradicts it or removes that edge. No new claim about remote\nforward edges, global Huber tightness, or the asymptotic exact profile is made.\n\nTargeted memory/fact searches found no existing version of (1)-(9). The\nMatlas query returned unrelated general convexity statements; no external\nresult is used. This is the already audited finite conjugate-quadratic\nregime of the accepted terminal fact, so no new literature layer is needed\nto construct or evaluate these explicit examples.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/terminal_game_obstructions.md", "target_claim": "Every fixed-prefix optimized terminal mixture has support at most two, counting the optimum; final-step convexity extends jointly to all steps.", "status": "refuted by explicit candidate calculations; unverified", "assumptions_satisfied": ["All six ordered prefix constraints strictly satisfied", "One fixed global convex1-smooth function", "Positive step h0=2 and final-step optimization over all b>=0", "Original normalization with ||x0||²=154"], "failed_conclusion": "The unique optimal pair is b=3 and alpha_star=alpha0=alpha1=1/3; exact W2(3,3)=16 exceeds the average of W2(2,4)=W2(4,2)=9.", "scope": "Support obstruction is fixed-prefix, not an assertion about globally worst prefixes or globally optimal certificate support.", "branch_id": "max2_terminal_game_closure"}, "glossary": {"H_tri": "The maximum-conjugate function ||v||²/2+max(0,<(8,7,2),v>-7,<(7,5,1),v>-5).", "J_mix": "The explicit quadratic terminal upper bound (7), minimized over final step and simplex weights.", "Y_proj": "Projected anchor barycenter (7alpha0+5alpha1,2alpha0+alpha1) in the triangle conv{(0,0),(7,2),(5,1)}."}}
{"id": "6658165fc85a3bd8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:23:55.889808+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C17 refutes the local plateau-rank update after removing flatness, using a globally legal asymmetric mixed-return episode on the exact blocks (H_r,b,H_r,b c^2,H_r,b): endpoint positive gradients increase 1->z>1 across levels(0,2), and the traversed positive intervals overlap with identical affine derivative data. Full areas and exact product/payoff formulas are supplied. The same seed cannot be embedded after the preceding(0,1) pair of H_m under the proposed crossing pattern, because its required input exceeds the exact incoming radius cap.", "evidence": "# An exact overlapping-return episode and its embedding obstruction\n\nStatus: unverified counterexample to a proposed LOCAL extension of the\nplateau-rank argument. This is not a benchmark-scale construction for H_m.\nThe last section identifies an explicit obstruction to inserting this same\nepisode after the preceding low-level pair in H_m.\n\n## 1. Fixed legal function and exact trajectory\n\nSet c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, H_0=(), and\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j). Choose r>=22, and let\n\n    b=b_r,  S=sum H_r=a(c^r-2^r)/(c-2),\n    lambda=1/(3b),\n    P_aff=product_{h in H_r}(1-lambda h),\n    v=(b-1)/(S+1).\n\nEquivalently, with lambda held fixed throughout, define P_0=1 and\nP_(j+1)=P_j^2(1-lambda b_j); then P_aff=P_r. This is an exact analytic\nproduct recurrence, not a numerical trajectory calculation.\n\nHere b>=c^5=118.81376>100, every factor defining P_aff lies in (0,1), and\nS/b is between eta=13/8 and 5/3. Define the positive and negative derivative\nmagnitudes of one fixed function by\n\n    g_+(R)=R                      for 0<=R<=1,\n    g_+(R)=1+lambda(R-1)          for R>=1,\n    g_-(R)=min(R,v)              for R>=0.\n\nThe derivative is g_+(x) on the positive ray and -g_-(-x) on the negative\nray. It is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz; across zero use\ng_+(R)+g_-(T)<=R+T. Its integral f is convex and 1-smooth, with minimum\nf(0)=0. All complete function-value areas are\n\n    f(R)=R^2/2                                      for 0<=R<=1,\n    f(R)=1/2+(R-1)+lambda(R-1)^2/2                    for R>=1,\n    f(-R)=R^2/2                                     for 0<=R<=v,\n    f(-R)=vR-v^2/2                                  for R>=v.  (1)\n\nStart at\n\n    X=1+3b(P_aff^(-1)-1).\n\nConsider the exact consecutive blocks and checkpoints\n\n    K_r=(H_r,b,H_r,b c^2,H_r,b).                           (2)\n\nOn the affine positive piece, a step h multiplies the gradient by\n1-lambda h. Thus the first H_r goes from X to 1. All its intermediate\nradii are at least 1 because its initial gradient is P_aff^(-1) and all partial\nproducts are at least P_aff. The low checkpoint b then lands at -(b-1).\nThe next H_r has constant gradient -v and goes from -(S+1)v to -v.\nThe checkpoint b c^2 lands at\n\n    Y=(b c^2-1)v.\n\nDefine\n\n    z=P_aff[1+(Y-1)/(3b)],\n    Z=1+3b(z-1).                                         (3)\n\nThe estimates below prove 1<z<3/2-1/(2b). Consequently the third H_r\nstays in the same affine positive piece and sends Y to Z. The final\ncheckpoint b strictly changes sign, landing at\n\n    -B,    B=bz-Z=b(3-2z)-1>0.                           (4)\n\nThis is one actual function and one initial point for the entire episode.\nNo gradient or knot is selected again at an intermediate time.\n\n## 2. Exact bounds proving overlap and the increased endpoint gradient\n\nPut alpha_h=lambda h for the steps of H_r. Then 0<=alpha_h<1 and\nsum alpha_h=lambda S<=5/9. Induction in the finite product gives\n\n    P_aff=product(1-alpha_h)>=1-sum alpha_h>=4/9.              (5)\n\nAlso log(1-t)<=-t for 0<=t<1 gives\nP_aff<=exp(-lambda S)<=exp(-13/24)<3/5. The last strict inequality follows\nalready from exp(13/24)>=1+13/24+(13/24)^2/2>5/3.\n\nSince b>100,\n\n    Y/b >= [(27/4)(99/100)]/(503/300)\n          =8019/2012 >39/10.\n\nHere b c^2-1>=(27/4)b, b-1>=(99/100)b, and\nS+1<=(503/300)b were used. Therefore\n\n    z>=(4/9)[1+(39/10-1/100)/3]=689/675>1.                (6)\n\nOn the other hand Y/b<c^2/eta=1352/325, hence\n\n    z<(3/5)[1+c^2/(3eta)]=2327/1625.\n\nSince 2327/1625<299/200 and 3/2-1/(2b)>299/200,\nthe sign-change condition in (4) follows. In particular the positive\nendpoint gradient has INCREASED from 1 to z>1 across checkpoint levels\n(0,2), even though both positive blocks and the intervening negative block\nstay on their assigned rays.\n\nThe two positive traversed intervals overlap strictly:\n\n    1<Z<X<Y.                                             (7)\n\nTo prove Z<X, compare z with P_aff^(-1). Using P_aff<3/5 gives\n\n    P_aff z=P_aff^2[1+(Y-1)/(3b)]\n         <(9/25)[1+c^2/(3eta)]<1.\n\nFor the final strict inequality, the bracket is 2327/975, and\n(9/25)(2327/975)=20943/24375<1. The inequality X<Y follows from z>1\nand the strictly increasing affine H_r map, which sends X to 1 and Y to Z.\nThus the old interval [1,X] and the return interval [Z,Y] have the genuine\noverlap [Z,X]. Their gradients are not constant; they are restrictions of\nthe very same affine derivative, so all overlap data agree automatically.\n\nAt the positive return endpoint the exact normalized value is\n\n    2f(Z)/X^2\n      = [1+3b(z^2-1)]/[1+3b(P_aff^(-1)-1)]^2.                (8)\n\nAt the end of (2), use (4) in the negative-ray formula (1): the normalized\nvalue is B^2/X^2 if B<=v, and (2vB-v^2)/X^2 if B>=v. These are complete\nareas, not squared-gradient surrogates. The episode is finite and these\nformulas do not establish a comparison to the exact benchmark on H_m.\n\n## 3. Precisely what local assertion fails\n\nWith a flat positive H_r block of derivative u, its traversed interval has\nlength S u. Canonical intervals belonging to different derivative heights\nare disjoint, forcing multiplicative separation of their outer radii.\nFor varying gradients the natural interval of length S u at the endpoint\nneed not be a constant-gradient interval and need not be disjoint from\nthe corresponding interval of another endpoint value.\n\nThe local claim that the endpoint-gradient rank cannot increase across a\npair of checkpoint levels (0,2), without the flat-block hypothesis, is\ntherefore false. The legal episode above has endpoint values 1<z and a\nrank increase in the finite set {1,z}. Its explicit overlapping interval\n(7) identifies the missing hypothesis. This does not refute a possible\nglobal all-scalar bound for H_m: the ordering of neighboring checkpoint\npairs may impose additional restrictions not present in a single episode.\n\n## 4. This exact seed cannot be inserted after the preceding (0,1) pair\n\nThe checkpoint substring (0,2,0) occurs in the ruler word for H_(r+3),\nbut its first positive block is preceded by a pair (0,1). Merely matching\nthe local substring is not an embedding proof.\n\nFor this fixed g_+, the largest negative landing radius at ANY positive\nlow checkpoint b is b-1. Indeed b g_+(R)-R=(b-1)R on [0,1], while on\n[1,infinity) its slope is b lambda-1=-2/3. Its maximum is at R=1.\n\nSuppose the following negative H_r stays negative, as required by the\ncoarse relay under test. Starting at B_0<=b-1=(S+1)v, it ends at a radius\nat most v. If it ever reaches radius at most v, this follows from its\nassumed same-sign motion. If it always stays above v, its constant\ngradient v makes its terminal radius B_0-Sv<=v, with the same conclusion.\nIts terminal gradient magnitude is thus at most v.\n\nThe next checkpoint of level 1 can therefore land at positive radius at\nmost\n\n    (b c-1)v < (c/eta)b=(8/5)b.\n\nBut the required initial radius of the episode is\n\n    X=1+3b(P_aff^(-1)-1)>1+2b,\n\nbecause P_aff<3/5. Hence no such preceding pair (0,1), with its negative\nordinary block staying on that ray, can supply X to this episode.\n\nThe concrete failure is an interface mismatch in initial radius, not\nillegal local interpolation or a missing function-value area. A recursive\nconstruction would need to modify the inner radial geometry, allow a new\ninternal crossing pattern, or build a different incoming state. The\ndisplayed fixed function and local episode alone do not solve that task.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"artifact": "workers/max3/research/c17_overlapping_return_episode.md", "finding_ids": ["cc1650c148b7676e"], "status": "unverified exact local counterexample and embedding obstruction; no fact_submit", "target_claim": "The flat-block rank increment <=ell-2 extends locally to varying positive gradients", "counterexample_status": "refuted", "impact": "Canonical disjointness fails for overlapping varying-gradient intervals; full H_m question remains open", "next_gap": "Repair incoming-radius mismatch with genuinely new inner geometry or different crossing pattern"}, "glossary": {"P_aff": "Exact multiplier product_{h in H_r}(1-h/(3b)) for the positive affine derivative piece; equivalently P_r under P_0=1, P_(j+1)=P_j^2(1-lambda b_j).", "K_r": "Local legal episode schedule (H_r,b,H_r,b c^2,H_r,b), not the whole recursive word H_m.", "X": "Initial positive radius 1+3b(P_aff^(-1)-1), taking H_r exactly to radius 1.", "Y": "Positive landing (b c^2-1)(b-1)/(S+1) after the higher checkpoint.", "Z": "Positive return endpoint 1+3b(z-1), with 1<Z<X<Y.", "z": "Positive gradient at Z, equal to P_aff[1+(Y-1)/(3b)] and strictly greater than one.", "B": "Negative landing radius b(3-2z)-1 after the second low checkpoint."}}
{"id": "fd5ec341651817dd", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:25:11.521683+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Prefix length AND total sum do not determine even the optimized final-stage worst case: for k>=1 and S=2k, rho_last(2,...,2)<=1/4 but rho_last(S,0,...,0)>=S^(2/3)/12, giving an unbounded separation between equal-length/equal-sum prefixes.", "evidence": "## 5. Even length and total sum together do not determine the final-stage value\n\nThis is a separate, quantitative obstruction to a sum-only state. For a fixed\nprefix h_pre of length k define directly\n\n    rho_last(h_pre)=inf_(b>=0) W_(k+1)(h_pre,b).\n\nFor every integer k>=1 let S=2k, and compare the two legal prefixes\n\n    h_even=(2,...,2),             k entries,\n    h_burst=(S,0,...,0),          k entries.\n\nThey have the SAME length and the SAME total sum. Nevertheless\n\n    rho_last(h_even)<=1/4,\n    rho_last(h_burst)>=S^(2/3)/12.                       (12)\n\nIn particular their values are different for k>=3 and have an unbounded\nseparation as k increases. This refutes an exact value closure depending\nonly on (k,S), not merely a claim about one selected function.\n\nFor the first upper bound, the norm calculation in Section3 with h=2 gives\n||x_k-x_star||<=||x_0-x_star|| for every legal function. Append b=3/2.\nAt that final stage write x=x_k-x_star, z=grad f(x_k)/L and w for the next\nnormalized gradient, and use values divided by L and shifted by f_star.\nThe normalized one-step identity, with the signed Q on samples 0,1,star,\nis\n\n    (1/4)||x||^2-2f_1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)\n                      =||(1/2)x-z-w||^2.\n\nExpanding Q and the recursion x_1=x-(3/2)z verifies every term. Since all\nQ<=0, it bounds the last objective by (1/4)||x||^2 in the W normalization.\nThe initial norm bound proves rho_last(h_even)<=1/4 in all dimensions.\n\nFor the second lower bound use the verified single-cut fact b98bb807d514e80d,\nwhose full statement and proof have been read. Its cut at the first step S\nand its cut at the appended final step b yield, for EVERY b>=0,\n\n    W_(k+1)(h_burst,b)\n       >=max{(S-1)^2/(1+2b), (b-1)_+^2/(S+1)^2}.        (13)\n\nThe internal zero steps change neither prefix nor suffix sums in that fact.\nIf b<=S^(4/3), then S>=2 and the first term in (13) is at least\n\n    (S^2/4)/(1+2S^(4/3)) >= S^(2/3)/12.\n\nIf b>S^(4/3), then b>2, b-1>=b/2 and S+1<=3S/2. The second term is\ntherefore at least\n\n    (b^2/4)/[(3S/2)^2] > S^(2/3)/9.\n\nTaking the infimum over b proves the second part of (12). No compactness\nor attainment assertion for this infimum is needed for either bound.\n\nThe explicit prefix-sum guard in research/prefix_sum_terminal_guard.md\naccordingly supplies a universal LOWER boundary condition q_S, not an exact\nschedule-level state variable. The proof of (12) does not use that new,\nunverified guard or the unverified minimax result.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/terminal_game_obstructions.md#section-5", "target_claim": "The exact optimal final-step value is a function only of prefix length and prefix sum.", "status": "refuted by explicit analytic bounds; unverified candidate", "scope": "Nonnegative schedules including legal zero steps. Uses true W bounds in all finite dimensions, not just one response curve.", "proof_dependency_note": "Uses verified single-cut fact plus explicit norm and one-step square; does not depend on unverified terminal minimax or guard."}, "glossary": {"h_even": "Length-k prefix with all entries2.", "h_burst": "Length-k prefix (2k,0,...,0).", "rho_last": "inf_(b>=0)W_(k+1)(h_pre,b), with the complete earlier schedule fixed."}}
{"id": "d5bc3c4799afdc51", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:52:06.599721+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Concrete omitted-normalization data: identical Phi,Psi,zero-slice distance, initial point, all prefix jets, and boundary gradient magnitude do not determine the closest-minimizer distance after a legal join raises the common minimum. On(t,y), take J_A=max(0,1-t,-y) and J_B=max(J_A,3(1-t-y)/4), with f_T=env_1 J_T. Both have zero set{t>=1,y>=0}. The extra B plane has values6 and3/4 at those proximal points, so all stated gradients are active. Add a fresh w coordinate and the SAME two planes C=11/16+t/2, E=1+t/4-w/2. The same bridge3/2 reaches query(1/2,0,0), with proposed proximal point(1/4,0,1/2), gradient(1/4,0,-1/2), and height13/16. There C=E=13/16 and the old functions equal3/4. Thus identical old state and new cell data give different actual normalization. Both retain positive final proximal gap13/16-19/24=1/48. {"id": "dd3ca4162bb552a3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:53:40.862770+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Candidate exact two-dimensional counterexample refutes the outstanding eight-step primitive seed29a1f4a5269032b1. More generally ANY primitive two-bridge join (a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c), without quadratic-balance assumptions, must satisfy r_L r_R[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)]<=A^2 BC. At a saturated first cut r_L T_tot=A D_tail this forces r_R D_tail<=BC; retained signed moments cannot repair an actual inner-cut defect while that cut stays saturated.", "evidence": "# A two-dimensional obstruction at a saturated primitive cut\n\nStatus: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or verifier\ncall. The only existing fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for its explicit finite\nsmooth-convex interpolation construction. Every other identity is proved here.\n\n## 1. Definitions and an energy identity\n\nFor a nonnegative physical schedule h of length n let T_tot=1+sum_i h_i.\nFor normalized smoothness 1 and an optimum jet (0,0,0), put\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)\n          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n          -T_tot(T_tot-1)||g_n||^2.\n\nHere primitive validity means V_h>=0 on every legal convex 1-smooth GD\ntrajectory. Suppose the sampled data additionally satisfy\n\n    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2 for every i,\n    x_n=g_n.\n\nThe first condition is exactly Q_(i,star)=0; the second gives\nf_n=||g_n||^2/2. Expanding\n\n    ||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2\n       =2 sum h_i<g_i,x_i>-sum h_i^2||g_i||^2\n\ntherefore gives the exact identity\n\n    V_h=||x_0||^2-T_tot^2||g_n||^2.                 (1)\n\nFor any such data, whether or not yet jointly interpolable, write y_i=x_i-g_i.\nDirect substitution also gives\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                             (2)\n\n## 2. General two-bridge construction\n\nLet a,b,c be arbitrary finite nonnegative blocks, with capacities\n\n    A=1+sum a_i, B=1+sum b_i, C=1+sum c_i.\n\nTake r_L,r_R>0 and h=(a,1+r_L,b,1+r_R,c). Define\n\n    D_tail=B+C+r_R, T_tot=A+D_tail+r_L,\n    eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,\n    p_aux=-B/r_L.\n\nThe first scalar array is a legal Huber trajectory: start at A and use\nderivative clip(x,-eta_cut,1). During a and at its endpoint the gradient is\n1. Its endpoint is 1. The first bridge lands at -r_L=-eta_cut D_tail.\nEvery remaining query has gradient -eta_cut, and the final point is\n-eta_cut. All the data satisfy the hypotheses of (1), so\n\n    V_base=A^2-T_tot^2 eta_cut^2.                   (3)\n\nThe second scalar array is defined as follows. During a use constant\ngradient p_aux, start at A p_aux, and end at p_aux. The first bridge lands\nat -r_L p_aux=B. On the remaining schedule (b,1+r_R,c) use the ACTUAL\nHuber trajectory with derivative clip(x,-beta_inner,1), starting at B.\nThe b block ends at 1, the second bridge lands at -r_R=-C beta_inner,\nand c ends at -beta_inner. Assign every value by\nf_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. Thus (1) yields\n\n    V_aux=A^2 B^2/r_L^2-T_tot^2 beta_inner^2.        (4)\n\nThe second array is generally NOT interpolable across the first bridge.\nIt is only auxiliary data; its possible violations are accounted for below.\n\nFor epsilon_mix>0, combine both arrays into two-dimensional data\n\n    X_i=(x_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) x_i^aux),\n    G_i=(g_i^base,sqrt(epsilon_mix) g_i^aux),\n    F_i=f_i^base+epsilon_mix f_i^aux.\n\nThe GD equations hold coordinate by coordinate, all Q_(i,star) remain zero,\nand X_n=G_n. Consequently\n\n    V_mix=V_base+epsilon_mix V_aux.                 (5)\n\nHere is a complete interpolation audit. Partition the samples into L (a\nand its endpoint), M (b and its endpoint after the first bridge), and R (c\nand its final endpoint after the second bridge). Overlaps are assigned to\nthe group whose gradient is used at the relevant bridge. The groups are\ndisjoint in original time: L includes the first bridge query, M includes\nthe second bridge query, and R includes the terminal sample.\n\n* Within L all gradients in each scalar array agree, so Q=0.\n* Within M union R the auxiliary data are one legal Huber trajectory;\n  the base gradients agree, so all Q<=0 there.\n* From L to M, the auxiliary target has y_j>=0 and the source gradient\n  p_aux<1, so Q_aux<=0. From M to L, the auxiliary target y_i is a\n  nonnegative multiple of p_aux<0 and 1-p_aux>0, so Q_aux<=0.\n* From L to R, if R_j is the remaining sum of physical c steps after its\n  target sample j, the two residuals are\n\n      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)eta_cut R_j,\n      Q_aux=beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)R_j.\n\n* From R to L, if H_i is the remaining sum of physical a steps after its\n  target sample i, then\n\n      Q_base=-(1+eta_cut)H_i,\n      Q_aux=(-beta_inner-p_aux)p_aux H_i.\n\nZero remaining sums give zero in both arrays. Thus duplicate positions\ncaused by zero steps do not require a separate limiting argument.\n\nThe optimum-outgoing inequalities hold too: Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,Y_i>,\nand the inner product is nonnegative separately in each scalar array.\nFor the auxiliary L data this is H_i p_aux^2>=0, and in the other groups\nit follows directly from their plateau coordinates. Q_(i,star)=0 already.\n\nWhen r_L r_R<BC, one has -p_aux>beta_inner. The ONLY potentially positive\nauxiliary residuals are L-to-R, and all interpolation inequalities hold for\n\n    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,\n    epsilon_max=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)\n                    /[beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].             (6)\n\nWhen r_L r_R>BC the ONLY potentially positive auxiliary residuals are\nR-to-L, and all inequalities hold for\n\n    0<epsilon_mix<=epsilon_max,\n    epsilon_max=(1+eta_cut)\n                    /[(-p_aux)(beta_inner+p_aux)].                 (7)\n\nWhen r_L r_R=BC both cross families are nonpositive for any epsilon_mix.\n\nAll these are complete original-index interpolation checks, including both\ncross directions and both optimum directions.\n\n## 3. One fixed globally legal function\n\nInclude the zero optimum sample and define, on R^2,\n\n    H_mix(v)=max_i { c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },\n    c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i=||G_i||^2/2,\n    f_mix(x)=sup_v { <x,v>-H_mix(v) }.\n\nThe maximum includes i=star. This is the explicit finite interpolation\nconstruction of accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290, proof section 2, applied to\nthe entire finite data set. The checked Q inequalities imply that f_mix\ninterpolates every (X_i,G_i,F_i), is convex differentiable and 1-smooth,\nand has f_mix(0)=0=min f_mix. Thus the mixed data are one actual GD\ntrajectory of one fixed function; they are not an adaptive oracle or a\nreset of the function at a bridge.\n\n## 4. A universal quantitative two-cut necessary condition\n\nAny primitive h as above necessarily satisfies\n\n    r_L r_R [A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] <= A^2 BC.           (8)\n\nFirst assume r_L r_R<BC and choose epsilon_mix=epsilon_max in (6).\nUsing D_tail=B+C+r_R, substitution into (3)-(5) gives\n\n    V_mix = (BC+r_R D_tail)(D_tail+r_L)\n              /[D_tail^2 r_R(BC-r_L r_R)]\n             * {A^2 BC-r_L r_R[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)]}.\n\nThe prefactor is positive, proving necessity of (8) in this case. For an\nalgebra check, before the final factorization the numerator is\n\n    (BC+r_R D_tail)\n       {A^2[BC(D_tail+r_L)-r_L r_R D_tail]\n                                     -T_tot^2 r_L^2 r_R}.\n\nUse A^2 D_tail+T_tot^2 r_L\n=(D_tail+r_L)[A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)] to obtain the displayed factorization.\n\nIf r_L r_R>BC, set z_aux=-p_aux=B/r_L and beta_inner>z_aux, and use (7).\nEquation (5) becomes\n\n    V_mix=(beta_inner+eta_cut z_aux)/(beta_inner-z_aux)\n             * {A^2-T_tot^2[(1+eta_cut)beta_inner/z_aux-eta_cut]}.\n\nThe bracket is strictly smaller than A^2-T_tot^2<0. Hence no such schedule\nis primitive. If r_L r_R=BC, V_aux=z_aux^2(A^2-T_tot^2)<0, so sufficiently\nlarge epsilon_mix in the fully legal mixed data again gives V_mix<0.\nThis excludes the two remaining cases and completes (8).\n\nNo quadratic-balance assumption about a,b,c appears in this argument.\n\n## 5. Saturated first cut forces its inner tail cut\n\nSuppose in addition r_L T_tot=A D_tail. Then eta_cut=A/T_tot, V_base=0,\nand A^2+r_L(T_tot+A)=A T_tot. Condition (8) reduces to\n\n    T_tot r_L r_R<=ABC,\n    equivalently r_R D_tail<=BC.                   (9)\n\nTherefore a positive defect r_R D_tail>BC in the inner tail cannot be\nrepaired by any signed storage or full collection of interpolation edges\nwhile keeping this outer cut exactly saturated. This statement concerns\nprimitive validity of this actual schedule, not all objective certificates.\n\nThe geometry also explains the conclusion without (8): at outer saturation\n(4) becomes (T_tot/D_tail)^2[B^2-D_tail^2 beta_inner^2], which is strictly\nnegative exactly when the tail cut fails. Any sufficiently small positive\nmixture allowed above then has V_mix<0.\n\n## 6. Explicit refutation candidate for the outstanding eight-step seed\n\nSet u=10/7, C=27/7, A=B=4, and\n\n    D_tail=(165+sqrt(65641))/42,\n    r_R=D_tail-55/7,\n    T_tot=(4+D_tail+sqrt((4+D_tail)^2+16D_tail))/2,\n    r_L=T_tot-4-D_tail.\n\nThe schedule is\n\n    (3/2,3/2,1+r_L,3/2,3/2,1+r_R,10/7,10/7).\n\nDirectly from these root equations,\n\n    r_R D_tail=196/9, r_L T_tot=4D_tail,\n    BC=108/7<196/9.\n\nThus (9) fails. Here is a concrete mixing scale without an unspecified\nsmallness parameter. Define eta_cut=r_L/D_tail, beta_inner=r_R/C,\np_aux=-4/r_L, and\n\n    epsilon_mix=eta_cut(1+eta_cut)\n                      /[2 beta_inner(-p_aux-beta_inner)].          (10)\n\nIt is positive: the first defining polynomial has its positive root in\n(10,101/10), and substituting 5/2 into\nr_L^2+(4+D_tail)r_L-4D_tail shows r_L<5/2. Hence\nr_L r_R<(5/2)(157/70)=157/28<108/7=BC. Formula (10) is half of (6).\n\nFor reference the two position arrays are\n\n    base: (4,5/2,1,-r_L,-r_L+(3/2)eta_cut,\n           -r_L+3eta_cut,-C eta_cut,-(1+u)eta_cut,-eta_cut),\n    aux:  (4p_aux,(5/2)p_aux,p_aux,4,5/2,1,\n           -r_R,-(1+u)beta_inner,-beta_inner).\n\nThe gradient arrays are\n\n    base: (1,1,1,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut,\n                           -eta_cut,-eta_cut,-eta_cut),\n    aux:  (p_aux,p_aux,p_aux,1,1,1,\n                           -beta_inner,-beta_inner,-beta_inner).\n\nValues are g_i x_i-g_i^2/2 in each coordinate. Sections 2-3 show that\ntheir mixed two-dimensional jets come from the explicit fixed f_mix.\nFinally beta_inner D_tail=1372/243=4(343/243), so\n\n    V_mix = epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2\n                  [16-(1372/243)^2]\n          = -epsilon_mix(T_tot/D_tail)^2 * 937600/59049 < 0.        (11)\n\nThis gives an exact concrete primitive counterexample for the proposed\neight-step seed. It closes that seed's proposed primitive amplification.\nIt makes no claim that a different direct objective certificate is\nimpossible, and does not settle the unrestricted asymptotic target.\n\n## Literature and verification status\n\nTargeted Matlas and web retrieval supplied no theorem used in this proof.\nThe primitive/Huber regime had already been audited in previous rounds;\nthis continuation uses the accepted finite interpolation construction\nrather than an external black box. All new results above remain candidates\npending a selected verifier allocation. No fact was submitted or written.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "primitive validity of seed29a1f4a5269032b1", "status": "refuted_by_candidate_proof_not_verifier_accepted", "branch_id": "max4_saturated_cut_defect_lifting", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["29a1f4a5269032b1"], "local_artifact": "local_memory/saturated_cut_defect_lifting.md", "verification_policy": "No routine verification allocated; no fact_submit called."}, "glossary": {"A": "One plus the physical stepsize sum of the first block a in this finding.", "B": "One plus the physical stepsize sum of the middle block b in this finding.", "C": "One plus the physical stepsize sum of the last block c in this finding.", "r_L": "Positive excess above one of the first bridge stepsize.", "r_R": "Positive excess above one of the second bridge stepsize.", "D_tail": "B+C+r_R, the physical capacity of the middle/last joined tail.", "T_tot": "A+D_tail+r_L, the physical capacity of the full schedule.", "V_h": "The intrinsic primitive energy defined in section1 of the evidence.", "eta_cut": "r_L/D_tail, the negative Huber magnitude of the base first-cut trajectory.", "beta_inner": "r_R/C, the negative Huber magnitude in the auxiliary tail.", "p_aux": "-B/r_L, the auxiliary constant gradient in the first block.", "epsilon_mix": "Positive coordinate-mixing scale for the two-dimensional fixed-function construction.", "epsilon_max": "Explicit interpolation-feasible upper scale given in evidence equations6 and7.", {"id": "75a0fbef6b918dff", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T13:57:28.446588+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C20 refutes the wider independent-secant envelope: on H_m, choosing lambda_t=1/4 for h_t<=1 and lambda_t=1 for h_t>1 yields relaxed radius >=exp(2^(m-18)/20) for every m>=23. It is NOT a legal scalar hard function: the derivative increases while the point decreases across the tiny step immediately preceding the first level18 checkpoint. The exact necessary small-step secant transition law is recorded.", "evidence": "# The factor-four sector relaxation grows, but its transcript is not legal\n\nStatus: complete unverified counterexample to an extension of the relaxed\nproduct bound. This is NOT a counterexample to a scalar GD convergence\nclaim and is NOT a legal hard function. The fixed-function incompatibility\nis proved explicitly below. No verifier call is allocated.\n\n## 1. The precise relaxed claim that fails\n\nSet c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, H_0=(), and\nH_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j). Consider the relaxed scalar recurrence\n\n    x_(t+1)=(1-h_t lambda_t)x_t,    x_0=1,\n\nwith independently selectable lambda_t in [1/4,1]. Use the deterministic\nrule\n\n    lambda_t=1/4 when h_t<=1,\n    lambda_t=1   when h_t>1.                              (1)\n\nOn the discrete step levels in H_m, this attains every factor of\nq_4(h)=max(1-h/4,h-1): the branch switch is at h=8/5, whereas the two\nconsecutive schedule levels around it are b_17=1 and b_18=13/5.\n\nFor every m>=23 the resulting terminal amplitude satisfies\n\n    |x_(2^m-1)| >= exp[2^(m-18)/20].                      (2)\n\nThus the uniform damping conclusion for independent secants in a\nfactor-13/5 band cannot be extended to an arbitrary fixed-width band\nwithout further restrictions. It already fails at width four.\n\n## 2. An exact product lower bound\n\nDefine\n\n    p_17=product_(h in H_17)(1-h/4)>0,\n    P_4(m)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)q_4(b_j)^(2^(m-j-1)).\n\nThe relaxed trajectory has |x_(2^m-1)|=P_4(m). In H_17, every h/4 is at\nmost 1/(4c)=5/52 and its total is at most (5/3)/4=5/12. The inequality\nlog(1-u)>=-u/(1-v) for 0<=u<=v<1 follows by integrating -1/(1-z).\nTherefore\n\n    log p_17 >= -(52/47)(5/12)=-65/141.                   (3)\n\nFor m>=23, divide log P_4(m) by 2^(m-18), retain the levels 17 through\n22, and discard only positive higher-level terms. This gives\n\n    log P_4(m)/2^(m-18)\n      >= 2 log p_17+log(3/4)\n                    +sum_(ell=1)^5 2^(-ell)log(c^ell-1).\n\nUse log(3/4)>=-1/3. For x>=1,\n\n    log x >= 2(x-1)/(x+1),\n\nbecause the derivative of the difference is\n(x-1)^2/[x(x+1)^2]>=0 and its value at 1 is zero. In particular,\n\n    (1/2)log(c-1) >= 3/13,\n    (1/4)log(c^2-1)=(1/2)log(12/5) >= 7/17,\n    (1/8)log(c^3-1) > (1/8)log 16 >= 1/3,\n    (1/16)log(c^4-1) > (1/16)log 32 >= 5/24,\n    (1/32)log(c^5-1) > (1/32)log 64 >= 1/8.\n\nThe last three use log 2>=2/3 and the direct rational comparisons\nc^3-1>16, c^4-1>32, c^5-1>64. Consequently\n\n    log P_4(m)/2^(m-18)\n      > -130/141+3/13+7/17+1/3\n      > -9/13+7/17+1/3\n       =35/663 >1/20.                                   (4)\n\nHere 130/141<12/13. Equation (4) proves (2) with room to spare.\nThis is an analytic calculation, not a numerical sweep.\n\n## 3. Why the maximizing transcript cannot be a fixed convex function\n\nLet t be the query index of the first step b_18=13/5 in H_m, m>=19.\nAll preceding steps belong to H_18 and have size at most one. Under (1)\nall preceding lambda values are 1/4, all preceding iterates are positive,\nand the immediately preceding step has size a. Write X=x_(t-1)>0.\n\nThe requested data at the two adjacent queries would be\n\n    x_(t-1)=X,                  g_(t-1)=X/4,\n    x_t=(1-a/4)X,               g_t=x_t.\n\nSince 0<a<1,\n\n    0<x_t<X,          g_t>(3/4)X>X/4=g_(t-1).\n\nA nondecreasing derivative must instead satisfy g_t<=g_(t-1). The\nproposed data violate monotonicity before the first large step has even\nbeen taken. Thus no single differentiable convex scalar function can\nproduce this transcript. Its growing amplitude cannot be advertised as\nan objective lower bound, a legal scalar counterexample, or a solution\nto the multiscale construction task.\n\n## 4. A necessary local repair, not a sufficient full-history condition\n\nFor any legitimate scalar convex function with 1-Lipschitz derivative and\nminimum at zero, take a nonzero query x with secant lambda=g(x)/x>0.\nFor a step 0<=h<=1 with h lambda<1, let x'=x-hg(x), and let\nlambda'=g(x')/x'. Then x' stays on the same ray and\n\n    (1-h)|g(x)| <= |g(x')| <= |g(x)|,\n\nby monotonicity and the 1-Lipschitz bound. Dividing by\n|x'|=(1-h lambda)|x| proves the exact necessary interval\n\n    lambda(1-h)/(1-h lambda)\n        <= lambda' <= lambda/(1-h lambda).              (5)\n\nFor the preceding small step h=a and lambda=1/4, (5) requires\nlambda'<=1/(4-a)<1, immediately rejecting (1).\n\nA general state recursion must retain at least this gradient/position\ncompatibility, in addition to the sign and secant band. It must also retain\ncompatibility at nonadjacent revisits to a previously traversed interval;\n(5) alone does not establish interpolation by one fixed function.
